Greedy aspects: only POINT layers get the low-entropy synthetic path; keyed features always pay 8 incompressible bytes/row; and the row-index sequence's monotonicity is degraded by the later `start_time` sort (encode.rs:791-867) — ids are order-independent by design so correctness holds, but the stored sequence is only near-monotonic when input arrives roughly time-ordered, and nothing re-densifies after the permutation.

**Class.** Universe reduction / docID reassignment in inverted indexes (renumbering to minimize compressed index size — NP-hard variants with strong practical heuristics); minimum-linear-arrangement flavor when co-optimized with row order. Deterministic, offline.

**Knobs.** None — policy hard-coded per geometry kind; FNV constants fixed by spec (offset 0xcbf29ce484222325, prime 0x100000001b3).

**Gap.** NOT PLANNED (gap analysis): a build-time global dense renumbering — a bijection source-id → small int in a canonical order (e.g. first appearance in time), stored once as a sidecar — would make _every_ id column delta-codable, including clipped-feature layers, while preserving cross-tile identity and joins. Cheaper still for synthetic-id point layers: omit the column entirely and let readers synthesize the row index (blocked only by the reader contract). Assigning row-index ids after the `start_time` sort would restore exact monotonicity for free. Measurable objective: compressed id-column bytes fleet-wide.

### 3.7 Sidecar materialization — baked triangles and part_offsets

**Setting.** Polygon layers can carry a `triangles` sidecar (flat earcut index lists, feature-local, tessellated part by part so MultiPolygon pieces don't vanish — `tessellate_parts` with a running vertex-base shift, columnar.rs:1041-1060) and a `part_offsets` sidecar (the ring index where each part begins — information GeoArrow's flat ring list erases, making part-vs-hole unrecoverable on the wire). Triangles are baked for the _whole layer_ iff `--pre-tessellate` is set or any feature has a hole or multiple parts — such features render corrupted through the renderers' single-boundary earcut (ring-bridging streaks), so for them the sidecar is a correctness requirement, not a perf win (columnar.rs:457-458, 950-958). The all-or-nothing granularity is forced by all three readers, which branch per _layer_ on the column's presence and trust per-feature slices verbatim — mixing empty and baked lists makes single-ring features silently vanish (reader inventory at columnar.rs:437-456). `part_offsets` is emitted only when some feature is multi-part; absence means all-single-part (columns.rs:591-633). The triangle column measures 40–45% of polygon-heavy tiles' wire bytes, most spent on single-ring features any renderer could earcut itself.

**Problem.** Given a polygon layer with features $i = 1..n$, ring structures $r_i$, indicator $h_i \in \{0,1\}$ (has a hole or >1 part), earcut index-list sizes $|T_i|$, client-side tessellation costs $c_i$ (CPU on tile arrival), and a reader contract $\rho \in \{\text{layer-global}, \text{per-feature}\}$ (today: layer-global). Choose emission $y_i \in \{0,1\}$ per feature (constrained by $\rho$), index width $w \in \{16, 32\}$, and part_offsets emission $p \in \{0,1\}$. Minimize

$$\underbrace{\sum_i y_i \, |T_i| \tfrac{w}{8}}_{\text{tile bytes}} + \lambda \underbrace{\sum_i (1 - y_i)\, c_i}_{\text{client CPU}} + p \cdot |\mathrm{offsets}|$$

Subject to:

1. Correctness: $h_i = 1 \Rightarrow y_i = 1$ (single-boundary earcut bridges disjoint rings — visual corruption).
2. Reader contract: under $\rho = \text{layer-global}$, $y_i = y_j\ \forall i, j$.
3. Width: $\max_{i: y_i = 1} \max(T_i) \le 2^w - 1$.
4. $p = 1$ iff some feature is multi-part — $p$ is forced, zero degrees of freedom, minimal by construction.

**Incumbent.** `needs_triangles` bakes all iff flag ∨ ∃i: $h_i$ (columnar.rs:457-458); narrowest-fitting width scan at encode.rs:446-484; the column is dropped entirely for non-polygon layers (poisoning guard, encode.rs:439-445); earcut itself at layer.rs:284-311. Heuristic aspects: the layer-global $y$ over-pays exactly the single-ring share of the 40–45%, and $\lambda$ is implicitly 0 — bytes are never traded against CPU except via the manual flag.

**Class.** Materialized-view / precomputation selection (compute-vs-storage, knapsack-flavored in the per-feature relaxation); here collapsed to feasibility by the consumer contract. Deterministic, offline.

**Knobs.** `--pre-tessellate` (columnar.rs:82-85); width menu {u16, u32}; no per-feature control (blocked by readers).

**Gap.** PLANNED-in-comments (columnar.rs:452-456 names the cheapest fix): a decoder-side backfill — earcut a feature's single ring in packages/core/src/tile.ts when its baked list is empty — relaxes $\rho$ to per-feature and unlocks $y_i = h_i$, saving the single-ring share of triangle bytes on polygon-heavy archives at unchanged visual output. Secondary: earcut currently runs on raw f64 coords, so slivers below the quantization step produce triangles that collapse post-quantization — wasted indices.

### 3.8 Schema-template factoring — metadata placement and cross-tile schema dedup

**Setting.** The v1 format paid a pure fixed per-blob tax — measured at ~430 B/blob ≈ 37% of _all_ pack bytes on earthquakes-v2 (102 K tiles averaging 1.1 KB). The v2 layer frame removes it: every layer's Arrow IPC stream is split at the schema-message boundary into a schema TEMPLATE and a batch TAIL; templates are content-addressed (blake3-128) into a `TemplateCollector` and published once in manifest.schemas, while frames carry 16-byte hash references — measured A/B at identical flags: −36.4% pack bytes on hurricanes (12,886 tiles, ~780 B/tile) grading down to −0.4% on taxi (600 tiles, ~220 KB/tile), headline −44.8% E2E on hurricanes. The factoring only works if the schema is _identical_ across tiles, so every per-tile-varying metadatum is hoisted into the frame's TILE_META JSON section (qa affines, $t_0$, vertex-time origin/step, bucket count, st/et forms, vq affines) while dataset-constant keys stay template-resident (layer name, geometry kind, CRS, has_triangles, and the coordinate-quantization affine — constant precisely _because_ the grid is world-anchored, §3.1). Decode splices template+tail and re-injects TILE_META byte-exactly. Separately, IPC buffer alignment was dropped from arrow-rs's 64-byte default to the 8-byte spec floor — padding was 19–39% of raw tile bytes fleet-wide — bounded below by the readers' zero-copy element-width requirement (encode.rs:645-672).

**Problem.** Given tiles $t \in \mathcal{D}$, each layer with schema $\Sigma_t = (\mathrm{fields}_t, \mathrm{meta}_t)$ where metadata keys $k$ have value functions $v_k(t)$, and the classification $\mathrm{Var} = \{k : v_k \text{ non-constant over } \mathcal{D}\}$. Decision variables: (a) a placement $P: \mathrm{keys} \to \{\mathrm{TEMPLATE}, \mathrm{TILEMETA}\}$; (b) — coupled — every per-tile encoding choice $e$ from §3.1–3.7 (leaf widths, dict-vs-utf8, end-time omission, sidecar presence), since each distinct realized field-list × type × template-resident-metadata combination forks one template. Minimize

$$B = \sum_{\tau \in \mathcal{T}(P, e)} |\tau| \;+\; \sum_t \big( |\mathrm{TILEMETA}_t(P)| + 16 \cdot \#\mathrm{refs} + |\mathrm{tail}_t| \big)$$

where $\mathcal{T}$ is the set of distinct template byte strings under $(P, e)$. Subject to: correctness — $P(k) = \mathrm{TEMPLATE} \Rightarrow k \notin \mathrm{Var}$ (a varying key in the template drives $|\mathcal{T}| \to |\mathcal{D}|$, destroying the factoring); byte-exact decode re-injection of the standalone schema; content-addressed templates (order-independence, parallel-encode-safe, byte-reproducible builds); tail buffer alignment ≥ max element width for zero-copy readers; TILE_META canonical JSON (sorted keys) for reproducibility.

**Incumbent.** A fixed hand partition in `encode_layer_v2_parts` (encode.rs:880-976: qa hoist 899-921, TileMeta assembly 923-935, dataset-constant core 939-948); split-boundary walk `split_ipc_at_schema` (encode.rs:759-789); frame assembly with hash refs (encode.rs:1034-1110); `TemplateCollector`/`TemplateRegistry` (frame.rs:86-174); TileMeta contract (frame.rs:219-260). The partition is exactly optimal _given_ the known variability structure — every hoisted key genuinely varies, every resident key is provably constant. What is heuristic is the coupling: the per-tile greedy encoders (dictionary choice, U16/I32 widths, EndTimeForm::Zero omission, sidecar presence) each fork templates, and none carries a $|\mathcal{T}|$ term.

**Class.** Dictionary compression at schema granularity / common-subexpression hoisting; the joint problem — choose per-tile encodings to minimize realized-schema count plus data bytes — is a clustering / facility-location-flavored set problem (NP-hard in general); the incumbent is "one template per realized shape," with shape count kept small by the stability constraints of §3.2–3.4.

**Knobs.** `template_collector` Some/None (packed vs self-contained frames, config.rs:218-223); `FRAME_ALIGN = 8` (frame.rs:15); `IPC_BUFFER_ALIGNMENT = 8`; blake3-128 reference width fixed at 16 B.

**Gap.** Two follow-ons. (1) A drift-aware global pass pinning width/type choices per column across the dataset (e.g. always-I32 for a U16/I32-straddling column when the extra 2 B/row loses to the marginal template); measurable objective: manifest.schemas count × template size + PROPS bytes. (2) DEFERRED — the dataset-global dictionary hoist, the biggest measured win left on the table: per-tile dictionary batches re-ship each categorical column's complete category list per tile at 43.9% (earthquakes-v2) / 33.6% (hurricanes) of uncompressed tile bytes, though the category set is a property of the dataset. It is reader-transparent by construction (both readers splice without asserting the template holds only a Schema message — a template carrying the DictionaryBatch would decode today in both languages) but writer-blocked: a stable global code assignment needs global cardinality _before_ the first tile encodes, i.e. a two-pass build, and a partial hoist is worse than none (a sometimes-hoisted dictionary forks a template per category subset). The same two-pass phase would also discharge §3.3's global attribute-range pin — one investment, two customers.

## 4. Summary tiers & level-of-detail

This subsystem decides what a client sees when the raw data is too dense for the zoom it is looking at: whether to materialize an aggregated summary tier and at what cell granularity, which statistics to precompute per cell, and how to assign individual features a zoom floor or home zoom so overview tiles stay serveable without ever deleting data. The resource trades are archive bytes and peak overview-tile cost against aggregation distortion and overview completeness, all under the project's binding no-thinning principle: the deepest tier is always lossless. Two temporal-axis LOD problems that formerly lived here are formalized elsewhere — the equi-count adaptive temporal windowing of a cell's time axis (see §2 — equal-mass 1-D partitioning) and the coarse-bucket temporal-LOD pyramid design (see §2 — tier widths and zoom cutoffs); likewise per-zoom geometry simplification, whose lossless-max-tier / watertight-seam constraint rides with it (see §1 — per-zoom simplification).

### 4.1 Summary-tier shaping: triggering, scheme choice, and the zoom → cell-resolution map

**Setting.** The summary tier walks every ParsedFeature, assigns it to an H3 or Quadbin cell at the resolution configured for the tile's zoom, accumulates per-cell aggregates, and emits one summary tile per (zoom, x, y, time-bucket) whose rows are cells (summary.rs:180-348). The resolution table is fixed by two constant maps: H3 uses the identity $r(z)=\min(z,15)$ (summary.rs:91-96); Quadbin uses $r(z)=\min(z+3,26)$ via `QUADBIN_RES_OFFSET=3`, because Quadbin cells _are_ XYZ tiles and a 1:1 map would render ~2–4 giant cells per viewport (summary.rs:98-116). The table is serialized as `cell_resolution_per_zoom` in the SummaryTier manifest block (summary.rs:60-79; metadata.rs:40-89) so the reader reconstructs cell polygons client-side from `cell_id` alone. A correctness sub-decision rides along: an H3 hexagon straddling a Web-Mercator tile seam is owned by the tile containing its **centroid**, so one cell never splits into partial-count duplicate rows (summary.rs:217-247, pinned by the seam test at summary.rs:807-884); Quadbin cells nest inside exactly one tile, so feature-position bucketing suffices (summary.rs:249-264). Upstream of the resolution map sits the _whether-and-which-scheme_ decision: the stt-optimize budget advisor triggers a summary tier on two threshold detectors over the density model — dense-uniform (count > 1M and avg overview features/tile > 5000) or hotspot-skewed (count > 100k, densest 1% of overview cells hold ≥60% of features, peak tile > 10k) — and picks H3 iff the spatial distribution is Localized, else tile-aligned Quadbin (advisors/budget.rs:23-44, 157-174; density.rs:208-214). The tier is strictly _additive_: the raw tier ships unchanged (`lossy: false`).

**Problem.** **Given:** a feature set $D=\{p_1,\dots,p_N\}\subset\mathbb{R}^2$ (lon/lat); a zoom band $[z_{\min},z_{\max}]$ with overview zoom $z_0$; a nested cell family indexed by resolution $r$ with cell area $A(r)$ (H3: $A(r)=A_0/7^{r}$, $r\in\{0,\dots,15\}$; Quadbin: $A(r)=A_E/4^{r}$, $r\in\{0,\dots,26\}$); a viewport model $V(z)$ = expected ground area visible at zoom $z$ ($V(z)\propto 4^{-z}$); zoom-usage weights $w_z\ge 0$; readability bounds $K_{\min},K_{\max}$ on cells per viewport; per-tile raw feature counts $\{n_t\}$ at $z_0$ with measured per-feature bytes $\beta$; and a serveability ceiling $\bar n$ (bytes or features a client can decode per overview tile).

**Decision variables:** (i) the tier decision $s\in\{\text{none},\text{H3},\text{Quadbin}\}$; (ii) given $s\neq\text{none}$, the resolution table $r:\{z_{\min},\dots,z_{\max}\}\to\{0,\dots,r_{\max}\}$ (one entry per zoom).

**Outer objective (load shaping):**
$$\min_{s,\,r}\;\; \lambda_1 \underbrace{\max_t\;\Big(\beta\, n_t + \mathrm{bytes}_{agg}(s,r,t)\Big)}_{\text{peak overview tile cost}} \;+\; \lambda_2 \underbrace{\Delta S(s,r)}_{\text{added archive bytes}} \;+\; \lambda_3 \underbrace{\mathrm{visloss}}_{\text{overview visibility loss}},$$
where $\mathrm{bytes}_{agg}(s,r,t)$ is the aggregate rows' cost in tile $t$, $\Delta S$ is the summary tier's total byte cost, and $\mathrm{visloss}$ is the importance-weighted loss from any per-feature zoom floors (that lever is formalized in §4.4). Setting $s=\text{none}$ and no floors recovers the raw build; the $\lambda$ constants encode a currently implicit tradeoff.

**Inner objective (aggregation distortion):** given $s$, choose $r(\cdot)$ to
$$\min_{r}\;\sum_{z=z_{\min}}^{z_{\max}} w_z\; \mathbb{E}_{p\sim D}\big[\, d\big(p,\; c_{r(z)}(p)\big)^2 \,\big]\;\; \text{(or within-cell variance of the rendered aggregate)},$$
where $c_{r}(p)$ is the centroid of the resolution-$r$ cell containing $p$ and $d$ is ground distance.

**Subject to:**

1. Readability/render budget: $K_{\min}\le \dfrac{V(z)}{A(r(z))}\le K_{\max}$ for all $z$ (bounded cells per viewport ⇒ bounded rows per tile);
2. Monotonicity: $r(z{+}1)\ge r(z)$ (finer cells at deeper zooms, consistent tier nesting);
3. Scheme band: $r(z)\le 15$ (H3) or $r(z)\le 26$ (Quadbin, so the 5-bit zoom field and 52-bit Morton payload never overflow, quadbin.rs:74-88);
4. Cell ownership: each cell owned by exactly one tile (centroid anchoring — no seam-split duplicate rows);
5. Additivity/losslessness: the raw tier ships unchanged, every feature present in the archive;
6. Tier consistency: aggregate cells are a deterministic, order-independent function of the raw features.

**Incumbent.** Two hardcoded constant maps with no data-dependence: `h3_resolution_for_zoom(z)=min(z,15)` at crates/stt-build/src/summary.rs:91-96 and `quadbin_zoom_for_zoom(z)=min(z+3,26)` at summary.rs:108-116. The H3 identity map lets cells-per-tile drift by ~$(7/4)^z$ across the band (the docstring concedes "a little coarser than perfect parity"); the Quadbin +3 offset pins exactly $4^3=64$ cells per carrying tile by fiat. Neither considers dataset density, viewport DPI, or per-zoom usage — the readability constraint holds by construction of the constants and the distortion objective is never evaluated. The outer decision is binary threshold classifiers with five hand-set constants (`SUMMARY_TIER_MIN_FEATURES`=1e6, heavy-avg 5000, skew share 0.60, 1e5, oversized 10k; advisors/budget.rs:23-44) plus one categorical scheme test (budget.rs:170-174); nothing evaluates $\Delta S$ against any budget, and $\bar n$ is the same folklore 10k constant as the per-tile cap. The advisory pipeline these thresholds live in is §12's problem.

**Class.** Vector quantization / rate–distortion (codebook = cell grid, rate = cells per viewport); optimal histogram bin-width selection; the outer decision is a bicriteria facility-design / heavy-tail detection problem. Offline and tiny: monotone maps over ≤27 resolutions × ~15 zooms admit exact DP in microseconds — the incumbent's gap is purely that no data-driven objective is evaluated at all.

**Knobs.** `QUADBIN_RES_OFFSET = 3` (compile-time const, summary.rs:108); `SummaryConfig.min_zoom/max_zoom` (CLI); scheme ∈ {H3, Quadbin}; clamps 15 (H3) / 26 (Quadbin); advisor thresholds 1e6 / 5000 avg / 0.60 top-1% share / 1e5 / 10k peak; scheme rule Localized ⇒ H3 else Quadbin.

**Gap.** A per-archive solver could pick $r(z)$ from the actual point distribution (minimize within-cell variance of the styled aggregate subject to the $K$-band), replacing the universal constants; the tier decision could compare its actual $\Delta S$ (trial-aggregating the sample) against fetch savings, replacing five constants with one bicriteria sweep; $\bar n$ could derive from the client decode budget rather than folklore. Measurable objectives: mean within-cell variance of the rendered statistic, cells-per-viewport spread across zooms, peak overview tile bytes, archive bytes added. Per-region adaptive resolution (finer cells only where dense) is a further step the fixed table cannot express — PLANNED at most; not implemented.

### 4.2 Aggregate materialization: which (column, agg) pairs and how many temporal sub-buckets

**Setting.** Each summary cell row carries an implicit count plus one numeric column per configured (source-column, aggregation) pair, parsed from `--summary-columns` syntax like `magnitude:mean,magnitude:max,count` (summary.rs:513-548). A single Accumulator per _source_ column stores (sum, sum_count, min, max) so several aggregations over the same source share one scan (summary.rs:134, 142-171, 294-311); mean is derived at emit time from sum/count (summary.rs:419-425). Independently, `sub_buckets=K>1` splits each outer time bucket into $K$ equal sub-intervals and emits $K$ extra `bucket_i` count columns per cell (summary.rs:48-53, 270-277, 446-463) — the renderer then animates inside a tile by switching a uniform index, with zero data re-upload between frames (metadata.rs:50-57). Nothing chooses the set automatically; columns and $K$ are entirely user-specified.

**Problem.** **Given:** property columns $P$; aggregate family $A=\{\mathrm{count},\mathrm{sum},\mathrm{mean},\mathrm{min},\mathrm{max}\}$; a render/query workload $Q$ (each $q\in Q$ needs some statistic $\sigma_q\in P\times A$ per cell, with usage weight $u_q$); the outer temporal bucket $b$ (ms); the playback frame cadence $f$ (ms); expected cells per tile $\bar n$; tile count $M$; and a per-column byte cost $\beta\approx 8\bar n M$ (one f64 per cell per tile).

**Decision variables:** the materialized set $S\subseteq P\times A$; the sub-bucket count $K\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$.

**Minimize** the archive/latency cost
$$\underbrace{\beta\,\big(|S'| + K\cdot\mathbf{1}[K>1]\big)}_{\text{stored bytes}} \;+\; \lambda \underbrace{\sum_{q\in Q} u_q\, \mathrm{miss}(q,S)}_{\text{unanswerable queries}} \;+\; \mu \underbrace{\mathrm{reupload}(K)}_{\text{per-frame re-upload cost}},$$
where $S'$ is the minimal stored basis for $S$ exploiting derivability ($\mathrm{mean}$ from $\mathrm{sum}+\mathrm{count}$, $\mathrm{count}$ shared), $\mathrm{miss}(q,S)=\mathbf{1}[\sigma_q\text{ not derivable from }S]$, and $\mathrm{reupload}(K)=0$ iff the animation step within a bucket resolves to a column switch, i.e. temporal resolution $b/K\le$ the visible frame step.

**Subject to:** $K\ge 1$; every aggregate in $S$ is algebraic (computable in one streaming pass — the accumulator state (sum, count, min, max) suffices); sub-bucket columns are counts only (current wire schema).

**Incumbent.** No solver — $S$ is verbatim user input via `parse_summary_columns` (crates/stt-build/src/summary.rs:513-548) and $K$ defaults to 1 (summary.rs:53). The only optimization present is the shared-accumulator basis reduction: aggregations over the same source column share one Accumulator keyed by source name (summary.rs:130-134, 294-311), and mean is derived rather than stored (summary.rs:419-425). $K$ is a pure knob with no automatic tie to `bucket_ms` or frame cadence; every extra sub-bucket costs 8 bytes/cell/tile whether or not playback ever samples that finely.

**Class.** Materialized view / data-cube selection under a storage budget — NP-hard; greedy achieves $(1-1/e)$ of the benefit (Harinarayan–Rajaraman–Ullman). The derivability lattice over {count, sum, mean, min, max} is the classic algebraic-aggregate structure. Offline, deterministic.

**Knobs.** `--summary-columns` spec (name:agg list, count special-cased); `sub_buckets` (default 1); `layer_name` (default "summary"); `temporal_bucket_ms` (should match the raw tier, summary.rs:41-43).

**Gap.** An advisor could derive $S$ from the archive's style_hints / demo styling workload and set $K=\mathrm{bucket\_ms}/\text{playback frame step}$ automatically; measurable objectives: summary-tier bytes, fraction of style queries answerable without the raw tier, per-frame upload bytes during playback. Percentile/heavy-hitter aggregates (non-algebraic) would need sketch columns — not representable in the current accumulator. All PLANNED at most.

### 4.3 Opt-in per-tile feature budget: importance-scored selection under count + byte caps (two-constraint knapsack)

**Setting.** Default OFF — the "no thinning by default" principle makes this lever inert unless `--maximum-tile-bytes`/`--maximum-tile-features` is passed (build_options.rs:299-319; tiler.rs:157-165), and the corresponding stt-optimize advice is permanently `lossy: true`, always led by non-destructive alternatives, suggesting 10,000 features (advisors/budget.rs:5-9, 23, 129). When a gathered tile exceeds either cap, `apply_tile_budget` runs `TileBudget::enforce_indexed` before layer construction (enforce site tiler.rs:1499-1513, 1633-1658), selecting features to keep purely from (vertex_count, property_count, estimated bytes) signals — index-based, so the tiler's borrowed columnar representation is never materialized (stt-core budget.rs:73-154; tiler.rs:1586-1611). Every drop is logged per tile with exact counts ("no silent caps", tiler.rs:1633-1658); under-budget tiles return byte-identical output (fast path budget.rs:104-109). The importance score is a fixed linear proxy: verts + 10·props for Combined, verts alone for `--drop-densest` (budget.rs:161-169; build_options.rs:303-319). `cover_t_min` is recomputed over the _kept_ features so a dropped early feature cannot widen the tile's temporal cover bound (tiler.rs:1564-1572).

**Problem.** **Given:** a tile's gathered features $i=1..n$ with importance $s_i$ and size $c_i$ (bytes), where currently $s_i = v_i + 10 p_i$ (vertex count $v_i$, property count $p_i$) or $s_i = v_i$, and $c_i = 16 v_i + 16 p_i + 32$; a feature cap $F$ and an uncompressed byte cap $B$.

**Decision variables:** keep set $K \subseteq \{1..n\}$ (binary $x_i \in \{0,1\}$).

**Maximize** retained importance:
$$\max_{x} \sum_{i=1}^{n} s_i x_i$$

**Subject to:**

1. $\sum_i x_i \le F$ (feature-count cap);
2. $\sum_i c_i x_i \le B$ (byte cap);
3. No-op guarantee: if $n \le F$ and $\sum_i c_i \le B$ then $K = \{1..n\}$ exactly (under-budget builds byte-identical to no-budget builds);
4. Output order stability: $K$ returned in ascending original index order; deterministic.

A deeper formulation treats $s_i$ itself as a decision — ideally $s_i$ encodes visual salience and $c_i$ the marginal _compressed_ bytes of feature $i$ in the final tile; the code fixes both by constant proxy.

**Incumbent.** Two-stage greedy (crates/stt-core/src/budget.rs:98-154): (1) count cap — sort by score descending, truncate to $F$; (2) byte cap — sort survivors by density $s_i/c_i$ descending and greedily fill to a target of $0.9B$, allowing items within a 95–105% window near the target ("slight overshoot to fill"). Gaps vs optimum: the two caps are applied _sequentially_ (stage 1 may evict small high-density items a joint solve would keep); density-greedy without the best-single-item comparison has no constant-factor guarantee (classically ½-approx with it); the 0.9 target deliberately underfills by ~10%; and the ×10 property weight is unjustified by any measured fidelity metric. Two folded findings sharpen this: under the Combined scorer against $c_i=16v_i+16p_i+32$, density is nearly constant ($\approx 1/16$) for vertex-dominated features, so the greedy order is close to arbitrary exactly when the cap binds hardest; and `max_compressed_size` is stored (budget.rs:33-34, set at build_options.rs:318) but _never enforced_ — only the uncompressed 16/16/32 estimate binds, so the actual compressed tile can miss the intended cap in either direction.

**Class.** 0/1 knapsack with a cardinality constraint (two-dimensional knapsack) — weakly NP-hard, FPTAS exists; the incumbent is the classic greedy-by-density heuristic; tippecanoe's `--drop-fraction-as-needed` addresses the same problem by rate-dropping. Deterministic, offline, per-tile independent (embarrassingly parallel); exact DP on bytes is feasible at per-tile scale ($n \le \sim 10^4$, $B \le \sim 500$ KB).

**Knobs.** `--maximum-tile-bytes`, `--maximum-tile-features` (both default unset ⇒ no budget; struct defaults 500 KB uncompressed / 128 KB compressed / 10k features, budget.rs:41-50); `--drop-densest` (score = GeometrySize); byte target factor 0.9 and ±5% overshoot window (compile-time constants budget.rs:126, 140); size-model constants 16/16/32 B (tiler.rs:1586-1589).

**Gap.** A joint exact solve (DP over bytes with a cardinality dimension, or Lagrangian on the count cap) removes both the sequential-stage loss and the intentional ~10% underfill; a measured importance score (contribution to the rendered image at the tile's zoom) and marginal-compressed-byte sizes (measurable via the sample encoder) would make the byte cap bind on wire size and move actual visual fidelity per byte; enforcing `max_compressed_size` — currently dead — or deleting it would close a silent spec/behavior gap. Measurable objective: retained-score fraction and rendered SSIM at fixed tile-byte caps, plus variance of realized tile bytes around the cap.

### 4.4 Stratified space–time LOD thinning: per-point min_zoom over a 4D grid

**Setting.** IMPLEMENTED in the dataset-generation scripts; the builder consumes the result. For huge animated point volumes (the 44M-point national MRMS radar), `lod_min_zoom` assigns each point the coarsest zoom at which it "wins" its 4D (lon, lat, alt, time-bucket) grid cell: points are stable-sorted by descending dBZ importance, then for each zoom from coarsest up — cell side doubling per zoom out from a top-tier size ≈ screen resolution at the framing zoom — the first (highest-dBZ) point in each occupied cell gets `min_zoom=z` (scripts/data-generation/mrms_volume.py:229-256). The 4D key _includes_ $\lfloor t/\text{bucket\_ms}\rfloor$ with `bucket_ms` equal to the archive's `--temporal-bucket` — the load-bearing constraint: the earlier space-only grid let one gate claim a cell for the whole 9.5 h window, so z8 showed a median 13% (worst 0%) of the visible bucket; keying the bucket raised it to 65% (mrms_volume.py:220-226; docs/roadmap/README.md:357-360). Streamed re-computation must pin the grid origin to a window-independent corner or per-frame grids stop nesting (mrms_volume.py:233-243; mrms_refloor.py:47-57, 111-115). stt-build consumes the column as a road-class-style LOD floor: `--min-zoom-field` skips a feature at any zoom below its value, whole-feature only (tiler.rs:143-148, 687-726, 1377-1382). The base (deepest) zoom is lossless: every point defaults to `min_zoom=max_zoom`. The same builder mechanism has an advisor-nominated variant for rank-classed features: when one hotspot holds >50% of all features and the sample carries a plausible low-cardinality rank column (2–16 distinct small-int/categorical values over ≥20 rows), stt-optimize suggests `--min-zoom-field` floors — zoom _placement_, not deletion — emitted suggestion-only, never auto-applied, with the constraint that floors are rank-monotone and dataset-uniform (identical features get identical floors everywhere); the rank-to-floor mapping itself is never computed, and on a categorical candidate the emitted flag is a documented silent no-op until a numeric column is baked (advisors/budget.rs:46-60, 231-271, 290-360).

**Problem.** **Given:** points $i=1..N$ with position $(x_i,y_i,a_i)\in\mathbb{R}^3$ (lon, lat, altitude), timestamp $t_i$, importance $w_i\ge 0$ (dBZ); zoom band $[z_{\min},z_{\max}]$; top-tier cell sizes $\Delta_{xy},\Delta_{a}$ with per-level scaling $\Delta(z)=\Delta\cdot 2^{\,z_{\max}-1-z}$; temporal bucket $\beta$ (= the archive's `temporal_bucket_ms`); pinned grid origin $o=(o_x,o_y,o_a)$. Define the level-$z$ cell of point $i$ as
$$g_z(i)=\Big(\big\lfloor \tfrac{x_i-o_x}{\Delta_{xy}(z)} \big\rfloor,\ \big\lfloor \tfrac{y_i-o_y}{\Delta_{xy}(z)} \big\rfloor,\ \big\lfloor \tfrac{a_i-o_a}{\Delta_{a}(z)} \big\rfloor,\ \big\lfloor \tfrac{t_i}{\beta} \big\rfloor\Big).$$

**Decision variables:** $\ell_i\in\{z_{\min},\dots,z_{\max}\}$ (the per-point min_zoom). The set visible at zoom $z$ is $V_z=\{i:\ell_i\le z\}$ (cumulative: $V_z\subseteq V_{z+1}$ by construction).

**Maximize** importance-weighted coverage at every zoom:
$$\max_{\ell} \sum_{z=z_{\min}}^{z_{\max}-1} \sum_{i\in V_z} w_i \qquad (\text{equivalently: at each } z \text{, each occupied cell is represented by its most important point}).$$

**Subject to:**

1. Density cap: $\big|V_z\cap g_z^{-1}(c)\big|\le 1$ for every zoom $z<z_{\max}$ and every 4D cell $c$ — at most one visible point per screen-resolution cell **per temporal bucket**;
2. Lossless base tier: $V_{z_{\max}}=\{1..N\}$ (no point is ever globally dropped);
3. Streaming determinism: $\ell$ computed per frame with pinned $o$ must equal the whole-window result (grid nesting invariance).

**Incumbent.** Greedy importance-first sweep (mrms_volume.py:229-256, re-run with pinned origins by mrms_refloor.py:111-115): stable argsort by $-$dBZ, then per level `np.unique` over hashed 4D codes takes the first (= max-weight) point per occupied cell and `np.minimum.at` lowers its min_zoom. For the stated one-per-cell objective this greedy is per-level exact; the heuristics are structural: (a) an axis-aligned grid with a single hash code $(c_x\!\ll\!40 \oplus c_y\!\ll\!20 \oplus c_z)\cdot 1000003 + c_t$ — collision-prone and alignment-artifacted versus true Poisson-disk uniformity; (b) hand-tuned top-tier cell sizes (0.02°/1000 m in mrms_volume.py:299-300, 0.0085°/60 m in mrms_refloor.py:47-48) tuned under the _old_ space-only key and known to overshoot density with the bucket keyed (mrms_volume.py:291-298); (c) exactly one representative per cell regardless of within-cell mass — a 1000-point cell and a 1-point cell render identically at coarse zoom. The builder side (tiler.rs:714-726, 1377-1382) is a pure filter that replicates each visible point at every zoom ≥ $\ell_i$, so storage is multiplicative in zoom span.

**Class.** Stratified spatial sampling / grid-based decimation; weighted maximum coverage (greedy $1-1/e$ in general, exact here per level due to unit cell capacity); Poisson-disk / max-min dispersion sampling (NP-hard) is the quality-optimal alternative the grid approximates; the streaming variant needs pinned-origin invariance. The rank-class floor variant is a load-balancing / peak-minimization problem with monotone assignment constraints — NP-hard in general, but per-rank-class it reduces to a small exactly-solvable DP/sweep. Deterministic, offline.

**Knobs.** `--lod-cell-deg` (0.02° national / 0.0085° refloor), `--lod-cell-alt` (1000 m / 60 m), `LOD_BUCKET_MS` = 300 000 ms (= `--temporal-bucket`), min/max zoom band (2–8 national, max 6 shipped), grid origin pin, `--no-lod` escape hatch; advisor variant: hotspot share 0.50, candidate-column gates ≥20 samples / 2–16 distinct / |int| ≤ 32.

**Gap.** Cell sizes are constants a solver could derive from a target points-per-viewport budget at each zoom (the script itself prints the pyramid and says "check the per-bucket column and coarsen if it overshoots" — a manual feedback loop begging to be closed). A mass-aware variant (representative count ∝ log cell mass, or jittered/blue-noise cells) would move the measured per-bucket coverage-uniformity metric without touching base-tier losslessness. Given the per-tile occupancy histogram (already computed, density.rs:184-218), the rank-class-to-floor assignment could be _solved_ — floors per class minimizing peak overview bytes subject to a visibility budget — instead of merely nominating a column. Nothing in stt-build itself computes $\ell$ — porting the assignment into the builder would make the guarantee archive-native rather than script-discipline. PLANNED at most.

### 4.5 Additive (Potree/COPC-style) home-zoom partition with curvature-aware level budgets

**Setting.** IMPLEMENTED for the AV LiDAR bundles and HD-map overviews (generation scripts + builder band mechanism + reader union); a _general_ build-side implementation is explicitly counted out as planned-only (docs/roadmap/stt-packed-format-decisions.md:442-451) — this additive partition is distinct from §1's multi-zoom replication problem, where it appears only as the counted-out alternative. `lod_home_zoom` (scripts/data-generation/av_common.py:2099-2186) assigns every LiDAR return exactly _one_ home zoom in [14,21]: per sweep, points are claimed coarse→fine — at each zoom the still-unclaimed points are voxelized at $v(z) = s_{px}\cdot 156543\cos\varphi/2^z$ metres (screen-space-error sizing tied to the tile pyramid) and one real representative per occupied voxel is claimed; the unclaimed residual lands at z=21 at full density. Curvature grading (default ON, `_lod_curv_reps` av_common.py:2077-2096) computes per-sweep surface variation $\sigma=\lambda_0/\Sigma\lambda$ from k-NN covariance and coarsens the voxel up to 3× for flat regions at coarse zooms, so edges/poles/curbs stay dense in the overview while road surfaces defer. stt-build places each point at exactly its home zoom via `--min-zoom-field`=`--max-zoom-field`=`home_zoom` (the [min,max] band mechanism, tiler.rs:149-156, 714-726), and the reader renders the _union_ of zooms [minZoom..cameraZoom] under `lodMode:'additive'` (packages/core/src/spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:609-618, 1715, 4074). The union at maxZoom is the complete cloud — lossless — while storing each point once (~½ the bytes of the previous 5-tier strided pyramid). An HD-map line-overview variant runs the ladder 3 levels deeper than the kept max and discards the finer claims + residual, producing a deliberately reduced overview tier whose lossless base is the separate line archive (av_common.py:2189-2240).

**Problem.** **Given:** per-sweep points $q_i\in\mathbb{R}^3$ (metric frame); sweep grouping $s(i)$ by timestamp (the cockpit time-windows ~one sweep, so quality is judged per sweep); zoom band $[\zeta_{\min},\zeta_{\max}]$; target screen spacing $s_{px}$ (px/point); ground resolution $g(z)=156543\cdot\cos\varphi/2^z$ m/px giving level pitch $v(z)=s_{px}\, g(z)$; per-point surface variation $\sigma_i=\lambda_0/(\lambda_0{+}\lambda_1{+}\lambda_2)$ from $k$-NN covariance (geometric-information proxy).

**Decision variables:** a partition $\{L_z\}_{z=\zeta_{\min}}^{\zeta_{\max}}$ of the points — equivalently $\ell_i\in\{\zeta_{\min},\dots,\zeta_{\max}\}$ with each point in _exactly one_ level. The client renders the cumulative union $U_z=\bigcup_{z'\le z}L_{z'}$.

**Maximize** per-level spatial uniformity plus retained geometric information:
$$\max_{\ell}\ \sum_{z=\zeta_{\min}}^{\zeta_{\max}-1}\Big[\underbrace{\big|\,\mathrm{occ}_v(U_z)\,\big|}_{\text{occupied }v(z)\text{-voxels: coverage}} \;-\; \underbrace{\mathrm{excess}_v(U_z)}_{\text{multi-occupancy: clumping}} \;+\; \gamma\sum_{i\in U_z}\hat\sigma_i\Big],$$
where $\mathrm{occ}_v(U)$ = voxels of pitch $v(z)$ containing ≥1 point of $U$, $\mathrm{excess}_v(U)=\sum_c\max(0,|U\cap c|-1)$, and $\hat\sigma_i$ = normalized curvature (rewarding high-$\sigma$ points at coarse levels).

**Subject to:**

1. Partition: each point in exactly one $L_z$ (single home zoom ⇒ $O(N)$ storage, no per-zoom duplication);
2. Union completeness (lossless): $\bigcup_z L_z = \{1..N\}$ — $U_{\zeta_{\max}}$ reconstructs the full cloud (no-thinning principle);
3. Per-sweep independence: the objective and caps apply within each sweep $s$ separately;
4. Determinism: no RNG (reproducible builds, D6);
5. Voxel pitch tied to the tile pyramid: $v(z)=s_{px}\cdot 156543\cos\varphi/2^z$.

**Incumbent.** Greedy coarse-to-fine voxel claiming (av_common.py:2099-2186): at each level, voxelize the _remaining_ points at $v(z)$ (curvature-graded: flat-tier voxel = base·penalty, penalty $=1+(3-1)(1-\text{tier}/\text{span})\cdot\text{lvl\_frac}$, av_common.py:2077-2096), claim the first real point per occupied voxel, remove claimed, recurse; residual dumps at z21. Greedy prefix-claiming guarantees ≥1 point per occupied voxel per level (a maximal independent set on the voxel grid) but not max-min dispersion: representative choice is first-in-array, not centroid-nearest or farthest-point; the voxel grid has alignment artifacts versus blue noise; and $s_{px}=0.4$ was tuned from _one_ measured cumulative-points-vs-zoom sweep on Miami (av_common.py:2051-2056). The map-overview variant adds `LADDER_HEADROOM=3` to stop the ~50× residual from un-sparsing the kept top level (av_common.py:2210-2218) — a manual patch for the greedy's residual-dumping pathology. The general build-side assignment does not exist; the roadmap holds it behind a trigger (per-zoom duplication dominating archive size) with a deterministic home-zoom metric required (stt-packed-format-decisions.md:442-451).

**Class.** Potree/COPC/Entwine additive octree construction; farthest-point / Poisson-disk sampling (max-min dispersion is NP-hard; greedy FPS gives a 2-approx); progressive transmission / successive refinement in rate–distortion; feature-preserving, curvature-weighted point-cloud simplification. Offline, deterministic. The renderer-side refinement (global point-budget priority traversal) is the Potree insight the roadmap notes but is out of this slice's scope.

**Knobs.** `LOD_MIN_ZOOM=14`, `LOD_MAX_ZOOM=21`, `LOD_PX_PER_POINT=0.4` px; curvature: on, `LOD_CURV_K=12`, `LOD_CURV_LEVELS=5`, `LOD_FLAT_RATIO=3.0`, `LOD_FLAT_PCT=50`, `LOD_COMPLEX_PCT=92`; map variant: zooms 10–12, `MAP_LOD_PX_PER_POINT=0.6`, `MAP_LOD_SPACING_M=3.0`, `MAP_LOD_LADDER_HEADROOM=3`.

**Gap.** Replace first-in-voxel with farthest-point-style representative choice and evaluate the coverage/clumping objective directly; optimize $s_{px}$ per scene against a point-budget-at-zoom curve instead of one hand sweep; generalize the assignment into stt-build (PLANNED, explicitly counted out until the trigger fires) so any archive can opt into $O(N)$ additive storage — earthquakes stores 523K rows for 47.5K quakes, the measured duplication poster child (stt-packed-format-decisions.md:447-449). Measurable objectives: bytes stored per unique point, occupied-voxel coverage per zoom, edge-detail-per-point (the repo measured ~2–4× for the curvature path via lidar_summarize_eval.py).

## 5. Compression & byte budgeting

This subsystem decides how many bytes a published archive is allowed to cost, and which fidelity levers pay for reductions. It trades offline build CPU (paid once) against wire bytes (paid per fetch), and — under the project's no-thinning doctrine — trades _bounded, declared_ distortion (zoom clamp, temporal binning, quantization) against size, while feature deletion stays outside the admissible set by default. Two problems live here: the entropy-codec operating point (§5.1) and the flagship allocation of a byte budget across all admissible levers (§5.2).

Concepts formerly inventoried here now live elsewhere: the per-tile feature-drop budget (cardinality-constrained knapsack, opt-in lossy) — see §4 — per-tile feature-drop budget; the render-resolution quantization distortion bound and world-grid dedup constraint — see §3 — quantization under dedup preservation; overview load shaping (summary-tier triggering, H3/Quadbin scheme choice, per-feature zoom floors) — see §4 — summary-tier granularity & materialization; the sampled size-estimation and per-column cost-attribution subproblem that all byte decisions rest on — see §12 — sampled measurement & inspection.

### 5.1 zstd codec/level operating-point selection (write-once / serve-many)

**Setting.** The packed format is zstd-only: every blob (tile payload, directory page) is an independent frame, with `Compression::None` reserved for already-incompressible blobs (crates/stt-core/src/compression.rs:1-9,37-50). One archive-wide level threads from `stt-build --zstd-level` (default 3, crates/spatiotemporal-tiles/src/bin/stt-build.rs:153-159) through `compress_zstd_with_dict_level` (compression.rs:134-155); `--publish` bumps it to 19 (stt-build.rs:970-974), as does the `--auto` advice path (stt-build.rs:1729-1743). The rationale is codified in comments: zstd decode speed is level-independent, so build CPU is paid once while wire bytes are paid per fetch; measured savings at 19 vs 3 are −10..19%, with 19 ≈ 22 on STT tiles (compression.rs:52-61,128-133; stt-build.rs:153-157). A dictionary-training API exists (`train_zstd_dictionary_from_slices`, compression.rs:190-208), returning None on sparse samples — a per-collection ship-with-dict-or-not decision left to callers. Bounded decompression (compression.rs:80-115,177-183) is a decode-side security constraint, not a lever.

**Problem.** Given blobs $b_1,\dots,b_n$ with raw sizes $|b_i|$, expected lifetime fetch counts $f_i \ge 0$, zstd's compressed-size function $Z(b,\ell)$ and encode-time function $T(b,\ell)$ for level $\ell \in \{1,\dots,22\}$, identity-codec size $Z(b,\text{none}) = |b|$ with $T = 0$, and an optional dictionary $D$ trained on a sample (training cost $T_{train}(D)$; all frames sharing $D$ require $D$ shipped once and present at decode).

Decision variables: per-blob codec assignment $c_i \in \{\text{none}\} \cup \{1,\dots,22\}$; dictionary choice $D \in \{\varnothing\} \cup \mathcal{D}(d_{max})$ per blob collection.

$$\min_{c, D} \; \sum_{i=1}^{n} f_i \cdot Z(b_i; c_i, D) \; + \; \mathbb{1}[D \ne \varnothing]\,|D|$$

subject to $\sum_i T(b_i, c_i) + T_{train}(D) \le T_{build}$ (offline CPU budget); each frame must decode in isolation given at most $D$ (random access — no inter-blob compression state); the decode-latency constraint is vacuous because zstd decode time is independent of $\ell$. With a single archive-wide level the problem collapses to one dimension: $\ell^* = \min\{\ell : Z(\ell) \le (1+\varepsilon)\,\min_{\ell'} Z(\ell')\}$ subject to $T(\ell) \le T_{build}$ — a Pareto operating-point pick on the (rate, encode-time) curve.

**Incumbent.** A two-point constant policy: level 3 for iterate builds (compression.rs:57, stt-build.rs:158-159), level 19 for publish (stt-build.rs:970-974), justified by one offline measurement ("19 ≈ 22 on STT tiles, measured", compression.rs:59-61). No per-blob adaptivity: `compress` always zstds when asked (compression.rs:37-42), nothing checks whether a blob would be smaller stored raw, $f_i$ is never modeled, and dictionary training is never jointly searched with the level. The level is clamped, never tuned per dataset (compression.rs:139).

**Class.** Offline, deterministic operating-point selection on a rate/encode-time Pareto curve — trivially solvable by sweep in the 1-D collapse. Per-blob codec choice with a shared dictionary is a facility-location-flavored grouping problem; joint dictionary+level choice is expensive-black-box discrete optimization. Nothing here is NP-hard at the implemented granularity.

**Knobs.** `--zstd-level` (default 3, max accepted 22, practical ceiling 19); `--publish` (bool, sets 19); `train_zstd_dictionary` `max_size` (caller-chosen bytes). Manifest codec vocabulary is restricted to {"zstd","none"} (compression.rs:7-9).

**Gap.** PLANNED-shaped, none implemented: a per-blob store-raw-if-zstd-expands rule and per-pack trained dictionaries at level 19 would strictly reduce archive bytes; a per-dataset level sweep on the measured sample (measure.rs already runs the production encoder+zstd — see §12) could replace the folklore two-point policy with a measured $\ell^*$ per archive. Measurable objective: total archive bytes and bytes-on-wire per demo session at equal decode cost.

### 5.2 FLAGSHIP: archive byte-budget allocation across admissible fidelity levers (no-thinning constrained)

**Setting.** The project's size-reduction doctrine is a lever hierarchy, not a solver: to shrink an archive one may clamp the zoom range, coarsen or adapt temporal bucketing, add lossless temporal-LOD or additive summary tiers, quantize coordinates/attributes (lossy but error-bounded), raise the zstd level, and pick blob ordering — but never drop features by default. This is enforced structurally: `Advice.lossy: true` is never auto-applied and never joins `to_command` (crates/stt-optimize/src/advisors/mod.rs:8-10,53-64), and the feature-dropping `--maximum-tile-features` advice always leads with the non-destructive alternatives (advisors/budget.rs:5-9,108-131). The stand-in for a solver is `run_all` (advisors/mod.rs:70-77): quantize, temporal, layout, and budget advisors each evaluate one lever against one shared baseline measurement of the sample (quantize.rs:40-60) and emit threshold-gated advice; `stt-build --auto` applies only the non-lossy, non-suggestion-only subset (stt-build.rs:1729-1743). Temporal levers are threshold rules too (`--temporal-lod` at ≥5000 base buckets, `--adaptive-temporal` at events-per-day skew ≥10, advisors/temporal.rs:21-44). No target archive size $B$ exists anywhere in the CLI — the only "target bytes" is pack-file sizing (stt-build.rs:1017-1018) — so the human closes the loop.

**Problem.** Given a source feature multiset $F$ (with $N = |F|$ features, geometry, timestamps, property columns $1..m$), a target archive size $B$ (bytes), a size oracle $S(x)$ — true encoded+zstd archive bytes under lever setting $x$, evaluable exactly only by building, approximately by sample trial-encode (see §12 — sampled measurement) — and the per-lever distortion functions below.

Decision variables $x = (z_{\min}, z_{\max}, \Delta, \mathcal{L}, q, a, \pi, \ell, s, o)$:

- zoom range $z_{\min} \le z_{\max}$, $z \in \{0..22\}$ — a clamp removes pyramid levels while all features remain present at kept zooms;
- base temporal bucket $\Delta \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}$ (ms) and temporal-LOD tier set $\mathcal{L} = \{(k_j\Delta, \bar z_j)\}$ with $k_j \in \mathbb{Z}_{>1}$ strictly increasing — each tier is an _additive_ lossless re-bucketing, so it increases $S$ to decrease client fetch cost;
- coordinate quantization $q \in \{5,1,0.5,0.1,0.05,0.01\} \cup \{\infty\}$ meters ($\infty$ = Float64);
- attribute quantization $a \in \{0,1\}$ (auto UInt16) and per-column explicit steps $\pi_c \in \mathbb{R}_{>0} \cup \{\infty\}$;
- zstd level $\ell \in \{1..22\}$ (§5.1); summary tier $s \in \{\text{none}, \text{h3}, \text{quadbin}\}$ (additive; see §4); blob ordering $o$ (affects dedup, hence $S$; see §6).

Distortion is lexicographic, most-protected component first:

$$D(x) = \Big(\; \mathbb{1}[\hat F(x) \ne F] \;,\; D_{zoom} = z^{data}_{\max} - z_{\max} \;,\; D_{geo} = q/2 \;,\; D_{attr} = \max_c s_c(\pi_c, a)/2 \;,\; D_{time} = \Delta - \Delta_0 \;\Big)$$

where $\hat F(x)$ is the decodable base-tier feature multiset, $s_c$ is column $c$'s dequantization step, and $\Delta_0$ the native event resolution.

$$\min_x \; D(x) \quad \text{(lexicographic)} \qquad \text{s.t.} \qquad S(x) \le B$$

Hard admissibility constraints (the no-thinning rule): $\hat F(x) = F$ as a multiset at the base tier — the first lexicographic component must be 0; per-tile feature caps, densest-drop, and per-feature zoom floors that hide features lie _outside_ the admissible set unless a human opts in (see §4). Temporal-LOD tiers must be strict multiples of $\Delta$, sorted ascending (stt-build.rs:176-186). $q \ge q_{floor} \approx 0.0187$ m (i32 world-grid overflow floor, stt-core quantize.rs:109-115). Geometric error is bounded by render resolution: $q/2 \le \tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\tfrac{2\pi R \cos\varphi}{4 \cdot 256 \cdot 2^{z_{\max}}}$, the quarter-pixel rule ($R$ = Earth radius, $\varphi$ = dataset mid-latitude; full rate–distortion treatment in §3). Because levers interact — $q$ changes the zstd ratio; $o$ changes content-addressed dedup, which $q$'s world-anchored grid protects (§3, §6); $\mathcal{L}$ and $s$ _add_ bytes — $S$ is not separable in $x$.

**Incumbent.** No solver and no $B$: a single-pass, threshold-gated, one-lever-at-a-time advisory pipeline plus a human. Each advisor trial-encodes its lever alone against one shared baseline (advisors/quantize.rs:40-60 — "ONE baseline at build defaults, shared by both trials") and accepts on fixed constants (MIN_COORD_SHRINK=0.05, MIN_ATTR_SHRINK=0.03, quantize.rs:29-33; LOD_BUCKET_THRESHOLD=5000, ADAPTIVE_SKEW_THRESHOLD=10, temporal.rs:21-36; summary-tier volume/skew floors, advisors/budget.rs:27-44). `stt-build --auto` applies the non-lossy subset (stt-build.rs:1729-1743); every lossy lever requires explicit human opt-in (advisors/mod.rs:8-10). Structurally this is _one iteration of coordinate descent from the default point_ $x_0 = (\text{data-derived } z, 1h, \varnothing, \infty, 0, \infty, 3, \text{none}, \text{auto})$: the constraint $S(x) \le B$ is never checked, no interaction terms are measured (e.g. quantize jointly with level 19), and additive tiers ($\mathcal{L}$, $s$) are recommended without accounting their byte cost against any budget.

**Class.** Multiple-choice knapsack / discrete resource allocation (NP-hard in general; FPTAS when costs are separable, which $S$ is not); Lagrangian bit-allocation à la Shoham–Gersho rate–distortion — the classical codec analogue of allocating "rate" across levers at equalized marginal distortion-per-byte. Because $S(x)$ is a non-separable, expensive black box, the realistic solver class is derivative-free/Bayesian optimization over ~10 mostly-discrete dimensions with a cheap surrogate (sample trial encodes) and a lexicographic feasibility filter. Offline and deterministic.

**Knobs.** `--min-zoom`/`--max-zoom`; `--temporal-bucket` (default 1h); `--temporal-lod` (e.g. `1d@8,30d@4`); `--quantize-coords` (ladder 5..0.01 m); `--quantize-attrs-auto` / `--quantize-attr col=step`; `--zstd-level` (3/19); `--summary-tier` {h3,quadbin}; `--blob-ordering`; advisor acceptance thresholds 0.05/0.03/0.15 shrink, 5000 buckets, skew 10/20.

**Gap.** PLANNED (not implemented): a `--target-size B` mode running a Lagrangian sweep / MCKP over sample-measured $(\Delta S, \Delta D)$ per lever combination would (1) hit publish budgets deterministically instead of by human iteration, (2) capture the interactions the one-baseline design misses — quantized coordinates compress differently at level 19; summary and LOD tiers cost bytes the advisors currently recommend for free — and (3) turn the fixed 0.05/0.03 acceptance thresholds into shadow prices. Any such solver inherits the hard lexicographic filter: feature-dropping levers stay inadmissible without human opt-in (constraint documentation, not an oversight). Measurable objectives: archive bytes at fixed lexicographic distortion class, and number of human build iterations to reach a publish size.

## 6. Packs, content-addressed dedup & blob ordering

This subsystem decides the physical life of tile payload bytes on the CDN: which compressed blobs exist at all (deduplication), the single byte order they are laid down in, where that byte stream is cut into immutable content-addressed objects, and how the reader converts a needed-blob set into HTTP range requests. It trades at-rest and transferred bytes against request count and latency, directory size against locality, and cache granularity against object count — all under a hard determinism constraint: every choice must be a pure function of the blob set, because the choices fix content addresses and immutable-CDN caching requires identical rebuilds to re-derive identical names (spec §7 D6).

### 6.1 Content-addressed deduplication and shared-blob placement

**Setting.** Every tile payload is per-blob zstd-compressed with no shared dictionary (the fzstd TS reader must decode each blob standalone), then byte-identical compressed blobs collapse to one physical blob via a blake3(compressed)→index map; placement is fixed at first sight in curve order, so a static cell repeated across many time buckets resolves to a single `(pack_id, offset)` that all its directory entries reference (crates/stt-core/src/pack/mod.rs:1020-1150, dedup insert at 1116-1150). The blake3-128 address does triple duty: dedup key, integrity proof with no trusted side-channel (`verify_packed_objects`, pack/mod.rs:1638-1802), and cross-rebuild physical sharing on the CDN (spec §7 D6). Dedup also feeds the directory codec: consecutive entries referencing the same physical blob collapse into one RLE run, writing the heavy blob columns once per run — the temporal analogue of PMTiles collapsing identical ocean tiles (docs/spec/stt-packed-format.md:411-432). Upstream encoding is deliberately shaped to preserve byte identity: world-anchored (not per-tile) quantization was chosen because tile-local grids destroyed cross-tile dedup at +61% size (see §3 — coordinate quantization under dedup preservation).

The binding constraint here is a REJECTED design: inter-timestep delta chains (keyframe + P-frame), evaluated 2026-07-21 and recorded NO-GO because they break three load-bearing guarantees at once — standalone tile decode, the directory-only time-prune seek (`getTileIdsInBounds` never touches earlier buckets), and dedup + blob-run RLE itself, since delta blobs differ byte-wise even when the underlying state is identical, destroying the existing lossless collapse (docs/roadmap/stt-packed-format-decisions.md:276-295). Supporting evidence: MLT's 3–6× over MVT collapses to 1.12–1.96× once gzip is applied to both sides; DuckDB found Parquet `DELTA_BINARY_PACKED` made files ~3× larger under zstd; where deltas do win (Trajic: 25.4→4.3 bits) the predictor is the win and consumption must be sequential — random-seek scrub fails that precondition. Standing rule: if live/streamed updates are ever added, keyframe-bounded deltas from day one — never open chains. Two DEFERRED refinements survive with named revival triggers: geometry-blob sharing (split a static geometry column-group into its own blob so content addressing dedups it across temporal chunks — a reference, not a delta; decode becomes a two-blob fetch; decisions doc :414-421) and the quantized path-delta experiment (intra-tile deltas over Int32 world-grid coordinates, ~57% of payload, unmeasured, ship only if >1%; decisions doc :709-724).

**Problem.** Given tiles $t \in T$, each with compressed payload bytes $b_t$ of length $\ell_t$; the byte-equality partition $T = E_1 \uplus \dots \uplus E_M$ ($t \sim t' \iff b_t = b_{t'}$, well-defined because compression is deterministic); the fixed directory order $d$ (by $(zoom, hilbert, t^{start}, \dots)$); the layout/query cost machinery of §6.3; and prices $\alpha$ (per stored+served byte) and $\beta$ (per directory byte, on the cold-start critical path).

Decide, for each equality class $e$: a copy count $r_e \ge 1$, layout positions for the $r_e$ physical copies, and an assignment $a: T \to \text{copies}$ mapping each directory entry to one copy.

$$\min_{r,\,\text{positions},\,a} \;\; \alpha \sum_{e=1}^{M} r_e\, \ell_e \; + \; \sum_{q} w_q\, \mathrm{cost}\big(q;\, \text{layout}, a\big) \; + \; \beta \cdot \mathrm{DirBytes}(a)$$

where $\mathrm{cost}$ is the blended range-read cost of §6.3 with each tile in $q$ fetching its assigned copy, and $\mathrm{DirBytes}(a) \propto R(a)$, the number of maximal runs of consecutive entries in $d$ sharing one physical copy (RLE: blob columns cost $O(1)$ per run, not per entry; back-references to non-contiguous blobs cost a flagged raw offset — spec §4 lines 426-432).

Subject to:

- Integrity/addressing: physical bytes are immutable and named by blake3-128 (128-bit collision safety at fleet object counts, spec §7 D2; frozen — never a second addressing path);
- Standalone decode: every blob decodes independently — no shared compression dictionary (fzstd, spec §5 step 2) and no inter-blob reference chains (the delta-chain NO-GO above); equal states must yield equal bytes or dedup and RLE are forfeit;
- u32 blob length/uncompressed-size fields (pack/mod.rs:466-475, 1127-1139);
- Determinism of the whole map $(r, \text{positions}, a)$ under rebuilds, which requires byte-deterministic encoding upstream (Arrow ≥59 sorted-metadata fix, spec §7 D6 layer 2).

Setting $r_e \equiv 1$ recovers pure dedup; allowing $r_e > 1$ trades bytes for locality (a duplicate placed inside a hot cluster removes a long back-reference read). Incumbent fixes $r_e = 1$, position = first occurrence in curve order, $a$ = the unique copy.

**Incumbent.** Exact greedy: compress in parallel chunks, then a strictly sequential pass keyed on blake3(compressed) — the first occurrence streams into the current pack and fixes the class's single placement forever; later occurrences only add a directory reference (pack/mod.rs:1086-1150). This is optimal for the pure storage term ($r_e=1$ minimizes $\sum_e r_e \ell_e$) and $O(n)$, but it optimizes neither locality nor directory bytes: a blob first seen in a cold corner of the curve is a far back-reference for every hot consumer (each access outside the coalescing gap costs a full extra range request), and the run count $R(a)$ is whatever curve order happens to produce. Granularity is the whole compressed blob — two tiles differing in one property byte share nothing. Measured anchor: blob dedup ratio 0.815 on earthquakes-v2 (decisions doc lines 60-66).

**Class.** Exact hash-based deduplication (the trivial part); the joint placement problem is replica/data placement, NP-hard in general and related to uncapacitated facility location (copies = facilities, consumer entries = clients, connection cost = marginal range-read cost); the RLE term is a consecutive-retrieval/arrangement problem (maximize run lengths). Sub-blob sharing maps to content-defined-chunking dedup under a random-access constraint that inter-blob deltas were shown to violate (the NO-GO record); optimal multi-blob factoring is set-cover-flavored (NP-hard).

**Knobs.** `--zstd-level` 1..=22 (default 3, publish 19 — write-once/serve-many; see §5); `--pack-memory-budget` (spill file, output-invariant, pack/mod.rs:639-651); encoder choices that preserve or destroy cross-tile byte identity (world-grid quantization §3, time-delta compaction §2).

**Gap.** (1) Locality-aware duplication: for classes whose consumers span ≥2 distant curve clusters, duplicating the blob under a byte budget can strictly reduce blended query cost and directory back-references — both terms already instrumented. (2) Placement by consumer centroid instead of first-seen: a 1-median on the line, polynomial. (3) DEFERRED: sub-blob geometry sharing and quantized path-delta (above, with revival triggers); REJECTED: inter-timestep delta chains (above, with reasons). (4) Dedup is measured but never drives decisions — no verb reports which classes are hot-and-far.

### 6.2 HTTP range-request coalescing: the gap parameter as a request/byte exchange rate

**Setting.** When a query needs many blobs from one pack, the reader groups needed entries by `pack_id`, sorts by offset, and fuses two needed ranges into one HTTP range request whenever at most `coalesceGapBytes` unneeded bytes lie between them; a range never bridges two pack objects (spec §6 step 4, docs/spec/stt-packed-format.md:880-882; constant `DEFAULT_RANGE_COALESCE_GAP = 2 MiB` at packages/core/src/archive.ts:149, fuse rule at 2164, per-pack grouping at 3171-3199). The identical greedy with the same constant serves a second workload: adjacent directory leaf-page ranges coalesce in `fetchAndMergePages` (archive.ts:2157-2170) — the leaf region of a `.sttd` is just another immutable object (the leaf breakpoints themselves are §7's problem). This one rule is the cost model everything upstream optimizes against: `ordering_sim` reproduces it exactly (ordering_sim.rs:266-330) and derives the blended ranking cost "bytes + reads × gap" from it (ordering_sim.rs:115-121). The constant was raised 512 KB → 2 MB on an explicit economic argument: on free-egress R2, over-fetched gap bytes cost only client bandwidth while every extra request costs latency and a request-op (archive.ts:137-146). Fused groups dispatch through a bounded, prioritized pool — scheduling and priority are §9's problem. The reader maintains a dual-EWMA throughput estimator fed by completed coalesced reads (archive.ts:1051, 1740) but does not feed it back into the gap — adaptation is unimplemented.

**Problem.** Given, within one immutable object (a pack `.sttp`, or the leaf region of a `.sttd`), the needed ranges of a query sorted by offset, with interior unneeded gaps $g_1, \dots, g_{r-1} \ge 0$ (bytes between consecutive needed ranges); per-request latency overhead $L$ seconds (RTT + queueing + per-op cost), throughput $\theta$ bytes/sec, and needed bytes $N$ (fixed).

Decide fuse indicators $u_j \in \{0,1\}$ for each gap ($u_j = 1$: bridge gap $j$, transferring its bytes; $u_j = 0$: split into a new request). Minimize total fetch time (serial model):

$$\min_{u \in \{0,1\}^{r-1}} \; \frac{N + \sum_j u_j\, g_j}{\theta} \; + \; L \cdot \Big(1 + \sum_j (1 - u_j)\Big)$$

Equivalently, in bytes-equivalent with $G^{\ast} \equiv L\,\theta$ (the bandwidth-delay/request price): $\min \sum_j [\,u_j g_j + (1-u_j) G^{\ast}\,] + \text{const}$. The objective is separable per gap, so the threshold rule $u_j = \mathbb{1}[g_j \le G^{\ast}]$ is **exactly optimal** — the incumbent's greedy is the true optimum _for its own cost model_. The genuine decision problem is choosing $G$ and the model's unmodeled terms:

$$\min_{G} \; \mathbb{E}_{L, \theta, \text{cache}, \text{concurrency}} \big[\, T_{\text{fetch}}(G) \,\big]$$

where the expectation ranges over session network conditions, edge-cache state (a warm pack has $L \approx$ edge RTT, cold has origin RTT), and the parallel dispatch pool (with $k$-way concurrency the marginal cost of an extra request is $\approx L/k$, shrinking the optimal gap).

Subject to:

- Per-object: a fused run cannot cross an object boundary (hard — HTTP range semantics per URL);
- Concurrency ceiling per coalesced batch (archive.ts:150);
- Correctness: each member sliced from a fused buffer must CRC-verify (archive.ts:345), with 206/Content-Range and exact-length validation so a truncating origin is detected;
- No max-fetch-size cap ("the gap only controls how aggressively neighbours fuse", archive.ts:146);
- Deterministic mirroring between reader and simulator — the constants must match or `measured` layout picks optimize the wrong reader (ordering_sim.rs:31-38).

**Incumbent.** Greedy threshold fuse at a fixed $G = 2$ MiB (archive.ts:149; mirrored at ordering_sim.rs:29). The greedy is provably optimal for the separable linear cost, so the heuristic is the constant itself: $G^{\ast} = L\theta$ varies by an order of magnitude across sessions (edge-warm hit ~20 ms/100 Mbps → $G^{\ast} \approx 250$ KB; cold origin ~200 ms → ≈2.5 MB; high-concurrency pools shrink it further), yet one constant serves all, and the throughput estimator that could calibrate it exists but is unwired. Second-order effect: the build-time ordering choice bakes this $G$ into content-addressed layouts forever, so a future reader-side gap change silently invalidates `measured` picks.

**Class.** Interval merging on a line (polynomial; here separable-optimal). The $G$-selection problem is classic latency–bandwidth tradeoff tuning — bandwidth-delay product, TCP pacing, OS readahead sizing, S3/Parquet readers' "merge small ranges" heuristics with per-store tuned gaps; online adaptation is stochastic approximation / a bandit over $G$. Deterministic and offline per batch; stochastic across sessions.

**Knobs.** `coalesceGapBytes` (default 2 MiB; per-archive override `ArchiveOptions.coalesceGapBytes`, archive.ts:147-149, 1168-1171); concurrency ceiling per batch (archive.ts:150, re-cappable at 1290); simulator twins in `SimOptions` (ordering_sim.rs:70-86).

**Gap.** (1) Adaptive $G \leftarrow \hat{L} \cdot \hat{\theta}$ from the existing dual-EWMA estimators per archive/session; measurable on the frame-cost bench as wall time-to-frame and request count. (2) Cache-state-aware pricing: warm-edge packs deserve a smaller gap (partially observable from response headers). (3) Concurrency-aware pricing $L/k$ instead of $L$. (4) Co-versioning: record the gap assumed at build in the manifest next to `blobOrdering` so order-audit can flag reader/layout drift. All four unimplemented.

### 6.3 Blob linear ordering: minimum linear arrangement under range-coalescing cost

**Setting.** At finalize the packed writer sorts every buffered tile blob into a single byte order before cutting packs; the order determines which query footprints are byte-contiguous and therefore how many coalesced range requests (and how much over-read) a client pays. The order is a 4-tuple sort key `space_time_key` (crates/stt-core/src/curve.rs:240-272) with zoom always leading (the directory codec requires zoom-major, curve.rs:10-11), extended by a total tiebreak `(z, x, y, time_start, temporal_bucket_ms)` so the layout — and every blake3 address derived from it — is byte-reproducible (pack/mod.rs:983-1008; spec §7 D6). Four concrete orderings exist: SpatialMajor `(zoom, hilbert2D, time)`, TimeMajor `(zoom, time_bucket, hilbert2D)`, and Hilbert3/Morton3 (3D curves over `(x, y, time_bucket)`, curve.rs:145-233), forming with `auto` the 5-valued `BlobOrdering` enum. The writer resolves the ordering by the `auto` cardinality heuristic or the opt-in `measured` simulation (pack/mod.rs:946-981) and records it in `manifest.blobOrdering` (pack/mod.rs:1311); upstream, the stt-optimize layout advisor makes the same pick pre-build as part of the recommend meta-loop (see §12).

**Problem.** Given a set $B=\{1,\dots,n\}$ of unique tile blobs, where blob $i$ has zoom $z_i$, grid cell $(x_i,y_i)$, 2D-Hilbert index $h_i$, time-bucket index $\tau_i = \lfloor t^{start}_i / \beta \rfloor$ (bucket size $\beta$ ms), and at-rest byte length $\ell_i$; a coalescing gap $G$ (bytes); a pack size target $C$ (bytes); and a workload $\mathcal{Q}$ of queries, each query $q \subseteq B$ (the set of blobs a session needs at once) with weight $w_q \ge 0$.

Decide a bijection $\pi : B \to \{1,\dots,n\}$ (the on-disk byte order).

Derived quantities: offsets $o_i^{\pi} = \sum_{j:\,\pi(j)<\pi(i)} \ell_j$; pack assignment $p^{\pi}(i)$ produced by the greedy capacity-$C$ cut over the sequence (§6.5). For a query $q$: within each pack, list the needed blobs in $\pi$-order; consecutive needed blobs fuse into one range request iff the unneeded bytes between them are $\le G$; a fused run is force-closed at every pack boundary. Let $\mathrm{reads}(q,\pi)$ be the number of resulting runs and $\mathrm{bytes}(q,\pi)$ the total bytes transferred (needed bytes plus interior swept gaps; leading/trailing gaps free).

Minimize the blended, self-consistent cost (a request is priced at exactly $G$ bytes because the reader over-reads up to $G$ bytes to save one request — §6.2):

$$\min_{\pi} \; \sum_{q \in \mathcal{Q}} w_q \Big[ \mathrm{bytes}(q,\pi) \; + \; G \cdot \mathrm{reads}(q,\pi) \Big]$$

Subject to:

- Zoom-major: $z_i < z_j \Rightarrow \pi(i) < \pi(j)$ (directory codec requirement);
- Determinism: $\pi$ must be a pure function of the blob key set (no RNG, no arrival-order dependence), because $\pi$ fixes every content-addressed pack name;
- The directory index order is _not_ a degree of freedom — it stays $(zoom, hilbert, t^{start})$ regardless of $\pi$; only payload bytes move.

Decision domain: $\pi \in S_n$ restricted to zoom-major permutations. The incumbent restricts it to 4 curve-keyed candidates $\{\text{SpatialMajor}, \text{TimeMajor}, \text{Hilbert3}, \text{Morton3}\}$, of which 3 are selectable. The objective is exactly `ordering_sim::OrderingCost::cost` (ordering_sim.rs:108-122, 186) — NOT raw request count: count alone called a 669 MiB fused read "2 reads = cheapest" on drifters versus spatial's 184 MiB/94 requests (decisions doc §5, lines 298-311); the recorded anti-lesson is never to rank by request count or adjacency breaks alone. A secondary un-modeled objective is playback buffered-range runway, acknowledged only as a text caveat (order_audit.rs:167-188). The ordering is resolved from native-(max-zoom)-tier tiles only; coarse LOD tiers ride along (pack/mod.rs:932, order_audit.rs:70-83).

**Incumbent.** A three-layer heuristic over the fixed candidate family. (1) `auto` (default): `BlobOrdering::choose(space_bits, time_bits)` — a pure threshold rule on occupied-extent cardinalities: `time_bits ≤ 1` → SpatialMajor; `time_bits > space_bits + 3` → SpatialMajor; else Hilbert3; it can never pick TimeMajor (curve.rs:83-97; occupied-bbox space bits at pack/mod.rs:927-944). (2) `measured` (opt-in, `--blob-ordering measured` / `stt-optimize order-audit`): exhaustively simulates all 4 candidates with the writer's exact sort key and greedy pack cut, ranks by blended cost, stable-tiebreaks to CANDIDATES order (spatial wins ties), and never selects Morton3 (ordering_sim.rs:139-205; wired at pack/mod.rs:946-971; audit at crates/stt-optimize/src/order_audit.rs:64-165). Build-path approximation: samples use uncompressed payload lengths versus the writer's compressed cut, shifting simulated pack boundaries early but uniformly across candidates (pack/mod.rs:960-964); the audit uses compressed `entry.length` and infers the pack target as max pack length (order_audit.rs:69,88-95). (3) The pre-build layout advisor synthesizes one TileSample per occupied $(x,y,\tau)$ cell at the recommended max zoom and runs the same simulator (layout.rs:205-239); under 8 synthesized tiles it falls back to a rule table (Localized + <7 d → spatial; Global/Regional + >90 d → time-major; else keep auto) at Low confidence (layout.rs:127-139, 267-306), demotes spatial-on-multi-bucket advice to suggestion_only with a playback caveat (layout.rs:176-195), and appends `--pack-size 128` for archives estimated >5 GiB (layout.rs:310-335). Fleet measurement: across 36 archives the measured picks were spatial 16 / time-major 12 / hilbert3 8 / morton3 0, and the fixed heuristic mis-picked 12 of 36 (decisions doc lines 318-320).

**Class.** Minimum Linear Arrangement / Optimal Linear Ordering (NP-hard, GT42); the Consecutive Retrieval Problem in file organization (existence poly via PQ-trees, optimization NP-hard); hypergraph bandwidth/storage layout for range queries; space-filling curves (Hilbert/Morton) are the classic constant-factor locality heuristic family, cf. Hilbert worst-case clustering bounds (Moon et al.) and cache-oblivious graph reordering (Gorder, RCM). Offline, deterministic, with a finite explicitly-enumerable incumbent search space of 4.

**Knobs.** `--blob-ordering {auto|measured|spatial|time-major|hilbert3|morton3}` (default `auto`); `ORDERING_BALANCE_BITS = 3` (curve.rs:23); `CURVE_BITS_CAP = 21` bits/axis (curve.rs:17); simulation gap $G$ = 2 MiB and pack target 64 MiB (ordering_sim.rs:29,38).

**Gap.** The true decision space is all zoom-major permutations; the incumbent searches 4 points. Candidates strictly more expressive than any global curve — per-zoom hybrids (spatial-major shallow, time-major deep), per-Hilbert-subtree time interleaving, or block-hybrid layouts (time-major within spatial super-cells of ~pack size, matching the per-pack coalescing structure) — can already be scored by the existing simulator; the measurable objective is its blended cost plus a playback-runway term. Second gap: `auto` can never pick TimeMajor yet `measured` chose it on 12/36 fleet archives; promoting `measured` to default is PLANNED, gated solely on the workload-weighting question of §6.4 (decisions doc :320-323). Third: ordering is chosen from native-tier tiles but applied to all tiers. Fourth: the bucket-count × ordering interaction is real (bucket count feeds the simulation, layout.rs:207 — a coarser bucket can flip the measured winner) and never explored. REJECTED: axis rebasing of the curve inputs — measured to lose three times; a standing do-not-repropose.

### 6.4 Surrogate workload design for the ordering picker

**Setting.** The `measured` picker cannot observe real sessions, so it evaluates candidate layouts against a hand-designed two-query surrogate: "scrub" = a bounded viewport played across all time, and "pan" = the same viewport at its single densest time bucket, weighted equally (ordering_sim.rs:139-194). The viewport is the "central band" — the middle quarter $[\lfloor 3m/8\rfloor, \lfloor 5m/8\rfloor)$ of the $m$ occupied cells in 2D-Hilbert order (ordering_sim.rs:343-354). The design encodes a hard-won correction: an earlier pan query read one instant across the entire map — an access no client issues — and under per-pack coalescing it systematically over-preferred time-major on every space-wide dataset (ordering_sim.rs:335-341). The workload deliberately omits time-playback buffering (sequential window advance needing contiguous buffered runway); that omission is patched by a textual caveat, not the cost model — `playback_caveat_for` fires whenever spatial wins on a multi-bucket dataset (order_audit.rs:167-188).

**Problem.** Given the candidate layout family $\Pi$ (the 4 curve orderings), the per-query cost $c(q,\pi) = \mathrm{bytes}(q,\pi) + G\cdot\mathrm{reads}(q,\pi)$, and an unknown true session workload $W^{\ast}$ — a distribution over query sets $q \subseteq B$ generated by real usage (pans, zooms, scrubs, continuous playback) — assumed drawn from an uncertainty class $\mathcal{W}$ of plausible mixes.

Decide a surrogate workload $\widehat{W} = \{(q_k, w_k)\}$ — a small set of synthetic queries with weights, computable from the tile inventory alone — inducing the picker

$$\hat{\pi}(\widehat{W}) = \arg\min_{\pi \in \Pi_{sel}} \sum_k w_k \, c(q_k, \pi), \qquad \Pi_{sel} = \Pi \setminus \{\text{Morton3}\}.$$

Minimize the regret of the induced pick against the true workload — robust form:

$$\min_{\widehat{W}} \; \max_{W^{\ast} \in \mathcal{W}} \; \Big[ \mathbb{E}_{q \sim W^{\ast}}\, c(q, \hat{\pi}(\widehat{W})) \; - \; \min_{\pi \in \Pi_{sel}} \mathbb{E}_{q \sim W^{\ast}}\, c(q, \pi) \Big]$$

(or the Bayesian variant $\min_{\widehat W} \mathbb{E}_{W^\ast \sim \mathcal{D}}[\text{regret}]$ given a prior $\mathcal{D}$ over session types).

Subject to:

- $\widehat{W}$ must be a deterministic pure function of the blob key set (it feeds content-address-determining decisions; all integer arithmetic, stable sorts, deterministic densest-bucket tie — ordering_sim.rs:14-19, 374-381);
- Queries must be client-realizable (bounded viewports; no whole-map instant reads — the pan-bias incident);
- Evaluation budget: $|\widehat{W}| \cdot |\Pi|$ simulator passes at build time;
- Shared definition with the FE showcase probe: the `floor(m·0.375)..floor(m·0.625)` integer math must match spaceCurve.ts (ordering_sim.rs:351-353).

Decision variables: the viewport selector (which Hilbert band, what fraction), the temporal selector per query (all-time vs one bucket vs sliding window), and the weight vector $w \in \Delta$. The incumbent fixes all three: band $= [3m/8, 5m/8)$, queries $= \{$scrub(band, all $\tau$), pan(band, $\arg\max_{\tau}$ bytes)$\}$, $w = (1,1)$.

**Incumbent.** Fixed two-query equal-weight surrogate: `central_band` (ordering_sim.rs:343-354) + `scrub_hits` (359-362) + `pan_hits` (367-389), summed 1:1 in `evaluate` (177-186). Playback is handled outside the optimization as a warning string (order_audit.rs:176-188). The gap versus optimum is structural: a playback-dominated dataset (the project's headline use case) gets its ordering chosen without a playback term; a pan-only static dataset pays for a scrub term it never issues.

**Class.** Robust/minimax-regret optimization under distributional uncertainty; surrogate benchmark design / workload synthesis for physical database design (index/layout advisors, cf. Microsoft AutoAdmin workload compression); statistical decision theory (a plug-in estimator over a 3-point action space). A telemetry-learned extension would be a stochastic bandit over layouts, but layout is write-once, so it is a one-shot offline decision.

**Knobs.** Band fraction (fixed 1/4, centered); query weights (fixed 1:1); pan bucket rule (densest-by-bytes, smallest index on ties); `coalesce_gap_bytes` and `pack_bytes` in `SimOptions` (ordering_sim.rs:70-86).

**Gap.** (1) PLANNED: an access-pattern-weighted picker (decisions doc :320-323 — "the simulator's scrub/pan query mix is equal-weighted and fixed… waits on that weighting decision"); this is exactly the open gate on making `measured` the default. (2) Add a third canonical query — playback: a sliding window $[\tau, \tau + \Delta]$ advanced sequentially over the band, costed with a buffered-runway constraint — turning the order-audit caveat into a term the ranking feels; the measurable objective is stall time of the player's buffered-range gate (observed in production: `blobOrdering=spatial` silently stalls playback). (3) Weight queries per dataset from telemetry or from `layer_hint`/demo type instead of 1:1.

### 6.5 Pack cutting: contiguity-constrained partitioning of the blob stream into CDN objects

**Setting.** After ordering and dedup, the writer cuts the byte stream into content-addressed pack objects: `PackStreamWriter::append` streams each unique blob into the current pack's temp file and seals/renames when the next blob would exceed `pack_target_bytes` (default 64 MiB), never splitting a blob — a lone oversized blob owns its own pack (pack/mod.rs:1552-1631; `DEFAULT_PACK_TARGET_BYTES` at 174-176). The cut matters twice: each pack is the immutable cache unit a dumb CDN serves natively (a >512 MB single file can never edge-cache — the format's raison d'être, pack/mod.rs:1-10), and each boundary force-closes reader-side coalescing, since a range request never bridges two objects (spec §6 step 4, docs/spec/stt-packed-format.md:880-882; simulated at ordering_sim.rs:245-259, 287-293).
