The 64 MiB target is a frozen decision balancing cache granularity and parallel range reads against object count and R2 GET-ops to warm the edge (spec §7 D1, lines 894-897).

**Problem.** Given the fixed byte order $\sigma$ of $m$ unique compressed blobs with lengths $\ell_1, \dots, \ell_m$ (in layout order), an 8-byte per-object magic prelude, a hard CDN per-object cap $C_{\max}$ (512 MB on Cloudflare), the query workload $\mathcal{Q}$ with the coalescing cost of §6.3, and unit prices $\lambda_{obj}$ (per-object cost: R2 PUT/GET ops, one manifest `PackRef`, one cold-edge miss per pack) and $\lambda_{inv}$ (cache-granularity cost: expected re-upload/invalidation waste when data changes).

Decide cut points $0 = c_0 < c_1 < \dots < c_K = m$, defining packs $P_k = \{c_{k-1}+1, \dots, c_k\}$ with sizes $S_k = 8 + \sum_{i \in P_k} \ell_i$.

$$\min_{K,\,c} \;\; \sum_{q \in \mathcal{Q}} w_q \, \mathrm{cost}\big(q;\, \sigma, c\big) \; + \; \lambda_{obj} \, K \; + \; \lambda_{inv} \sum_{k=1}^{K} \mathbb{E}\big[\text{invalidated bytes} \mid \text{pack } k \text{ touched}\big]$$

where $\mathrm{cost}(q;\sigma,c)$ is the blended range-read cost in which every boundary $c_k$ landing inside a query's would-be coalesced run adds one extra request (the force-close).

Subject to:

- Contiguity: packs are contiguous segments of $\sigma$ (required by content addressing and the streaming writer);
- Capacity: $S_k \le C_{\max}$ for all $k$; soft target $S_k \le C$ except singleton packs ($|P_k| = 1$ exempt — blobs are never split, the atomic addressable unit, spec §5 step 3);
- Determinism: cuts are a pure function of $(\sigma, \ell, C)$ (reproducible content addresses);
- Streaming discipline: only the open pack's handle resident, same-filesystem atomic rename (pack/mod.rs:1500-1511).

Decision variables: $K \in \mathbb{N}$ and cut vector $c \in \mathbb{N}^{K-1}$ (equivalently a binary cut/no-cut after each blob), plus the scalar target $C$ when treated as tunable.

**Incumbent.** Greedy next-fit on the fixed sequence: cut before blob $i$ iff the open pack is non-empty and $S + \ell_i > C$ (pack/mod.rs:1558-1564). For a fixed sequence and pure segment-count objective, cut-as-late-as-possible is provably optimal (fewest segments), so the incumbent is exact for $\min K$ s.t. capacity — but it is boundary-oblivious: it never nudges a cut to align with a Hilbert-subtree, zoom, or time-bucket cluster boundary, so a hot viewport's coalesced run can straddle two packs when moving the cut a few blobs earlier (one slightly-underfull pack) would have kept it whole. The trade-off constants $\lambda_{obj}, \lambda_{inv}$ are implicit in the frozen 64 MiB choice rather than derived from measured RTT, egress pricing, or update cadence. The ordering simulator replays this exact greedy cut per candidate so measured picks see boundary effects; the build-path simulation, however, models the cut on pre-dedup uncompressed lengths (pack/mod.rs:960-964). No boundary-placement optimization exists or is planned.

**Class.** Sequential/ordered partitioning ("chains-on-chains" — polynomial DP for additive per-segment objectives; the coalescing term is non-additive across segments but DP-decomposable per query set); bin packing degenerates to trivial next-fit under contiguity; block-boundary alignment in storage systems (Parquet row-group sizing, COPC chunking). Offline, deterministic, polynomial for most variants — this is not the NP-hard part of the subsystem.

**Knobs.** `--pack-size` (MiB, default 64; pack/mod.rs:174-176), clamped to ≥1 byte at create (pack/mod.rs:623); the audit infers a built archive's target as $\max_k S_k$ floored at 1 MiB (order_audit.rs:88-95); the layout advisor bumps to 128 MiB above 5 GiB estimated archive size (§6.3).

**Gap.** (1) Boundary-aware cutting: a shortest-path DP over cut positions with edge cost = incremental blended query cost + $\lambda_{obj}$ optimizes boundaries exactly in $O(m \cdot C/\ell_{min})$, stays byte-deterministic, and is measurable against the same `ordering_sim` cost that currently treats cuts as exogenous. (2) Principled $C$: derive the target from measured per-request overhead and edge-warm cost instead of a constant — tiny-blob datasets (earthquakes ~1.1 KB avg) versus huge-blob ones (taxi ~220 KB) plausibly want different targets. (3) The build-time simulation's pre-dedup uncompressed lengths make simulated boundaries drift from real ones on dedup-heavy datasets.

## 7. Directory, index pages & remote random access

This subsystem decides how a reader turns a query — a viewport, a time window, a tile id — into the minimal set of HTTP range requests against immutable CDN objects: how the tile index is encoded, how it is split into independently fetchable pages, how each page advertises what it might contain, and how many round trips stand between a cold open and the first decoded tile. The resources traded are at-rest index bytes, cold-path bytes fetched, billed GET requests, round-trip latencies, and client memory. Byte-range coalescing of pack and leaf reads — the request/byte exchange-rate problem — is treated in §6 (see §6 — HTTP range coalescing); it appears here only as the $\mathrm{coalesce}(\cdot)$ operator inside §7.2's objective.

### 7.1 Directory entry ordering & delta/varint/RLE codec compactness

**Setting.** The v6 directory codec (Rust encoder at crates/stt-core/src/directory.rs:217-341, mirrored byte-for-byte by the TS decoder at packages/core/src/directory.ts:347-517 decode / 576-721 encode) serializes $N$ tile entries as columnar delta + zig-zag LEB128 varints over the key columns (zoom, hilbert, x, y, time_start, duration, feature_count, bucket, variant) and collapses consecutive entries pointing at the same physical blob into runs, storing the heavy blob columns (pack id, offset, length, uncompressed size, crc32c) once per run. A pack-relative offset-contiguity sentinel makes sequential archives pay ~1 byte for the entire offset column; pack ids are delta-coded across runs. An optional trailing covering section stores cover_t_min deltas, emitted only when _every_ entry carries one (all-or-nothing). The encoded size is entirely determined by the sort order, which the encoder hard-codes.

**Problem.** Given a finite entry set $E=\{e_1,\dots,e_N\}$ where each entry has key columns $k(e)=(z,h,x,y,t_s,t_e,f,b,v)$ and blob columns $\beta(e)=(p,o,\ell,u,c)$, the LEB128 cost function $\lambda(n)=\max(1,\lceil\mathrm{bits}(n)/7\rceil)$, and the zig-zag map $\zeta(d)$: choose a permutation $\pi$ of $\{1..N\}$ minimizing the wire size

$$S(\pi)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\text{col}\in K}\lambda\big(\zeta(\text{col}_{\pi(i)}-\text{col}_{\pi(i-1)})\big)\;+\;\sum_{r\in R(\pi)}\Big(\lambda(|r|)+\lambda(\zeta(\Delta p_r))+c_{\text{off}}(r)+\lambda(\ell_r)+\lambda(u_r)+4\Big)$$

where $K$ is the key-column set, $R(\pi)$ is the set of maximal runs of $\pi$-consecutive entries with identical $\beta$ (within one pack), and $c_{\text{off}}(r)=1$ if run $r$ starts exactly at the previous run's $o+\ell$ in the same pack (sentinel) else $1+\lambda(o_r)$. Subject to: (i) lossless round-trip of every field; (ii) $\pi$ is a deterministic function of $E$ (byte-reproducible builds); (iii) $\pi$ is a total order on a key readers can range-partition on — the paged container requires leaves to be contiguous slices of one global monotone order (verified by `verify_paged_structure`); (iv) decoder DoS guard $8(N+R)\le|\text{buf}|$. Both the delta-magnitude term and the run-count term depend on $\pi$. A secondary per-corpus decision is whether to emit the covering section (currently all-or-nothing, not per-entry).

**Incumbent.** A fixed lexicographic sort by (zoom, hilbert, time_start, variant_id) at crates/stt-core/src/directory.rs:219, followed by greedy maximal-run RLE over that fixed order (directory.rs:225-253), the offset-contiguity sentinel with per-pack reset (directory.rs:298-323), and the all-or-nothing cover section (directory.rs:330-338). The Hilbert curve is the spatial-locality heuristic that keeps deltas small and makes static-cell time-runs adjacent; the order is never optimized per dataset — it is a format constant.

**Class.** Minimum-cost Hamiltonian path / asymmetric TSP with $d(a,b)=\sum_{\text{col}}\lambda(\zeta(\text{col}_b-\text{col}_a))$ — NP-hard; minimum linear arrangement; consecutive-block arrangement (maximizing duplicate-blob adjacency for RLE). Space-filling-curve ordering is the standard constant-factor locality heuristic (PMTiles v3 lineage).

**Knobs.** None at runtime — the sort key and column schema are format constants (DIRECTORY_VERSION=6, directory.rs:126). The only lever affecting entry adjacency is the tiler's blob ordering (spatial vs time-major), which changes offset contiguity, not key deltas.

**Gap.** A per-dataset order search (time-major vs hilbert-major sort, or column-permutation-aware delta bases) could shrink the at-rest directory measurably where time cardinality dominates spatial cardinality; a per-entry cover-presence bitmap would keep the tight-bound prune for a corpus with one uncovered entry, which the all-or-nothing rule currently forgoes. Neither is implemented or scheduled. Measurable objective: at-rest .sttd bytes and (via §7.2) cold leaf bytes fetched.

### 7.2 Paged-directory partitioning: page breakpoints under expected cold-lookup cost

**Setting.** The writer sorts entries into directory order and slices them into fixed-size chunks of `page_entries` (default 4096), encoding each chunk with the unchanged v6 codec as its own zstd frame, plus a fixed-width root table of 52-byte descriptors carrying each leaf's byte range and pruning bounds (crates/stt-core/src/directory_page.rs:364-488). Paging is adaptive: archives under `paged_directory_min_entries` (8192) get a single whole-load frame, and the TS reader whole-loads any paged directory ≤ 256 KiB at rest. On a cold query the reader fetches the root prefix (one range GET), prunes leaves by zoom ∧ bbox ∧ time, and streams in only survivors — cold cost proportional to query footprint, not dataset size. The 1024–4096 sweet spot and the descriptor choice were frozen by offline simulations, not an in-code optimizer.

**Problem.** Given directory-ordered entries $e_1\prec\dots\prec e_N$ (order fixed by §7.1), a query workload distribution $Q$ over $q=(z_q, B_q, [w^-_q, w^+_q])$ (zoom, geographic bbox, time window), a per-HTTP-request cost $\alpha$ (RTT × bandwidth + per-GET fee), a per-byte cost $\beta$, and the leaf encoder $L(i,j]$ = at-rest zstd bytes of the v6 codec over segment $(i,j]$ (slicing resets delta state and splits RLE runs, so $\sum_j L_j \ge L_{\text{whole}}$, measured +6–19%): choose the page count $P$ and breakpoints $0=n_0<n_1<\dots<n_P=N$. Each leaf $j$ gets the conservative descriptor $d_j$ = (zoom range, outward-rounded geo bbox of its tiles, $[\min(\mathrm{cover\_t\_min}\,\|\,t_s),\max(t_e)]$), and a query fetches $F(q)=\{j: \mathrm{overlaps}(d_j,q)\}$ — a guaranteed superset of the leaves holding matches. Minimize

$$\mathbb{E}_{q\sim Q}\Big[\;\alpha\cdot\big|\mathrm{coalesce}(F(q))\big| \;+\; \beta\sum_{j\in F(q)} L_j\;\Big]$$

subject to: (i) at-rest inflation budget $\sum_j L_j + \mathrm{zstd}(12+52P) \le (1+\delta)\,L_{\text{whole}}$, paid once by the immutable CDN object; (ii) the root stays a cheap prefix fetch, i.e. $12+52P$ small; (iii) descriptors computed exactly as min/max over the segment (soundness); (iv) warm lookups over already-resident leaves cost 0. $\mathrm{coalesce}(\cdot)$ merges leaves adjacent in object byte space whose gap falls below the reader's coalescing threshold (see §6 — HTTP range coalescing). Decision variables: integer $P\ge 1$ and breakpoints $n_1<\dots<n_{P-1}$ (equivalently per-leaf sizes $K_j=n_j-n_{j-1}\in[1,2^{32})$); the binary paged-vs-single decision; the reader-side whole-load threshold $\theta$. Cross-page monotonicity is validated (directory_page.rs:613-742); descriptor rounding is outward (directory_page.rs:446-449); the root descriptor is fixed at 52 B/leaf.

**Incumbent.** Uniform fixed-size chunking: `sorted.chunks(page_entries)` at crates/stt-core/src/directory_page.rs:397 with the constant DEFAULT_PAGE_ENTRIES=4096 (directory_page.rs:61; CLI default crates/spatiotemporal-tiles/src/bin/stt-build.rs:143-145); paged iff $N\ge 8192$ (stt-build.rs:147-152, crates/stt-core/src/pack/mod.rs:760-763 and 1218); reader whole-load cutoff a constant 256 KiB (packages/core/src/archive.ts:132, applied at archive.ts:1887-1893). The chunking is workload-blind and boundary-blind: a leaf straddling a zoom boundary, or spanning sparse low-zoom world tiles, gets a near-global bbox descriptor that overlaps every viewport query — pure false-positive fetches a structure-aware cut would avoid.

**Class.** 1-D sequence segmentation / interval partitioning (offline DP in $O(N^2|Q|)$; convexity/SMAWK speedups); B-tree and packed R-tree node sizing (STR, Hilbert packing); COPC hierarchy page construction; block-size selection in LSM/columnar formats. Jointly with §7.1's ordering it becomes NP-hard (partition + arrangement).

**Knobs.** `--page-entries` (default 4096 entries/leaf), `--paged-directory-min-entries` (default 8192), `--single-directory` (opt out), `directoryPageThresholdBytes` reader option (default 262144), zstd level for directory frames (default 3, publish 19).

**Gap.** A workload- or structure-aware segmentation — cut at zoom transitions; variable $K_j$ targeting equal at-rest leaf bytes rather than equal entry counts; smaller leaves at low zoom where descriptors are geographically huge — is a straightforward DP against a simulated viewport/time workload, directly reducing measured cold leaf bytes and request count per session. The 4096 constant was validated by one simulation (2026-07) and never re-fit per dataset. Page hashes (32 hex chars/leaf in the manifest) also scale with $P$ and belong in the same objective. Not implemented.

### 7.3 Leaf-page pruning descriptors: false positives under a fixed sketch budget

**Setting.** One descriptor kind is implemented: DESCRIPTOR_GEO_BBOX = zoom range + lon/lat×1e7 bbox + temporal $[t_{\min},t_{\max}]$, 52 bytes fixed (crates/stt-core/src/directory_page.rs:79-129). Rust `PageDescriptor::overlaps` and the TS `ensurePagesForBounds` implement the identical viewport predicate (zoom membership ∧ bbox∩viewport with antimeridian-split longitude intervals ∧ time overlap); `ensurePagesForTiles` implements the point-lookup predicate with a $b_{\max}$ slack bound derived from metadata. A Hilbert-key-range descriptor alternative was REJECTED after an A/B simulation lost to the geo-bbox kind (the decision is recorded in a comment); the kind tag byte remains so new descriptor families can be added.

**Problem.** For each leaf $j$ with entry set $S_j$, choose a summary $d_j\in\mathcal{D}(S_j)$ from a family of fixed-width sketches (width $w=52$ bytes) inducing a constant-time test $\mathrm{overlaps}(d_j,q)$ that must be SOUND: $S_j\cap M(q)\neq\emptyset \Rightarrow \mathrm{overlaps}(d_j,q)$ for every query $q$ — both viewport queries $q=(z,B,[w^-,w^+])$ and point-lookups $q=(z,x,y,t,\text{bucket})$ — where $M(q)$ is the set of directory entries matching $q$. Minimize the expected false-positive cost

$$\mathbb{E}_{q\sim Q}\;\sum_{j}\big(\alpha' + \beta L_j\big)\cdot\mathbb{1}\big[\mathrm{overlaps}(d_j,q)\;\wedge\;S_j\cap M(q)=\emptyset\big]$$

subject to $|d_j|\le w$, $O(1)$ evaluation, and exact conservative coverage: stored bounds must dominate every member (integer bbox rounded outward so quantization never under-covers; $t_{\min,j}=\min_e(\mathrm{cover\_t\_min}_e\,\|\,t_{s,e})$, $t_{\max,j}=\max_e t_{e,e}$). Point-lookup subtlety: $t_{\min,j}$ is _not_ a bound on bucket starts (a tile's cover_t_min can exceed its bucket's timeStart), so the only sound lower-side prune is $t_{\min,j} > t + b_{\max}$, where $b_{\max}$ is the widest bucket any tile can carry (base or any temporal-LOD tier). Decision variables: per-leaf sketch $d_j$ from family $\mathcal{D}$ (currently the singleton geo-bbox kind 0); the family design itself (kind tag $\in$ u8); the width budget $w$. Zero false negatives are validated build-side by `verify_paged_structure`'s cover checks, and the predicate must be mirrored exactly across Rust and TS readers.

**Incumbent.** A hard-coded single sketch: min/max accumulation over the chunk at crates/stt-core/src/directory_page.rs:413-426 with outward e7 rounding (446-449); prune predicate directory_page.rs:111-129; TS viewport prune packages/core/src/archive.ts:2313-2348 (wrap-aware longitude via lonQueryIntervals, archive.ts:4048-4070); TS point-lookup prune with the maxBucketMs bound archive.ts:2355-2411 (slack computed at 2364-2368, soundness argument in comments 2383-2406). Greedy in the sense that the sketch is the trivially computable bounding box of whatever entries fixed-size chunking put in the leaf — selectivity is an accident of the partition, not a design target. The $b_{\max}$ slack is an implemented soundness fix that deliberately weakens pruning: a leaf can be faulted in for a point query up to one max-bucket-width away.

**Class.** Zone maps / BRIN min-max sketches; Bloom-filter budget allocation; synopsis/filter design as rate–distortion (sketch bits vs false-positive probability). The frozen geo-bbox-vs-Hilbert-range choice was a one-shot filter-family selection.

**Knobs.** None runtime-tunable; the descriptor kind tag (u8) and reserved fields exist for evolution. Indirect: cover_t_min presence (build-time covering pass) tightens $t_{\min,j}$.

**Gap.** A per-leaf min-bucket-start column (8 bytes, fitting in the reserved space or a v2 descriptor) would eliminate the $b_{\max}$ slack and its spurious leaf faults near dataset start; a second sketch dimension (variant bitmask, or a 2-level bbox for zoom-straddling leaves) would cut false positives on mixed leaves. Neither is implemented. Measurable: leaves fetched per query whose decoded entries contribute zero matches.

### 7.4 Cold-start critical path: manifest → root → leaves → pack ranges as round-trip minimization

**Setting.** The manifest is the single mutable root pointer: one GET yields metadata (folded in — no separate metadata fetch), the content-addressed directory pointer, the ordered pack table, embedded hash-validated schema templates, and required-capability declarations, so the reader reaches byte-addressable state in exactly one round trip (packages/core/src/archive.ts:1379-1477). The directory is then either whole-loaded (one GET, blake3-verified against its content-addressed key) or, when paged and > 256 KiB at rest, opened with a root-prefix range GET followed by on-demand leaf range GETs verified against manifest pageHashes. Manifest and directory GETs ride the same jittered retry + stall timeout as tile ranges (archive.ts:1535-1555) because nothing proceeds without them. Directory zstd level is a build knob (default 3, `--publish` bumps to 19) because the directory sits on the cold path with no CDN content-encoding rescue. Stages are strictly serial per query; the stt-serve protocol (docs/spec/stt-serve-protocol.md:355-372) is the degenerate alternative design point — no manifest or directory at all, one RTT per tile, `Cache-Control: no-store`, trading all indexing away for liveness.

**Problem.** Model the cold open as a serial dependency chain over a link with RTT $\rho$ and bandwidth $W$: manifest (size $m$) first, then directory, then pack ranges for the first query $q_0$, then decode. With reader-side whole/paged threshold $\theta$, time-to-first-tile is

$$T(\theta) = \Big(\rho+\tfrac{m}{W}\Big) + \begin{cases}\rho+\tfrac{D}{W} & D\le\theta \quad\text{(whole-load)}\\[4pt] \Big(\rho+\tfrac{8+52P'}{W}\Big) + \Big(\rho+\tfrac{\sum_{j\in F(q_0)}L_j}{W}\Big) & D>\theta \quad\text{(root + leaves)}\end{cases} + \Big(\rho+\tfrac{\sum_{i\in\text{tiles}(q_0)}\ell_i}{W}\Big) + T_{\text{dec}}$$

where $D$ is the at-rest directory size and $P'$ the compressed root size. Choose (a) the reader threshold $\theta\in[0,\infty)$, (b) the builder's paging trigger $N_{\min}$ and directory zstd level $\lambda_z\in[1,22]$ (affecting $D$ and $L_j$ at zero decode cost), and (c) what rides in the manifest versus separate objects, to minimize $\mathbb{E}[T]$ over the joint distribution of $(\rho, W, D, q_0)$ — subject to: exactly one mutable (never-cached) object per dataset (atomic redeploy); every independently fetched frame authenticated _before_ decompression (whole object: content-addressed key; paged frames: rootHash/pageHashes in the manifest, which grow $m$ by $32(P{+}1)$ hex chars); decompression-bomb ceilings at every stage (MAX_DECODED_DIRECTORY_BYTES = 512 MiB, per-entry 256 B ceilings); retries never on abort. The whole-load branch beats the paged branch iff $\tfrac{D - \sum_{j\in F(q_0)}L_j - (8+52P')}{W} \le \rho$, so the optimal threshold is $\theta^* \approx \rho W + \mathbb{E}[\text{root} + \text{leaf bytes}]$ — a stochastic threshold rule. Secondary objective: total cold bytes and billed GET count.

**Incumbent.** A fixed two-branch policy with hard-coded constants: whole-load iff at-rest $\le$ SMALL_DIR_THRESHOLD = 256 KiB (packages/core/src/archive.ts:124-132, branch at 1887-1920, whole path 1922-1953); builder pages iff $N\ge 8192$ (crates/spatiotemporal-tiles/src/bin/stt-build.rs:147-152; crates/stt-core/src/pack/mod.rs:1218). The 256 KiB constant encodes an assumed $\rho W$ product; neither $\rho$ nor $W$ is measured at open. Content-address verification at archive.ts:1969-1997; directory zstd level at stt-build.rs:154-168 and pack/mod.rs:1220-1236 (`encode_paged_directory_level` exists precisely so publish builds can spend level 19 on the cold path, directory_page.rs:375-380). No speculative pack prefetch from root-page information.

**Class.** Round-trip/pipeline minimization (PMTiles packs its root directory into the first 16 KiB for a one-request open — the same trade this threshold governs); optimal stopping / newsvendor threshold under stochastic $\rho, W$; prefetch-vs-demand as a two-stage stochastic program.

**Knobs.** `directoryPageThresholdBytes` (reader, default 262144), `--zstd-level` / `--publish` (build; 3 vs 19), `--single-directory`, `--paged-directory-min-entries` (8192), retry schedule and transfer timeout (shared with tile ranges).

**Gap.** Three unimplemented moves: (1) an adaptive threshold — fetch the root range unconditionally and decide whole-vs-paged from the manifest's directory length plus a measured first-sample $W$; (2) pipelining leaves into pack fetches — leaves decode incrementally, and the first resident leaf's entries could dispatch pack ranges while later leaves are still in flight (currently `ensurePages*` fully settles before any pack GET, archive.ts:3049-3053); (3) an initial-viewport hint in the manifest, letting the reader speculatively fetch $q_0$'s leaves in the same round as the root. Measurable: cold time-to-first-decoded-tile at fixed network profiles.

### 7.5 Temporal-LOD tier selection: request-granularity policy per zoom

**Setting.** Reader-side counterpart to §2's build-side pyramid design (which tiers to build lives there). Build time declares the pyramid in metadata, strictly validated — tiers ascending, strict multiples of the base bucket, coarser than base (crates/stt-core/src/metadata.rs:513-549); each directory entry carries its temporal_bucket_ms, and v6 made variant_id + bucket part of the lookup key, so tiers sharing a $(z,x,y,t)$ never collide in caches or lookups. At request time the reader picks the _coarsest applicable_ tier, implemented identically in Rust (`temporal_lod_for_zoom`), TS (`pickTemporalLodForZoom`), and server-side in stt-serve's per-request bucket resolution (largest applicable bucket_ms wins). `getTileIdsInBounds` defaults to base-bucket tiles; `getTileIdsInBoundsForTemporalLod` selects one declared tier and stamps bucketMs into the TileId so every downstream key stays tier-distinct.

**Problem.** An archive carries a base bucket $b_0$ and a validated LOD pyramid $\{(b_k, \bar z_k)\}_{k=1..K}$ with $b_k$ strictly ascending multiples of $b_0$ ($b_k > b_{k-1}$, $b_k \equiv 0 \pmod{b_0}$). A client at spatial zoom $z$, displaying a time window $[w^-, w^+]$ of width $|W|$ over $V(z)$ visible cells, must choose a tier $b \in \{b_0\}\cup\{b_k : z \le \bar z_k\}$ (the addressable set at $z$) for its tile requests. Fetch cost is approximately

$$\mathrm{cost}(b) \approx V(z)\cdot\Big\lceil \tfrac{|W|}{b} \Big\rceil \cdot \big(\alpha' + \beta\,\bar\ell(z,b)\big)$$

where $\bar\ell(z,b)$ is the mean compressed tile size at that (zoom, bucket) — coarser buckets aggregate more feature-time into fewer, larger tiles. Choose $b$ to minimize $\mathrm{cost}(b)$ plus the re-fetch churn rate $V(z)\cdot(\alpha'+\beta\bar\ell)/b \cdot \dot{w}$ as the playhead advances at scrub speed $\dot w$, subject to: (i) losslessness — every tier carries the full feature set re-bucketed, so any choice is correct; (ii) addressability — only tiers with $z\le\bar z_k$ exist at $z$; (iii) tier-qualified tile ids end-to-end (the directory key includes bucket, packages/core/src/archive.ts:2419-2442). At build time the pyramid $\{(b_k,\bar z_k)\}$ itself is a decision variable, chosen by the operator via `--temporal-lod` (advisor coverage in §12).

**Incumbent.** A hard threshold policy, "coarsest applicable by zoom": crates/stt-core/src/metadata.rs:387-393 (max_by_key over bucket_ms among levels with zoom ≤ max_zoom_level); TS twin packages/core/src/archive.ts:3758-3771; server twin docs/spec/stt-serve-protocol.md:127-146. Constant/greedy in two ways: the $\bar z_k$ cutoffs are baked at build with no knowledge of client window widths, and the reader never compares $\mathrm{cost}(b)$ across the addressable set — a window narrower than $b_k$ still fetches the coarse tile (over-fetch), and a very wide window at a zoom just above a cutoff streams base tiles (under-aggregation).

**Class.** Multi-resolution / mipmap level selection generalized to the time axis; paging-granularity selection. The cost comparison is a one-dimensional discrete minimization, trivially solvable online given $\bar\ell(z,b)$ — which the directory already knows exactly via entry lengths.

**Knobs.** `--temporal-lod` build flag (list of bucket_ms:max_zoom pairs); base `--temporal-bucket` (default 1h, stt-build.rs:170-174); reader chooses via `pickTemporalLodForZoom` or an explicit bucketMs.

**Gap.** The reader could evaluate $\mathrm{cost}(b)$ exactly from resident directory entries (`getTileByteSize` at archive.ts:2452 already exposes lengths) and pick the argmin per (viewport, window) instead of the zoom threshold. Not implemented. Would measurably cut bytes fetched during wide-window scrubbing and during narrow-window playback at low zoom.

### 7.6 Two-tier compressed-byte LRU caching

**Setting.** Distinct from §9's decoded-tile eviction (playhead-relative tiers over GPU-ready tiles) and from §9's OPFS persistent tier — this problem governs _compressed_ blobs in memory. Each archive keeps an insertion-ordered Map as an exact LRU of compressed tile bytes, and every entry is also registered in one process-global insertion-ordered LRU keyed by (archive-id, tile-key), so a ten-source composite cannot multiply the byte budget tenfold — inserting past the shared budget evicts the globally oldest entry via its owner's evict callback. Lookup order per tile is memory → OPFS → network (OPFS admission, fingerprint invalidation, and corruption self-healing belong to §9 — the persistent tier); poisoned memory entries are evicted on decode failure and re-fetched once.

**Problem.** Online file caching: a request sequence $\sigma = r_1, r_2, \dots$ of tile keys arrives (driven by viewport moves and playback), each tile $i$ having compressed size $\ell_i$. Maintain a memory cache $C_t$ subject to $\sum_{i\in C_t}\ell_i \le K_B$ (device-aware: 512 MiB, 256 MiB low-memory — shared across ALL live archives in the process) and $|C_t^{(a)}|\le K_N$ per archive (500 / 100 tiles). Serving costs per tier satisfy $c_{\text{mem}} \approx$ decode only $<$ $c_{\text{opfs}} =$ disk read + IPC decode (zstd skipped) $<$ $c_{\text{net}} =$ range fetch + zstd + decode. Choose, online, admission and eviction at each access to minimize total cost

$$\min \sum_t c_{\text{tier}(r_t)} \quad \text{s.t. capacity constraints at every } t$$

with correctness side-constraints: a redeployed dataset must never serve stale bytes (cache keys covary with content — enforced in the persistent tier via the directory-hash fingerprint, see §9), corrupt entries must self-heal rather than poison future requests, and eviction must be $O(1)$ per operation. Decision variables: online eviction victim selection (currently recency order), admission policy (currently admit-all), and the per-device budgets $K_B, K_N$.

**Incumbent.** Exact LRU via JS Map insertion order, at two levels: per-archive (packages/core/src/archive.ts:3326-3404, evictIfNeeded loop 3386-3404) and process-shared byte LRU (archive.ts:422-464), with device-aware constants at archive.ts:395-414 (500/100 tiles, 512/256 MiB via `navigator.deviceMemory` sniffing). LRU is the greedy incumbent: it ignores tile size, refetch cost, and — critically for this system — playback structure. Looping playback is a cyclic scan, LRU's pathological worst case: it evicts exactly the tiles the playhead will need next loop.

**Class.** Weighted/file caching with variable sizes and costs: offline OPT is NP-hard, with LP-rounding $O(1)$-approximations (Bar-Noy et al. local ratio); LRU is $k/(k-h+1)$-competitive; cyclic-scan workloads favor MRU/ARC/segmented policies; admission control (TinyLFU) handles one-touch scans.

**Knobs.** `maxCacheTiles`, `maxCacheBytes` (per archive); device heuristics otherwise. OPFS knobs (`opfsCache` et al.) belong to §9.

**Gap.** A playhead-aware eviction score (distance-to-playhead in the loop metric, mirroring the tileset's decoded-tile eviction tiers in §9) or even ARC would beat LRU on looping demos; size-aware eviction (GreedyDual-Size: evict the max size/refetch-cost ratio) would raise byte hit rate at fixed budgets. Neither is implemented. Measurable: byte-weighted hit rate and refetched bytes per playback loop; the offline benchmark is generalized Belady/MIN on the realized trace.

## 8. Client tile selection & spatiotemporal coverage

This subsystem is the demand side of the pipeline: each camera or clock change it decides _which_ directory entries the client must fetch (the spatiotemporal request set), _at what zoom_ to ask for them, _what_ resident subset to hand the renderer while primaries stream, _when_ to give up on a failing tile, and _how often_ to recompute any of the above. The resources traded are fetched/decoded bytes against completeness (an under-covered viewport is a blank region — a hard constraint throughout), resolution against enumeration cost, and recompute cost against staleness. Three neighboring problems that originate here are formalized in §9 and only cross-referenced below: the AIMD prefetch-horizon controller (see §9 — prefetch horizon under a known playhead trajectory), playhead-relative tiered eviction and the pinned overview tier (see §9 — Belady-framed cache eviction), and multi-class request dispatch with batch-granular cancellation (see §9 — EDF scheduling with cancellation).

### 8.1 Spatiotemporal viewport coverage: conservative frustum-grid cover × time-window overlap

**Setting.** Every camera frame the layer derives a lon/lat AABB from the (possibly pitched/rotated) view frustum and hands it to `SpatioTemporalTileset.update()`. `normalizeViewportBounds` (packages/core/src/geo/viewport-bounds.ts:102) repairs degenerate boxes: reject non-finite, swap inverted latitudes, treat `minLon>maxLon` as an antimeridian crossing only when the implied span is < 350° (viewport-bounds.ts:60), clamp latitude to the Web-Mercator edge. `selectAndLoadTiles` (packages/core/src/spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:1782) then queries the directory — one query per selected zoom, in parallel (:1905) — for all tiles whose grid cell intersects the box AND whose time interval overlaps $[t_0-\tfrac{W}{2}, t_0+\tfrac{W}{2}]$ (:1788). The archive-side scan (`tileScanWindow`, archive.ts:3950; `tileXSpanForLonRange`, archive.ts:3870; interval-overlap filter, archive.ts:3556-3564) walks wrap-aware columns and clamped rows and never truncates: an oversized grid warns (`MAX_QUERY_SCAN_CELLS`) and switches to the occupied-cell index. The result, `neededTileKeys`, is the authoritative working set; a monotonic generation stamp (:1897) discards stale async passes.

**Problem.** Given the visible ground region $V \subset \mathbb{S}^2$ (footprint of the 3D view frustum, possibly dilated by an altitude slab $[z_{lo}, z_{hi}]$); a display zoom $z^*$; the slippy grid $G_z = \{(z,x,y): 0 \le x,y < 2^z\}$ with cell footprints $\mathrm{cell}(z,x,y)$; the archive directory $D$ of tiles $e=(z,x,y,t)$, each with time interval $[\tau_{\min}(e), \tau_{\max}(e)]$ and byte size $s(e)$; playhead $t_0$ and load window $W$. Decision variables: the request set $R \subseteq D$, i.e. indicators $r_e \in \{0,1\}$ per directory entry. Minimize total fetch cost

$$\sum_{e \in R} s(e)$$

subject to full spatiotemporal coverage — every datum visible in the view at the display zoom is requested:

$$\forall e \in D_{z^*}: \; \big(\mathrm{cell}(e) \cap V \neq \emptyset\big) \wedge \big([\tau_{\min}(e), \tau_{\max}(e)] \cap [t_0 - \tfrac{W}{2},\, t_0 + \tfrac{W}{2}] \neq \emptyset\big) \Rightarrow e \in R,$$

plus wrap correctness (columns taken $\bmod\, 2^z$ across the antimeridian) and a soft enumeration-cost constraint: the number of grid cells examined to compute $R$ stays bounded (scan cost, distinct from fetch cost). Because each (cell, bucket) holds exactly one tile, the unique minimal cover is exactly the set of intersecting entries; the real optimization is the choice of the geometric predicate $\hat{V} \supseteq V$ used in place of $V$: minimize $\sum_{e:\, \mathrm{cell}(e) \cap \hat{V} \neq \emptyset} s(e)$ subject to $\hat{V} \supseteq V$ (never under-cover) and predicate-evaluation cost $\le$ budget. Hard constraints: latitude clamped to $\pm 85.05°$; non-finite boxes rejected (previous viewport kept); enumeration returns every matching cell.

**Incumbent.** A conservative AABB relaxation: $\hat{V}$ is the axis-aligned lon/lat box of the frustum footprint, with defence-in-depth repairs. Exact at pitch = 0 and bearing = 0; over-covers badly under pitch — measured 832 cells at pitch 85/z8 vs ~12 for a flat camera (docs/roadmap/tile-loading-3d-2026-07.md §4.4). Before 2026-07-26 a two-corner diagonal box UNDER-covered 20–44% — a correctness bug, since fixed. The exact frustum-grid walk (deck's getOSMTileIndices style: 47 tiles across z5–z8 vs 754 at z8) is PLANNED (Wave 3/A1), not built; the cell-budget zoom step-down (§8.2) is the shipped stopgap.

**Class.** Conservative rasterization/voxelization of a frustum onto a grid; spatial join of a convex region against a quadtree; view-frustum culling. Polynomial and output-sensitive ($O(\text{cells touched})$) — the tension is approximation tightness vs predicate cost, not NP-hardness. Online only in that the camera stream is adversarial; each frame's instance is deterministic and offline.

**Knobs.** `MAX_SEAM_SPAN_DEG` = 350° (crossing-vs-inversion threshold, viewport-bounds.ts:60); `zRange` altitude-slab dilation (layer prop, default null); `MAX_QUERY_SCAN_CELLS` warn threshold (archive.ts).

**Gap.** The PLANNED frustum-quadtree primitive (`getFrustumPlanes()` + CullingVolume walk with per-node distance zoom, Wave 3/A1) would cut pitched-view fetch bytes ~10–16× at high pitch (47 vs 754 tiles measured); the objective moved is tiles/bytes enumerated + fetched per pitched frame at equal coverage. Secondary: `metadata.bounds` is a centroid bbox that does not bound the data (archive.ts:3983-4014), so query boxes cannot be safely pre-intersected with it — the builder-side vertex-bbox fix (roadmap K11) is PLANNED.

### 8.2 Display-zoom selection under perspective: cell-budget fitting with hysteresis, overzoom clamp, and scrub degrade

**Setting.** The layer converts fractional camera zoom to one integer tile zoom per frame: `getZoomLevel` (packages/layers/src/layers/spatiotemporal-layer.ts:2332) floors and clamps to the archive's `[minZoom, maxZoom]` (:2340-2348) — overzoom past the max tiled zoom keeps fetching $z_{\max}$ tiles and the renderer scales them. Then `fitZoomToCellBudget` (packages/core/src/tile-budget.ts:145) steps the zoom down one rung at a time while the wrap-aware viewport cell count exceeds a budget, with a hysteresis release band: a clamped zoom is released back up only when the count at the higher rung falls below $(1-h)C$, because each flip trips `zoom !== lastSpatialZoom` and flushes the whole prefetch runway. Descent is judged on the plain cap (banding the descent measurably ratcheted a stationary camera an extra zoom down). `viewportCellCount` (tile-budget.ts:122) deliberately reuses the scan's own arithmetic, so prediction = actual enumeration. Separately, while the user scrubs, `effectiveSelectionZoom` (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:1472) drops the selection zoom by up to 2, clamped inside the parent-fallback band so the coarse tiles are usually already resident — preview-only; readiness and prefetch keep measuring the fine tier.

**Problem.** Given per frame $t$: camera zoom $\zeta_t \in \mathbb{R}$; viewport box $B_t$; archive range $[z_{\min}, z_{\max}]$; cell-count function $N(B,z)$ = tiles enumerated at zoom $z$ (wrap-aware, growing $\approx 4^z$); budget $C$; hysteresis $h \in [0, 0.9]$; previous decision $z_{t-1}$; a resolution utility $u(z)$ increasing in $z$; and a per-flip switching cost $\kappa$ (prefetch-runway flush + full re-selection + refetch churn). Decision variable: integer display zoom $z_t \in \{z_{\min}, \dots, \min(z_{\max}, \lfloor\zeta_t\rfloor)\}$ per frame. Minimize over the frame sequence

$$\sum_t \Big[ \alpha \underbrace{(\lfloor\zeta_t\rfloor - z_t)}_{\text{resolution loss}} + \beta\, \underbrace{N(B_t, z_t)}_{\text{enumeration/fetch cost}} + \kappa\, \mathbb{1}[z_t \neq z_{t-1}] \Big]$$

subject to: $N(B_t, z_t) \le C$ whenever any feasible $z \ge z_{\min}$ satisfies it — infeasibility at $z_{\min}$ resolves in favor of completeness (the box is selected in full: a blank map has $-\infty$ utility); $z_t \le \lfloor\zeta_t\rfloor$ (never sharper than the camera); $z_t \ge z_{\min}$; release from a clamp only through the band $N(B, z{+}1) \le C(1-h)$; a non-finite cell count returns the requested zoom (a garbage box must not trigger a coarse refetch). In practice the objective is lexicographic: (1) completeness, (2) enumeration cost $\le C$, (3) maximize resolution, (4) minimize flips. This is a metrical task system / convex-function-chasing instance on the integer zoom chain with movement cost $\kappa$ and per-state service cost = resolution loss; the adversary is the camera trajectory. The formal ideal (per-tile, NOT built): choose a zoom $z(c)$ per visible tile $c$ minimizing $\sum_c s(c, z(c))$ subject to a screen-space-error constraint $\mathrm{SSE}(c, z(c)) = \frac{\text{ground res at } z(c)}{\text{pixels per meter at } \mathrm{dist}(c)} \le \tau$ for every $c$ — the classic HLOD traversal, which the single-integer-zoom incumbent approximates with one global $z$.

**Incumbent.** Greedy threshold-with-hysteresis: linear descent judged on the plain cap (tile-budget.ts:201-210), release gated at $(1-h)C$ against the held zoom (:220-230), memoised counts; scrub drop is a constant. It is a documented stopgap for the AABB over-cover of §8.1: it spends geometric over-approximation as ONE coarser uniform integer zoom (quartering cells; same features at coarser simplification on no-thinning archives) and is explicitly never a truncation of the tile list. Gap vs optimum: one global integer zoom cannot express the 1.5–5.8 true zoom levels a pitched frame spans (RC4: pitch 55 → 1.49 levels, pitch 80 → 5.8), so the near field is over-resolved or the far field over-fetched; the hysteresis band is a hand-tuned deadband, not an optimal switching policy.

**Class.** Screen-space-error-driven HLOD / quadtree refinement (Cesium, 3D Tiles); deadband control; metrical task systems / online convex body chasing for the switching-cost sequence (competitive-ratio bounds apply); ski-rental flavor in each flip-vs-hold decision. The per-frame subproblem is trivial ($N$ monotone in $z$); the sequence problem with switching costs is the online part.

**Knobs.** `DEFAULT_VIEWPORT_CELL_BUDGET` = 256 (tile-budget.ts:56; ~7× a flat camera's ~35 cells, so inert unpitched); `DEFAULT_CELL_BUDGET_HYSTERESIS` = 0.25 (:62); `scrubLod.spatialZoomDrop` default 2, clamped to `PARENT_FALLBACK_LEVELS` = 4 (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:106, :99); archive minZoom/maxZoom clamp; `zoomOverride` and `useGlobalBounds` bypass the budget entirely.

**Gap.** Per-tile distance-based zoom (PLANNED — subsumed by Wave 3/A1) changes the problem from one global $z$ to a per-node quadtree cut maximizing $\sum u(z_{node})$ under the same budget, strictly dominating any uniform clamp (47 vs 754 cells at equal coverage) while holding SSE constant. Short of that, $(C, h)$ are fixed; an adaptive deadband sized from measured flush cost vs tile cost (an MTS work-function policy) could reduce total stall + fetch. The scrub drop $\delta$ is a constant 2 rather than chosen from measured decode latency vs preview quality.

### 8.3 Best-available fallback: placeholder fetch policy + resident-cover antichain selection

**Setting.** Two coupled decisions while primary tiles stream. Fetch side: under `refinementStrategy: 'best-available'`, `getZoomLevelsToLoad` (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:1699) also requests up to 4 coarser parent levels as placeholders; `isOversizedParent` (:1743) skips any parent whose directory size exceeds 2 MiB (a 14 MB z10 Manhattan tile is a near-useless placeholder under a z14 view) — the primary zoom is never skipped. Cover side: `getVisibleTiles` (:4065) selects the resident subset delivered to the renderer: all loaded primaries; a loaded parent only while at least one of its primary child cells inside the viewport-clamped range (plus a one-tile slack ring, :4181) is uncovered — the clamp fixed the bug where a parent larger than the viewport always had uncovered _off-screen_ children and so was never dropped, staying in the delivered set alongside its own children in permanent violation of the single-cover contract (a parent is never "covered" when <16 children are in view); a third pass stands in already-resident finer descendants (depth ≤ 2) for needed-but-unloaded cells, else a coarser ancestor (≤ 2 up) but only when the ancestor's entire $4^{up}$-cell block is blank, descendants resolved first as a separate sub-pass so iteration order cannot yield two covers. A degenerate viewport intersection disables the clamp (fail-open: a fallback tile must never be dropped because visibility could not be computed). `lodMode: 'additive'` bypasses all de-dup (points live at exactly one home zoom; the union is the cloud).

**Problem.** Given: primary zoom $z^*$; needed primary cells $P = \{(x,y,t)\}$ (viewport × window at $z^*$); the resident loaded set $L$ across zooms $[z^*{-}4, z^*{+}2]$; the covering relation — tile $u$ at zoom $z<z^*$ covers child cells $\mathrm{ch}(u) = \{(x,y): x \in [x_u 2^{d}, (x_u{+}1)2^{d}), \dots\}$, $d = z^*-z$; per-cell detail value $v(c, z)$ increasing in $z$; visibility indicator $\mathrm{vis}(c) \in \{0,1\}$. Decision variables: delivered cover set $E \subseteq L$ and fallback fetch set $F \subseteq D_{z < z^*}$. Cover objective — maximize covered detail:

$$\max_E \sum_{c \in P,\, \mathrm{vis}(c)=1} \max_{u \in E,\, c \in \mathrm{ch}(u)} v(c, z_u) \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \forall c: \big|\{u \in E : c \in \mathrm{ch}(u)\}\big| \le 1,$$

where the $\le 1$ constraint makes $E$ an antichain of the quadtree restricted to visible cells — the delivery contract is that each visible cell reaches the renderer with at most one cover — and $E$ may only reuse $L$ (zero fetch cost). Fetch objective — placeholder cost/benefit (stochastic): with $T_c$ the random arrival time of primary tile $c$ and $A(u) = |\{c \in \mathrm{ch}(u): \mathrm{vis}(c)\}|$,

$$\max_F \sum_{u \in F} \Big[ \lambda \cdot A(u) \cdot \mathbb{E}[\text{time } u \text{ arrives before its children}] - s(u) - \text{decode}(u) \Big],$$

i.e. fetch a coarse placeholder iff its blank-screen-time-averted value exceeds its byte + decode cost. Constraints: no blank that residency could fill (any cell with a resident cover must be covered); an ancestor covers only when its whole visible block is blank; fail-open on degenerate viewport; base-tier only for stand-ins — a temporal-LOD tile must never satisfy a base cell (the `@bucketMs` suffix in tileKey enforces this, packages/core/src/tile-key.ts:62); bounded search — cover checks $O(4^d)$ capped at $d \le 4$ for parents, stand-in walks capped at depth 2 both directions; the primary zoom is never byte-skipped. (Request dispatch and cancellation for $F$ are §9's scheduling problem.)

**Incumbent.** Greedy three-pass selection in `getVisibleTiles` (:4065): pass 1 emits loaded primaries and builds `primaryCover`/`primaryPending` (:4112-4123); pass 2 keeps a parent iff some viewport-clamped + slack-ringed child cell is uncovered (:4144-4238); pass 3 emits resident descendants then ancestors in two ordered sub-passes (:4266-4302; helpers :4318, :4388). Fetch side: a fixed 4-level ladder (:1699) plus a constant byte threshold (:1743) — a hardcoded cutoff, not a value model: it never weighs how long a placeholder will actually be useful (arrival-time distribution) or how much visible area it covers. The greedy is optimal for the cover problem _given_ its bounded lookback (quadtree cover is polynomial by bottom-up DP); the depth-2 caps mean a resident tile 3+ levels away is never reused even when it is the only cover available.

**Class.** Quadtree exact cover / maximum-weight antichain (polynomial via tree DP); HLOD refinement with interior-node placeholders; the fetch side is a stochastic knapsack / ski-rental (placeholder bought against unknown child arrival time). The per-frame cover is offline; fetch value depends on future arrivals.

**Knobs.** `PARENT_FALLBACK_LEVELS` = 4 (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:99); `maxParentTileBytes` default 2 MiB (:85); `CHILD_LOOKAHEAD_LEVELS` = 2 (:117); `ANCESTOR_LOOKBACK_LEVELS` = 2 (:226); one-tile slack ring (:4181); `refinementStrategy` 'best-available' | 'no-overlap' (:822); `lodMode` 'parent-fallback' | 'additive' (:823).

**Gap.** The 2 MiB skip is a fixed threshold where the principled rule is $s(u) < \lambda \cdot A(u) \cdot \mathbb{E}[\text{cover time}]$ — with per-tile directory sizes and the throughput estimator both already wired, an expected-value rule is computable and would move placeholder bytes fetched vs blank-frame time. The 2/2 lookback/lookahead caps discard usable resident covers after multi-notch zooms; a full bottom-up DP over the resident set is $O(|L|)$ and would raise covered area at zero fetch cost.

### 8.4 Failed-tile retry scheduling and readiness write-off: probing an intermittently available resource

**Setting.** A needed tile whose fetch settles without data would otherwise become a permanent hole — the only enqueue site is selection's candidate loop, short-circuited by the exact-params fast path — and while it counts as "missing" it pins the buffered runway at zero, so the playback governor never advances the clock. The shipped design splits two verdicts a single counter used to conflate. Fetch eligibility: an expiring quarantine gate `retryAfter = now + min(500ms × 2^(n−1), 60 s)` where $n$ counts ALL settles including aborts (an abort loop is as expensive as a failure loop); every enqueue/dispatch site consults `isQuarantined` (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:2808), so camera activity can never reset a failing tile's request rate; one shared timer sweeps due retries (:2906). Readiness: `isFailed` latches (sticky) after 3 non-abort failures or 8 settles of any kind — bounding the abort exemption so a transport that aborts every request cannot pin the runway forever; a written-off tile stops counting as missing in `getBufferedRunway`/`getBufferedRanges`/`estimateCost` but keeps being retried at the 60 s ceiling (readiness is not a fetch verdict; un-latching per retry would re-pin the runway once per backoff window — periodic stutter instead of one clean write-off).

**Problem.** Given a tile whose availability is an unknown two-state process: with prior probability $q$ it is transiently unavailable and recovers at unknown time $T \sim F$ (network blip, cold edge, propagating republish); with probability $1{-}q$ it is permanently absent (genuine 404). Each probe costs $c_p$ (a coalesced range request — probes of co-viewport tiles share one batch, so cost is per-sweep, not per-tile); while unhealed the user sees a hole at cost rate $c_h$; while the tile counts as missing for readiness, playback is stalled at cost rate $c_g$. Decision variables: probe schedule $0 < \tau_1 < \tau_2 < \dots$ (wall-clock retry times); write-off time $\omega$ (when readiness stops counting the tile); whether write-off is reversible per retry. Minimize expected cost

$$\mathbb{E}\Big[ c_h \cdot (\text{heal time} - T)^+ \;+\; c_p \cdot |\{\tau_i\}| \;+\; c_g \cdot \min(\omega, \text{heal time}) \;+\; c_{\text{stutter}} \cdot |\{\text{write-off reversals}\}| \Big]$$

where heal time $= \min\{\tau_i : \tau_i \ge T\}$ for a transient tile and $\infty$ for an absent one, whose long-run probe rate must be bounded: $\limsup |\{\tau_i \le t\}|/t \le r_{\max}$. Subject to: readiness monotonicity — once written off, a tile re-enters readiness only by LOADING (hard constraint; reversal re-pins the runway periodically); the probe schedule must be independent of camera activity (selection frequency must not set the request rate); bounded timer count (one shared timer).

**Incumbent.** `noteSettledWithoutTile` (:2833) charges attempts/settles and arms `retryAfter`; `isQuarantined` (:2808) is THE dispatch gate; `scheduleRetrySweep` (:2906) keeps one timer for the earliest deadline; `retryFailedTiles` (:2940) re-enqueues due, still-needed, still-unqueued holes at priority; the sticky write-off changes readiness in `isCoverageReady` (:3106). The ladder is a fixed geometric schedule with hand-derived thresholds: no belief update from response class (404, timeout, and 5xx advance the same ladder), no cross-tile pooling (100 holes from one outage run independent ladders, though probes coalesce), and $q$, $F$ are never estimated. REJECTED first cut: a 3-attempt _hard fetch_ gate — it blanked regions for the whole session after a 1.5 s blip, and was replaced by the quarantine/write-off split (F5 as shipped).

**Class.** Optimal probing/polling of an intermittently available channel; Bayesian search with per-look cost (Gittins-flavored when multiple holes compete for probe bandwidth); exponential backoff competitiveness (constant-competitive against unknown recovery time, cf. ski-rental analysis); transmission scheduling with deadline-free jobs. Online, stochastic, unknown distribution.

**Knobs.** `FAILED_TILE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` = 3 (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:157); `FAILED_TILE_RETRY_COOLDOWN_MS` = 500 base, ×2 per settle (:184); `FAILED_TILE_RETRY_MAX_BACKOFF_MS` = 60 s (:193); `FAILED_TILE_READINESS_WRITEOFF_SETTLES` = 8 (:215) — sized to fire only after ~64 s of trying, past any blip.

**Gap.** Response-class-aware ladders (a definitive 404 jumps straight to the 60 s ceiling; a 503 + Retry-After honors the header) would cut both probe waste and heal latency — measurable as heal-time-after-recovery and probes/minute on absent tiles. Outage pooling (one ladder per origin outage rather than per tile) would collapse correlated backoff state.

### 8.5 Recomputation scheduling: staleness vs cost of selection passes, coverage rebuilds, and flushes

**Setting.** Camera and clock updates arrive at up to 60 Hz, but the expensive reactions are asynchronous directory queries (a real network round-trip on paged archives) and destructive flushes (aborting in-flight prefetch). A layered tolerance scheme rations them: (1) the exact-params fast path — a `selectKey` string of (bounds, zoom, selectionZoom, scrubBucket, timeRange) — skips the whole awaited selection when nothing changed (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:1810-1815; cleared on error :1927 and on flush :3406 so failures retry); (2) the layer pushes tick updates only when sim time moved ≥ W/20 with a 100 ms wall floor (spatiotemporal-layer.ts:1291-1296); (3) the destructive prefetch flush fires only when center or span moved > 1/8 of viewport extent or zoom changed (:1828-1862, seam-aware span arithmetic :475, :486) — sub-tile drift (AV ego-follow, easing pans) used to flush ~60×/s so the runway could never build; (4) the coverage index, whose rebuild is the single heaviest query in the system (full-time-range directory slice for the viewport), is keyed to bounds quantized to a 1/8-of-extent grid (`quantizedSpatialKey` :1768, `maybeRebuildCoverageIndex` :3715); (5) prefetch planning is debounced 250 ms and throttled on consumed runway; (6) buffer-change emissions are throttled to ≤ 10 Hz. Tile selection itself always tracks the exact viewport — only flush and coverage are quantized.

**Problem.** Given an input stream $x_1, x_2, \dots$ of viewport/clock states arriving at rate $\phi \le 60$ Hz; a family of derived computations $f_k$ with per-evaluation costs $c_k$ (selection slice $c_{\text{sel}}$; coverage rebuild $c_{\text{cov}} \gg c_{\text{sel}}$; flush $c_{\text{fl}}$ = aborted in-flight bytes + runway rebuild) and staleness penalties $p_k(x_t, \hat{x}_k)$ where $\hat{x}_k$ is the input $f_k$ was last evaluated at (selection: viewport area not covered by the stale needed set; coverage: error in the reported buffered runway; flush: bytes warmed for a viewport the camera left). Decision variables: per computation $k$, a re-evaluation policy $\pi_k: x_t \mapsto \{\text{reuse}, \text{recompute}\}$ (in the incumbent, a quantization/tolerance predicate). Minimize the long-run rate

$$\lim_T \frac{1}{T} \sum_{t \le T} \Big[ \sum_k c_k \cdot \mathbb{1}[\pi_k \text{ recomputes at } t] + \sum_k p_k(x_t, \hat{x}_k) \Big]$$

subject to: zero staleness for correctness-critical outputs — the needed-tile set must be recomputed whenever the true (bounds, zoom, window) tuple changes ($p_{\text{sel}}$ tolerance = 0; only literally identical inputs may reuse); bounded staleness for approximate outputs — coverage-index spatial error $\le \epsilon \cdot \text{viewport extent}$ with $\epsilon = 1/8$; hysteresis on destructive actions — a flush must not fire on drift the runway survives ($\|\Delta\text{center}\|, \|\Delta\text{span}\| \le \epsilon \cdot \text{extent}$ and same zoom $\Rightarrow$ no flush); ordering safety — concurrent async recomputes resolved by generation stamps (selection :1897, coverage :3735, prefetch beginPass/isCurrentPass): a stale pass must never clobber a newer result.

**Incumbent.** Fixed-tolerance predicates throughout, with all constants chosen from incident postmortems (60×/s flush storms, 10 Hz coverage rebuilds) rather than derived from measured $c_k$ vs $p_k$. The seek threshold max(W, |speed| × 1 s) separates playback steps from genuine jumps; a seam-crossing box whose negative span zeroed the flush tolerance (flushing every pass) was a fixed bug. The load-bearing structural insight is the two-tier design: correctness-critical outputs exact, expensive/destructive side-effects quantized.

**Class.** Online function tracking / lazy re-evaluation with staleness budgets; filtering and debouncing in control systems; the flush hysteresis is again a deadband controller; self-adjusting computation (recompute-on-change with cutoffs). Each per-decision subproblem is trivial; the sequence against an adversarial camera is online with competitive-ratio flavor.

**Knobs.** `SPATIAL_FLUSH_TOLERANCE` = 1/8 (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:72); `SEEK_DETECTION_REAL_MS` = 1000 (:54); `debounceTime` default 0 (:816); layer tick threshold W/20 + 100 ms wall floor (spatiotemporal-layer.ts:1291-1296, :814-818); `PREFETCH_DEBOUNCE_MS` = 250 (prefetch-policy.ts:183); `BUFFER_CHANGE_THROTTLE_MS` = 100 (:233).

**Gap.** Adaptive tolerances — sizing the coverage quantization from measured rebuild cost (paged vs resident directory) and the flush tolerance from current runway value (bytes in flight × time-to-consume) — would trade measurably in directory queries/min and aborted bytes/min during camera motion. The coverage-staleness bound (runway reported for a box up to 1/8 off the true viewport) is a KNOWN unmeasured risk — open item, docs/roadmap/tile-loading-3d-2026-07.md §8.

## 9. Prefetch, request scheduling & the cache hierarchy

This subsystem decides which bytes to move over the network, in what order, and which results to retain across a cache hierarchy of decoded tiles in memory and decompressed payloads on disk (the compressed-byte two-tier LRU is formalized in §7 — directory & remote access). Its structural advantage over generic streaming is that the playhead trajectory is published by the time controller, so future demand is piecewise-deterministic and the offline optimum is computable between interruptions; its resources are link bandwidth, main-thread CPU, memory bytes, and origin disk quota, all traded against stall-free playback. The viewport cell-budget zoom clamp that bounds what selection may enumerate lives in §8 (client tile selection); decode work placement is §10; the stall-free gating control problem — including the fluid-buffer runway feasibility condition and the dual-EWMA throughput estimator that this layer's coverage index feeds — is formalized in §11 (playback governor & QoS).

### 9.1 Speculative prefetch under a known playhead trajectory

**Setting.** The time controller publishes a signed speed $s$ (sim-ms per real-ms), so future data needs are known up to seeks and direction flips. `PrefetchPolicy.plan()` computes an order-up-to horizon each pass: $\max$(configured window lookahead, $|s| \cdot 8000$ ms), capped by a 64-bucket ceiling with speed-scaled floor $\max(64\beta, |s|\cdot 5000)$, capped again by the governor's run-ahead limit, then multiplied by an AIMD pressure scale in $[0,1]$ — decay $\times 0.7$ on each eviction that reached the protected runway, $+0.1$ recovery per wall-second after 5 s quiet, disabled below $0.25$ (packages/core/src/prefetch-policy.ts:414-419, 507-575). A pass is skipped while planned runway exceeds $(1-0.5)\times$ horizon, debounced to one pass per 250 ms (prefetch-policy.ts:583-587). The consumer `prefetchFutureTiles` issues one directory query over the span, sorts candidates nearest-upcoming-bucket-first via `plan.aheadDistance()`, enqueues at most $\max(64, 0.5 \cdot \text{maxCacheSize})$ tiles, and re-anchors the runway frontier if the budget truncated the span (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:2195-2224, 2282-2284; prefetch-policy.ts:618-645). Direction commits with hysteresis — 3 consecutive reversed deltas, or immediately on a signed-speed reversal (prefetch-policy.ts:314-366); seeks (jump $> \max(W, |s|\cdot 1000)$), spatial moves ($> 1/8$ viewport), and flips flush the runway (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:1644, 1857, 1351/1383). All IMPLEMENTED; only the constants are hand-fixed.

**Problem.** Given wall time $\tau$; playhead trajectory $\theta(\tau)=\theta_0+s\,\tau$ with known signed speed $s$; temporal bucket width $\beta$ (ms); resident time window $W$; a tile universe where tile $i$ has bucket start $t_i$, cell $c_i$, and byte size $b_i$, all known exactly from the directory; viewport cell set $V$; stochastic link rate $\mu(\tau)$ (bytes/ms); cache capacities $N_{\max}$ tiles and $B_{\max}$ bytes; and an interruption process (seeks, viewport moves, direction flips) at rate $\lambda_{inv}$ that invalidates planned work. The needed set at playhead $\theta$ is

$$N(\theta)=\{i:\ [t_i,t_i+\beta]\cap[\theta-\tfrac{W}{2},\theta+\tfrac{W}{2}]\neq\emptyset,\ c_i\in V\}.$$

Decide, at each planning instant $\tau_k$: a horizon $h_k\ge 0$ (sim-ms), an ordered enqueue set $E_k\subseteq\{i: 0\le d\cdot(t_i-\theta(\tau_k))\le h_k\}$ with committed direction $d_k\in\{+1,-1\}$ (a filtered estimate of $\operatorname{sign}(s)$), a pressure multiplier $p_k\in[0,1]$ acting on $h_k$, and the replanning instants $\tau_k$ themselves. Minimize

$$J=\mathbb{E}\Big[\lambda_s\!\int\!\mathbf{1}[\text{stall at }\tau]\,d\tau\;+\;\lambda_w\!\sum_i b_i\,\mathbf{1}[i\text{ fetched but evicted or invalidated before use}]\;+\;\lambda_c\sum_k C_{plan}(h_k)\Big],$$

where a stall occurs when $N(\theta(\tau))$ is not fully resident and $C_{plan}(h)=O((h/\beta)\cdot Z\log(\cdot))$ is the main-thread directory walk-and-sort cost over $Z$ zoom levels. Subject to: resident set $\le N_{\max}$ tiles and $\le B_{\max}$ bytes at all times; aggregate fetch rate $\le\mu(\tau)$; prefetched tiles must remain resident until $\theta$ reaches them (coupling with §9.4 eviction); the per-pass working set $\sum_{i\in E_k} b_i$ must fit the cache share the runway may occupy, else it self-evicts (thrash); and the horizon must cover the governor's speed-scaled gates (resume gate $\approx 2\times 2000\ \text{wall-ms}\times|s|$, see §11) or playback deadlocks — hence the hard floor $h\ge\max(\beta, W, |s|\cdot 5000)$ under every cap, which the pressure scale never shrinks. Because the access sequence is known absent interruptions, the offline optimum is Belady-informed just-in-time prefetching: fetch exactly $N(\theta(\tau'))$ for increasing $\tau'$, as late as feasibility allows, never exceeding the residency share; interruptions make it a stochastic shortest path / MDP with known drift and Poisson restarts.

**Incumbent.** An (s,S)/base-stock inventory policy with AIMD capacity adaptation, all constants hand-tuned: reorder point = the skip-while-runway-remains rule (prefetch-policy.ts:583-587), order-up-to level = the capped horizon (:507-575), greedy nearest-deadline-first enqueue truncated at $\max(64, 0.5\cdot\text{maxCacheSize})$ tiles (tileset:2195-2224; prefetch-policy.ts:618-623). The AIMD pressure ladder (:414-419, 545-573) is a multiplicative-decrease/additive-increase feedback loop standing in for the Lagrange multiplier of the residency constraint — it discovers "the runway does not fit" only after tier-C/D evictions occur, rather than computing the largest feasible horizon from known byte sums. There is no stochastic model of seek likelihood, and the horizon choice ignores per-bucket byte costs (the enqueue budget counts tiles; only slices count bytes).

**Class.** Base-stock / (s,S) inventory control under deterministic demand with stochastic lead time; anticipatory (lookahead) scheduling; MDP with known drift plus Poisson restarts; AIMD as dual ascent on a capacity constraint (TCP congestion control); offline near-Belady prefetching — polynomial given the trajectory, with the online difficulty residing entirely in the interruption process.

**Knobs.** `PREFETCH_LOOKAHEAD_REAL_MS`=8000, `MAX_PREFETCH_BUCKETS`=64, `PREFETCH_CAP_FLOOR_REAL_MS`=5000, `PREFETCH_RELOAD_FRACTION`=0.5, `PREFETCH_DEBOUNCE_MS`=250; pressure ladder: decay 0.7, recovery 0.1/s, quiet 5000 ms, disable threshold 0.25; `PREFETCH_CACHE_FRACTION`=0.5, min budget 64 tiles; direction flip threshold 3 frames (prefetch-policy.ts:35-183).

**Gap.** The directory knows every tile's byte size, so the largest thrash-free horizon is directly computable — $h^\*=\max\{h: \sum_{i\in N[\theta,\theta+dh]} b_i \le 0.5\,B_{\max}\}$ — and a one-shot feasibility solve would replace the reactive AIMD ladder, eliminating the fetch-evict-refetch transient (measurable: `cacheStats.runwayEvictions` → 0, fewer refetched bytes). The 8000/5000/64 constants could be derived per archive from $\mu$, bucket byte densities, and the governor's gate sizes; today a mis-sized archive recovers only via pressure oscillation. The enqueue budget counts tiles, not bytes — a 64-tile floor of 17 MB satellite tiles vastly overshoots the byte intent. Seek-risk-aware discounting (scrub-heavy sessions should hold shorter runways) is unmodeled.

### 9.2 Request prioritization, supersession & slice sizing: EDF with cancellation and bounded commitment

**Setting.** Tile fetches are coalesced into byte-adjacent range groups and dispatched through three strict tiers: priority (current window), prefetch (lookahead), overview (pinned storyboard, only when priority is idle). The priority queue drains nearest-to-playhead-first with primary-zoom-before-parent precedence — sorted by the _undirected_ $|t-\theta|$ (tileset:2445-2467), unlike the prefetch path's directional `aheadDistance` (prefetch-policy.ts:605). Prefetch dispatches exactly one slice at a time, sized to ~1000 ms of measured download (clamped [1 MB, 16 MB], 4 MB cold-start), so a playhead catching an in-flight slice waits at most ~1 s and a flush wastes at most one slice (prefetch-policy.ts:648-666; tileset:2409-2430). Cross-source, each group carries priority value = tier base ($10^{15}$ for prefetch) + distance-to-playhead in sim-ms (behind-head offset $5\times 10^{14}$) + a spatial tie-break $0.4\cdot\min(\text{distSq},2)$; the shared scheduler picks the lowest value per free slot. Cancellation is batch-granular: one `AbortController` per batch (tileset:2587), a priority batch aborted only when _every_ member has left the needed set — during playback the trailing window edge always supersedes a few members, and aborting then would kill the still-needed rest (`cancelSupersededRequests`, tileset:3025). Prefetch is exempt from per-selection supersession (aborting it per pass collapsed the runway at fast playback) but is dropped by `flushPrefetch` on seeks/pans/flips, sparing batches containing currently-needed tiles on spatial pans, with generation stamps discarding stale planning passes (tileset:3354-3407, 2160-2176; prefetch-policy.ts:454-470). All IMPLEMENTED.

**Problem.** Given $m$ parallel request slots (default 24); jobs (coalesced range groups) $j$ with byte size $b_j$ and members' bucket starts; service rate $\mu$ bytes/ms so service time $\approx b_j/\mu$; playhead $\theta(\tau)=\theta_0+s\tau$, giving each job a deadline — the wall time its nearest member is consumed, $d_j=\tau_{now}+\mathrm{dist}_j/|s|$ with $\mathrm{dist}_j=\min_{i\in j}(d\cdot(t_i-\theta))^+$; per-job gate weight $w_j$ (a late priority-tier job = playhead stall; a late prefetch job = merely wasted speculation); an invalidation process (seek/pan/flip) at rate $\lambda_{inv}$ rendering unconsumed work worthless; and per-dispatch latency overhead $RTT$. Decide: (a) the dispatch order — which queued job runs when a slot frees, re-decided at every slot-free event; (b) the cancellation set $A(\tau)$ on each invalidation event, at batch granularity — aborting batch $B$ discards _all_ of $B$'s in-flight bytes (all-or-nothing preemption); (c) the commitment quantum $\sigma$ — bytes of speculative work in flight at once (the slice size); (d) per-pass batch composition. Minimize

$$\alpha\sum_j w_j\,(C_j-d_j)^+\;+\;\gamma\,\mathbb{E}[\text{in-flight bytes discarded per invalidation}]\;+\;\delta\,\frac{B_{total}}{\sigma}\,RTT,$$

with $C_j$ the completion time. For the slice-size subproblem, expected discarded work per invalidation $\approx\sigma/2$ and pipeline overhead $\propto B/\sigma$ give the EOQ-style optimum

$$\sigma^\*=\sqrt{\tfrac{2\,\mu\,RTT\,B}{\lambda_{inv}}}\ \text{(suitably clamped)},$$

while stall-avoidance alone wants nearest-deadline-first (EDF) dispatch. Subject to: at most $m$ jobs running; running jobs non-preemptible except by abort-with-total-work-loss; at most one speculative slice in flight (self-imposed, so every slice boundary is a fresh decision point — late commitment); strict tier precedence encoding lexicographic weights $w_{priority}\gg w_{prefetch}\gg w_{overview}$; prefetch share $\le 0.5m$ animating / $0.33m$ paused on the per-tile fallback path; a slice takes at least one tile even if oversized (progress guarantee, tileset:2422-2424); batch cap 1024 tiles; unknown tile sizes billed at 64 KiB.

**Incumbent.** Greedy EDF-by-static-snapshot: groups are ranked once by `groupSchedulerPriority` (archive.ts:2782-2803, 2926-2942) using distance-to-playhead frozen at enqueue — the `getPriority` closure re-evaluated at dispatch recomputes from the _same_ captured `minDist`/`fallbackSeq` (archive.ts:2953-2959), so the scheduler's dispatch-time re-prioritization (request-scheduler.ts:479-505) never tracks the moving playhead within a queue's lifetime; it re-ranks only across newly-enqueued work. Slice sizing is a fixed 1000-ms-of-throughput target (prefetch-policy.ts:648-666); queue drain at tileset:2318-2436; the 0.5/0.33 prefetch shares are constants rather than derived from measured priority pressure; batch abort is all-or-nothing where the coalescer could support per-range-group cancellation.

**Class.** EDF (optimal for single-machine feasibility, Liu & Layland); $P\,\|\,\sum w_jU_j$ and $1\,\|\,\sum w_jT_j$ are NP-hard — the tier system is a lexicographic heuristic; online scheduling with job cancellations / interval scheduling; EOQ lot-sizing for the commitment quantum; "late commitment" is the Cesium RequestScheduler principle, slicing the hls.js/Shaka segment-fetch model.

**Knobs.** `PREFETCH_SLICE_TARGET_REAL_MS`=1000; slice min/max/cold = 1 MiB/16 MiB/4 MiB; `SCHEDULER_PREFETCH_TIER_BASE`=1e15; behind-head offset 5e14; spatial tie-break weight 0.4; `MAX_COALESCE_BATCH`=1024 (tileset:34); `PREFETCH_UNKNOWN_TILE_BYTES`=64 KiB; `maxRequests`=24 (tileset:815); per-tile fallback prefetch shares 0.5/0.33 (tileset:2360).

**Gap.** (1) True dynamic EDF — thread the live playhead into `getPriority` so a queued group's priority decays as the head approaches; today a long-lived queue misorders as time passes, measurable as priority-tier tiles arriving after their bucket is current. (2) Replace the 1000 ms slice constant with the EOQ optimum using the _observable_ invalidation rate (seeks/pans per minute): fast-scrubbing sessions want smaller slices, static-camera playback larger; moves aborted-bytes-per-session and slice-boundary idle. (3) The one-slice rule leaves ~1 RTT idle per boundary that double-buffering (dispatch slice $k{+}1$ when $k$ is ~90% done) would recover without extending the hostage window. (4) Per-range-group abort granularity would cut aborted-byte waste on seeks that supersede half a batch. (5) Directional deadlines ($d_j=$ directional distance $/|s|$, behind-head-but-in-window ranked by window exit) would fix the undirected-tie misorder in the priority sort.

### 9.3 Multi-source slot sharing: DRR as the fairness mechanism

**Setting.** $n$ composited archives share one process-wide scheduler with a fixed budget of 24 slots (module singleton, shared-scheduler.ts:57-93). Fairness is explicitly two-level: the _problem_ — maximize the minimum buffered runway across incomplete required sources, since the clock min-gates on the slowest source — lives in §11 (playback governor & QoS), where a proportional controller sets per-source run-ahead caps and sheds DRR weights. This subsection keeps only the lower level: the scheduler mechanism that realizes whatever effective weights $\tilde w_i$ the §11 controller writes. Each source with runnable work is credited $\tilde w_i$ once per round (deficit clamped to one quantum), may dispatch only with deficit $\ge 1$, and spends 1 per dispatch; within eligibility the globally lowest §9.2 priority value wins (ties FIFO); the scheme is work-conserving (idle sources earn nothing; a top-up guarantees no wasted slot). Kill switch `multiSourceFairness`. All IMPLEMENTED.

**Problem.** Given sources $i=1..n$ with effective weights $\tilde w_i>0$ (clamped to $[0.25,4]\times w_i$ by the §11 controller), $m=24$ discrete slots, and per-source queues of jobs carrying priority values: decide the source served at each slot-free event, with $O(1)$ work per dispatch, so that the weighted max-min (GPS) relaxation holds at slot granularity — over any interval $[T_1,T_2]$ during which source $i$ is continuously backlogged, slots granted $S_i$ satisfy the DRR bound

$$\Big|S_i-\frac{\tilde w_i}{\sum_j \tilde w_j}\,m\,(T_2-T_1)\Big|\le O\big(\max_j \tilde w_j\big),$$

with no starvation (every backlogged source eventually accumulates a full credit). Subject to: work conservation (no slot idles while any source has runnable work); running requests never re-ranked or preempted; deficits bounded (credit at most once per round, clamp at one quantum) so no source banks unbounded share; the single-archive case must be unchanged (work conservation hands one source all 24 slots); fairness engages only with $\ge 2$ sources and $\ge 1$ incomplete required source.

**Incumbent.** DRR with per-round crediting, deficit clamp, and priority-within-eligibility at request-scheduler.ts:479-610 (`creditRound` :584-593, `pickEligible` :599-610); the code documents the prior broken variant that credited per-dispatch and starved light sources (:445-478). Singleton wiring at shared-scheduler.ts:57-93; archive-side sourceId/weight at archive.ts:2948-2963 with a per-archive concurrency ceiling at archive.ts:2994-3003. The upper level — the water-filling approximation via run-ahead caps and weight shedding — is §11's incumbent.

**Class.** Generalized Processor Sharing / Weighted Fair Queuing; Deficit Round Robin (Shreedhar & Varghese 1996: $O(1)$ per packet, quantum-bounded unfairness); the discrete-slot version of proportional fairness. Max-min fairness and water-filling (convex, exactly solvable) belong to §11's formulation.

**Knobs.** `maxRequests`=24 (`configureSharedScheduler`); default weight 1, first-weight-pinned per outstanding queue, live-overridable via `setSourceWeight`; `multiSourceFairness` kill switch. (Cap/shed/hysteresis knobs — slack, clamp bands, deadband — are §11's.)

**Gap.** DRR's fair share binds only while leaders have queued work — in practice §11's run-ahead cap does most of the real work. Slot-granular fairness could instead meter _bytes_ (weighted byte-share), since range-group sizes vary by orders of magnitude and one slot is not one unit of link capacity. Byte-density-aware weight shedding ($\rho_i$ in the controller) is §11's gap; the mechanism here would consume it unchanged.

### 9.4 Decoded-tile eviction: approximate Belady with a known future

**Setting.** The tileset caches decoded tiles under two budgets (default 2000 tiles / 2 GiB, tileset:815-818, byte-accounted incrementally). Under budget, eviction is a wall-clock grace timer (120 s animating / 30 s paused, :3816) from which coverage-index tiles — current viewport at primary zoom, full time range, exactly what the buffered-ranges bar reports — are exempt: timing them out would silently un-buffer time the governor was just told is ready. Over budget, a playhead-relative tiered plan replaces plain LRU (which would evict exactly the just-prefetched runway, since prefetched = least-recently-touched): tier A = tiles outside the coverage index (stale viewports/zooms), LRU order; tier B = coverage tiles more than $\text{keepBehind}=\max(W,\beta)$ behind the playhead along the committed direction, furthest-behind first; tier C = coverage tiles more than $\text{protectedAhead}=\max(W,2\beta)$ ahead, furthest-ahead first; tier D = the near-playhead protected window, LRU, last resort (:3914-3915, 3922-3947). Needed, pinned, and in-flight tiles are never candidates (evicting in-flight corrupts byte accounting — a batch holds direct references, :3852-3858). Any tier-C/D eviction increments `runwayEvictions` and signals §9.1's pressure ladder. The pinned set is the overview storyboard: `preloadOverviewTier` makes an all-or-nothing decision to pin the coarsest tiles (zooms $\le 1$, whole world, full time range) as a scrub preview, taken iff their directory byte sum $\le 20$ MiB (satellites z0 is ~17 MB per tile — pinning those would wreck the cache); pinned tiles are exempt from eviction, flush, and supersession but count against the limits (:3442, :3472, budget :257, zoom cutoff :260). All IMPLEMENTED.

**Problem.** Given cached tiles $i$ with byte size $b_i$, bucket start $t_i$, cell $c_i$; capacities $N_{\max}$ (count) and $B_{\max}$ (bytes); playhead $\theta(\tau)=\theta_0+s\tau$ with committed direction $d$; window $W$; refetch cost $c_i$ (bytes over the link plus decode CPU) for any evicted tile accessed again; and an overview candidate set $O$ with per-subset scrub-coverage utility $u(P)$ for $P\subseteq O$. Because the trajectory is known, each tile's next-access time is approximately computable:

$$\nu_i=\begin{cases}\tau_{now}+\dfrac{\big(d\,(t_i-\theta)-W/2\big)^+}{|s|} & c_i\in V \text{ and } d\,(t_i+\beta-\theta)\ge -\tfrac{W}{2}\\[4pt] \infty & \text{behind the head (no loop) or } c_i\notin V.\end{cases}$$

Decide (a) on each over-budget event, an eviction set $E$ with $\sum_{i\notin E} b_i\le B_{\max}$ and $|\{i\notin E\}|\le N_{\max}$ — implicitly a total order truncated where capacities are satisfied, plus the tier boundaries keepBehind and protectedAhead; and (b) the pinned set $P\subseteq O$. Minimize total future refetch cost

$$\sum_{i\in E} c_i\,\Pr[i\text{ re-accessed}]\;\approx\;\sum_{i\in E} c_i\,\mathbf{1}[\nu_i<\infty],$$

and for the pin subproblem maximize $u(P)$ subject to $\sum_{u\in P}s(u)\le 20\ \text{MiB}$, decided before any fetch from directory sizes alone. Subject to the capacity constraints and hard exclusions: never evict `neededTileKeys`, pinned tiles, or in-flight headers; grace exemption for coverage tiles under budget (the runway must not evaporate by wall-clock age while paused). With uniform sizes the eviction problem is exactly Belady's MIN — evict $\arg\max_i \nu_i$; with variable sizes and costs it is offline generalized caching (NP-hard; 4-approximation via primal-dual/local-ratio known). The known trajectory makes STT's instance quasi-offline between interruptions.

**Incumbent.** Tiered greedy at tileset:3811-3982: candidate filter :3880-3890, tier classification :3922-3943, per-tier sort orders :3944-3947, concatenated plan truncated when under limits :3949-3973, runway-eviction feedback :3969-3979, physical evict with incremental accounting :3988-4014. Under one-directional playback the tier order B→C→D matches the Belady order ($\nu=\infty$ behind, decreasing in ahead-distance for C, imminent for D), so the incumbent is a good Belady approximation _given its assumptions_; the fallback without a coverage index/playhead is plain LRU (:3901-3907). Pinning is an all-or-nothing threshold accept/reject at a fixed zoom cutoff — no subset selection when slightly over budget; the overview queue drains only at priority-idle (:3582).

**Class.** Belady/MIN (offline optimal, uniform sizes); weighted caching (k-server on a uniform metric); generalized/file caching with sizes and costs — offline NP-hard, 4-approx (Bar-Noy et al. local-ratio), online GreedyDual-Size; LRU is $k$-competitive, and the tier policy exploits future knowledge LRU forfeits; the pin decision is budgeted maximum coverage (submodular, greedy $1-1/e$); MSE back/forward buffer trimming is the same structure with $W$ as the protected window.

**Knobs.** `maxCacheSize`=2000 tiles, `maxCacheByteSize`=2 GiB (tileset:817-818); grace 120 s animating / 30 s paused (:3816); keepBehind = $\max(W,\beta)$, protectedAhead = $\max(W,2\beta)$ (:3914-3915); `DEFAULT_OVERVIEW_BUDGET_BYTES`=20 MiB (:257), `DEFAULT_OVERVIEW_MAX_ZOOM`=1 (:260).
