**Gap.** (1) Byte-blindness within tiers: ranking is purely temporal-distance/LRU while the freed resource is bytes — a cost-density rule (evict max $b_i/c_i\cdot\nu_i$, GreedyDual-Size style) frees the byte budget with fewer future refetches; measurable as refetched bytes per session under memory pressure. (2) Looping playback breaks the $\nu_i=\infty$-behind assumption: on a wrap, far-behind tiles are the _most_ imminent, and tier B evicts them first — the exact inverse of Belady; the governor knows the loop boundary and could rotate the distance metric. (3) Tier A treats all non-coverage tiles as equal-value LRU, but a tile one pan away has far higher re-access probability than one from a long-departed viewport — a spatial distance term is free from the tile key. (4) Pin subset selection (greedy submodular) when the candidate set slightly exceeds 20 MiB; the roadmap also flags an unconfirmed pinned-_count_ gate gap (hurricanes: 17,899 candidate pins vs the 2000-tile cap — the budget gates bytes, not count).

### 9.5 Persistent (OPFS) cache admission and eviction across sessions

**Setting.** Every decompressed tile payload fetched over the network is written through to an origin-scoped OPFS directory keyed by (archive-url, tile-id, archive-fingerprint), where the fingerprint is the content-addressed directory hash — it changes iff the tiles change, giving free invalidation on redeploy (archive.ts:2456-2519). A returning visitor skips both the network range request and zstd decompression; only Arrow IPC parse remains. Lookup order per tile is memory → OPFS → network (getTile archive.ts:2696-2755, getTiles :3106-3162; the memory tier is §7's compressed-byte two-level LRU), and corrupt OPFS payloads — validated by declared uncompressedSize, then by decode — self-heal by refetching from origin, overwriting the bad entry. An in-memory index (mirrored to index.json, batch-flushed, Web-Locks-serialized across tabs, opfs-cache.ts:317-329) tracks per-entry bytes and a monotonic lastAccess counter (:156-165); orphan .bin files from crashed tabs are swept once per init (:345-368); unavailable OPFS degrades to a silent no-op. The feature is opt-in and default OFF (`opfsCache: false`) because the cold-path duplicate decompress hurts first-visit pan/zoom. All IMPLEMENTED; there is no admission filter and no cost/size awareness.

**Problem.** Given a stream of tile fetches where tile $i$ has byte size $b_i$; hit benefit $c^{hit}_i$ = network transfer time at link rate + zstd decompress CPU saved on a future access; write cost $c^w_i$ = OPFS write + index churn; cross-session re-access probability $p_i$ (the probability the same origin requests key $i$ again before invalidation, driven by revisit behavior and the fingerprint in the key); and disk budget $D=\min(512\ \text{MB},\ \text{quota}/2)$. Decide admission $x_i\in\{0,1\}$ (persist tile $i$ when fetched?) and an eviction policy over the resident set whenever $\sum_{resident} b_j > D$. Maximize expected net benefit of the resident set:

$$\max\;\sum_i x_i\big(p_i\,c^{hit}_i-c^w_i\big)\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\sum_i x_i\,b_i\le D.$$

Subject to: correctness under multi-tab concurrency (last-writer-wins index + Web Locks + orphan sweep); silent degradation when OPFS is unavailable; keys self-invalidate on redeploy via the fingerprint, so there is no explicit invalidation decision. The offline version is a 0/1 knapsack (NP-hard; the greedy density rule $(p_ic^{hit}_i-c^w_i)/b_i$ is near-optimal for $b_i\ll D$); the online version with unknown $p_i$ is generalized caching with admission, where competitive algorithms rank by benefit/size with recency/frequency estimating $p_i$. A further open design variable is the placement relation to the memory tier — inclusive today; exclusive placement is an unresolved choice.

**Incumbent.** Admit-everything write-through: every network-fetched decompressed payload is `set()` unconditionally (opfs-cache.ts:408-450) — $x_i\equiv 1$, i.e. the admission problem is not solved at all. Eviction is pure LRU by monotonic lastAccess (sort ascending, delete until under budget, :457-478), size- and cost-blind; the monotonic counter makes LRU deterministic across same-millisecond writes and persists ordering across sessions.

**Class.** 0/1 knapsack (offline admission); generalized file caching with variable sizes and costs — offline NP-hard, 4-approx; online GreedyDual-Size is $k$-competitive for the generalized problem; cache admission (TinyLFU, AdaptSize); two-level cache hierarchy placement; write-amplification vs hit-rate tradeoffs from the SSD cache literature (e.g. Flashield).

**Knobs.** `maxBytes` default 512 MiB (opfs-cache.ts:169); quota clamp factor 1/2 (:211-224); `opfsCache` (default false), `opfsCacheMaxBytes`, `opfsCacheDirectory`; directory name; index flush batching (setTimeout 0); `INDEX_VERSION` wipe-on-breaking-change.

**Gap.** (1) LRU ranks a 17 MB satellite z0 tile and a 5 KB leaf identically by recency; GreedyDual-Size (evict min $H_j=L+c^{hit}_j/b_j$) would keep more hit-value per byte — measurable as cold-start bytes fetched on second visit at fixed $D$. (2) No admission filter: tiles whose refetch is cheaper than an OPFS write+read round trip (tiny tiles on fast links) still pay the write and index churn — a size/benefit threshold or TinyLFU-style frequency gate would cut write amplification with no hit-rate loss. (3) The what-to-persist decision could exploit the same playhead/coverage structure as §9.4: overview/storyboard and low-zoom tiles have the highest cross-session re-access probability (every revisit starts there), yet get no preference. (4) $p_i$ is estimable from the persisted index itself (hits per entry) at zero extra I/O.

## 10. Decode scheduling

Decode is the CPU half of the data-delivery pipeline and the twin of the network request scheduler (see §9 — EDF request scheduling with cancellation): every fetched tile pays a decompress + Arrow-IPC-parse + binary-extraction cost of roughly 5–20 ms (tile-decoder.ts:11–14) before it can render. This section formalizes where that work runs — main thread vs a worker pool, and which worker — as two coupled problems: capacity provisioning (mode and pool size) and online job placement with cancellations. The resources traded are main-thread frame budget, worker parallelism, copy bytes at the transfer boundary, and CPU wasted on work that gets aborted.

### 10.1 Decode placement and pool sizing

**Setting.** `createDefaultTileDecoder` (tile-decoder.ts:579–596) picks between two implementations: `InlineTileDecoder` (:113), which runs CRC check, decompress, and `decodeTile` synchronously on the calling thread (:146–165), and `WorkerTileDecoder` (:227), which fans work out to a pool of module Workers. The worker path is documented as the only way to sustain 60 fps while streaming a many-thousand-tile dataset, since one inline decode consumes a full frame budget (:11–14). Pool size is fixed at construction: $m = \max(1, \min(4, \kappa - 1))$ where $\kappa$ is `navigator.hardwareConcurrency` (defaulting to 4 when unreported), with the stated rationale "one core for the main thread + render loop; cap at 4 so we don't out-spawn the network or thrash on small machines" (:244–255). The pool is a process-wide singleton shared by every open archive through reference-counted leases; workers survive until the last archive finalizes (:534–571). Each worker carries standing cost: a spawned isolate plus a structured-cloned copy of the v2 schema-template registry, re-sent on every spawn and respawn (:277–284, tile-decoder.worker.ts:55–58).

**Problem.** Given: logical core count $\kappa \in \mathbb{N}$; frame period $F$ (16.7 ms at 60 fps); an online stream of decode jobs $j$ with release times $r_j$ and service times $p_j$ (empirically 5–20 ms); network tile-delivery rate $\lambda_{\text{net}}$ (tiles/s, set by §9's scheduler and the link); per-worker decode rate $\mu = 1/\mathbb{E}[p_j]$; per-worker memory footprint $M_w$ (isolate + template registry). Decision variables: mode $\in \{\text{inline}, \text{pool}\}$ and, in pool mode, $m \in \{1, \dots, \kappa\}$. Let $C_j$ be job $j$'s completion time and $B_{\text{main}}$ the main-thread decode busy time per frame. Minimize

$$\mathbb{E}[C_j - r_j] \;+\; \alpha \cdot B_{\text{main}}$$

(expected tile time-to-ready plus render-interference penalty, $\alpha$ the exchange rate between a late tile and a dropped frame), subject to:

- **Feasibility.** Pool mode requires `new Worker(url, {type:'module'})` to construct; otherwise mode = inline is forced (Node, SSR, older browsers) (:579–596).
- **Frame budget.** $B_{\text{main}} \le (1-\beta)F$ for render share $\beta$; inline mode makes $B_{\text{main}} = \sum_{j \text{ decoded this frame}} p_j$, and any single decode with $p_j \ge F$ drops at least one frame.
- **Render-core reservation.** Pool workers occupy cores: $m \le \kappa - 1$ keeps one core for the render loop.
- **Provisioning.** $m\mu \gtrsim \lambda_{\text{net}}$ (decode keeps pace with the network) but not $\gg$ — workers beyond delivery rate sit idle while costing $M_w$ each ("out-spawn the network", :252–254).
- **Memory.** $m \cdot M_w \le M_{\max}$.

**Incumbent.** Mode selection is a feasibility probe, not an optimization: try the shared pool, fall back to inline on synchronous throw with a console warning (:580–595). Pool size is the hardcoded rule $m = \max(1, \min(4, \kappa-1))$ (:254) — the reservation of one core and the cap of 4 are both fixed constants, chosen once, never revisited against measured queue wait, even though the worker-path telemetry sample deliberately includes main→worker→main queue latency precisely to expose pool saturation (:331–335, :358–363).

**Class.** Static capacity provisioning for an $M/G/m$ queue with a shared-resource constraint (the render core); the mode choice is a degenerate two-point feasibility problem. Choosing $m$ to balance flow time against idle capacity is the classic square-root-staffing regime, here answered with a constant.

**Knobs.** `poolSize` (overrides the sizing rule, :244–247), `workerUrl` (:257–258). The constants 4 (cap) and 1 (reserved core) are not exposed.

**Gap.** An adaptive controller could resize the pool from the queue-wait telemetry it already emits: grow $m$ when queue wait dominates raw decode, shrink when workers idle — moving $\mathbb{E}[C_j - r_j]$ directly. The shared singleton pool also serves all open archives with no per-source accounting, so one dataset's decode burst starves another's; fairness machinery exists only upstream (see §11 — multi-source run-ahead fairness; §9 — DRR slot sharing). Neither is implemented, and the repo backlog carries no decode-scheduling item.

### 10.2 Online dispatch to the pool with free pre-dispatch cancellation

**Setting.** Each `decode()` call assigns the job, immediately and irrevocably, to the worker with the smallest pending count — a linear scan justified as negligible at $m \le 4$ (tile-decoder.ts:314–323). The compressed payload is _not_ copied at enqueue: jobs wait in a per-worker host-side queue, and `dispatchNext` makes the transferable `slice(0)` at the last responsible moment, dispatching exactly one job per worker at a time (:325–328, :392–410, :419–434). This is what makes cancellation real: an abort before dispatch deletes the host-queued entry — no copy, no worker CPU, immediate rejection (:374–391); an abort after dispatch cannot preempt the synchronous decode — the caller settles immediately, the worker runs to completion, and the late response is discarded while releasing the slot (:57–63, :488–490). The worker itself implements a mid-pipeline cancellation point — a `cancel` message checked after the unavoidable decompress but before the more expensive IPC parse (tile-decoder.worker.ts:45–48, :66–76, :116) — but no host code ever posts it, so the point is dormant. A worker crash fails every job assigned to it, _including host-queued jobs the worker never saw_, then respawns a replacement to hold $m$ constant (:493–526).

**Problem.** Given: $m$ identical machines; jobs $j$ arriving online at release times $r_j$, each with observable compressed size $b_j$ (bytes), unobservable service time $p_j$ (revealed at completion; increasing in $b_j$), and class weight $w_j$ (viewport-blocking vs prefetch vs overview — assigned upstream by §9's network scheduler and _not visible_ in `DecodeArgs`, :25–71); an online abort process marking a subset $A$ of jobs at times $a_j$; a per-dispatch copy cost $c \cdot b_j$ (the transferable slice). Decision variables: an assignment $\sigma(j) \in \{1,\dots,m\}$ chosen irrevocably at $r_j$ (no migration, no work stealing), and a service order $\pi_i$ over each machine's host queue, which determine dispatch times $d_j$ and completions $C_j = d_j + p_j$. Minimize

$$\sum_{j \notin A} w_j\,(C_j - r_j) \;+\; \gamma \sum_{\substack{j \in A \\ a_j \ge d_j}} p_j$$

— class-weighted flow time of completed jobs, plus (at exchange rate $\gamma$) the CPU wasted on jobs aborted after dispatch — subject to:

- **Unit capacity.** Machine $i$ serves at most one job at a time; $d_j \ge \max(r_j,\; C_{j'})$ for the job $j'$ preceding $j$ in $\pi_{\sigma(j)}$.
- **Non-preemption.** Once dispatched, a job occupies its machine until $C_j$ even if aborted at $a_j \ge d_j$ (result discarded, slot freed only at completion, :488–490).
- **Free pre-dispatch abort.** A job with $a_j < d_j$ leaves the system at cost 0 (host-queue removal, no copy) (:374–391).
- **Immediate irrevocable dispatch.** $\sigma(j)$ is fixed at $r_j$; queues do not rebalance when another machine idles.
- **Crash coupling.** If machine $i$ fails at time $\tau$, every uncompleted job with $\sigma(j) = i$ fails — active and host-queued alike (:500–511).

**Incumbent.** Greedy least-pending-count: $\sigma(j) = \arg\min_i n_i$ where $n_i$ counts that worker's active + host-queued jobs, ties to the lowest index (:314–319). The rule ignores $b_j$ (a 5 MB tile and a 2 KB tile each count 1) and $w_j$ (invisible at this layer); each host queue is served strictly FIFO (`queue.shift()`, :421–422). Dispatch defers the copy (:325–328, :427) and transfers the decoded typed-array buffers back zero-copy (worker.ts:10–14, :127), with the decompressed payload riding the same transferred buffer when an OPFS write will follow (:47–55, worker.ts:129–139; the OPFS tier itself is §9's problem). Priority ordering exists only upstream in the network scheduler; by decode time it has been erased.

**Class.** Online list scheduling on $m$ identical machines in the immediate-dispatch model — Graham's greedy is $(2 - \tfrac{1}{m})$-competitive, but for _makespan_, not the objective that matters here. The true objective is online non-preemptive weighted flow time, which is NP-hard offline and admits no constant-competitive online algorithm without resource augmentation (speed scaling); the abort process adds scheduling-with-cancellations structure, where the incumbent's host-side queue is precisely a mechanism for making cancellations free before commitment.

**Knobs.** None for policy: assignment rule, FIFO order, and one-active-job-per-worker are all hardcoded. Two designs are documented as REJECTED in the code itself, as constraints worth keeping: copy-at-enqueue (rejected — it doubled peak memory for jobs that were cancelled before any worker saw them, :325–328) and worker-side queueing with cancel messages (rejected — a cancel would queue _behind_ the work it targets, :414–418).

**Gap.** Four unimplemented improvements, in decreasing bang-per-line (none is on the backlog):

1. **A single pool-wide host queue with pull-on-idle.** Because _all_ queued work is host-resident and the copy happens at dispatch anyway, early per-worker assignment buys nothing and costs head-of-line blocking: a long decode strands the tiles queued behind it while another worker idles. A central work-conserving queue strictly dominates immediate dispatch here, and shrinks the crash blast radius to the one active job (host-queued jobs would simply be re-pulled instead of failed, :500–511).
2. **Class/deadline-aware ordering.** Propagate $w_j$ or a playhead-proximity deadline from §9's scheduler into `DecodeArgs` and serve the host queue by it instead of FIFO — the decode stage currently randomizes exactly the priority the network stage worked to establish.
3. **Size-aware balancing.** $b_j$ = `compressed.byteLength` is observable at enqueue (:329) and is a usable proxy for $p_j$; least-pending-_bytes_ beats least-pending-count whenever tile sizes are skewed.
4. **Wire the dormant cancel.** Post the worker's `cancel` message on abort-of-active-job so the worker skips the IPC parse after decompress — the code's own comment identifies this as "the biggest chunk of wasted work a cancel can still save" once dispatched (worker.ts:66–76), yet the message has no sender; this directly reduces the $\gamma \sum p_j$ waste term.

The measurable objectives all exist already: per-decode round-trip latency including queue wait on the worker path (:358–363) and the decompress/IPC split on the inline path (:175–183).

## 11. Playback governor & quality of service

The playback governor (`packages/playback`) is the online controller that decides whether the sim-clock may advance, at what rate, and which of several competing archives the shared link should feed first. It trades the classic streaming QoE currencies — startup delay, stall count, stall duration, playback rate — against buffered-runway inventory, bandwidth, and cache pressure, with one structural advantage over video players: the tile directory prices any future sim-window in exact bytes. Upstream, a pure parameter-derivation function fixes the operating point (speed, window, range) each dataset plays at; downstream, its run-ahead caps and weights actuate the prefetch and scheduling machinery of §9.

### 11.1 Stall-free playback gating as online control

**Setting.** `PlaybackGovernor` wraps a dumb wall-clock×speed `TimeController` (packages/playback/src/time-controller.ts:394-493) and continuously gates the playhead. Play/resume requires a buffered runway of `startGateWallMs×|speed|` sim-ms (×`resumeFactor` after a mid-playback stall — ExoPlayer-style hysteresis); while playing, a 200 ms tick probe checks a low watermark and freezes the clock into a `buffering` gate when the runway drains. A _canplaythrough_ predictor overrides both gates: if the missing bytes of the gate window (exact, from the directory) are estimated to download within the wall time the buffered runway buys, play anyway — so gates do not scale linearly with `|speed|`. A per-tick frontier clamp snaps an overrunning playhead back onto loaded data (playback-governor.ts:543-576); after `maxStartWaitMs` an escape hatch enters _degraded creep_, pinning the playhead to the data-arrival frontier instead of looping 8-s freezes (:1582-1607, re-arm :1666-1671). The state estimator is the archive's dual-EWMA throughput tracker (packages/core/src/throughput.ts:55-123); runway and byte-cost measurement are exact walks over the tileset's coverage index (packages/core/src/spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:3135-3223, :3283-3330). QoE counters (stallCount, totalStallMs, startupMs, degradedResumeCount, creepMs) accumulate on the transitions. All IMPLEMENTED.

**Problem.** _Given:_ wall time $t \ge 0$; signed speed $s$ (sim-ms per wall-ms); direction $d = \mathrm{sign}(s)$. A stochastic arrival process grows the resident sim-time set $R(t) \subseteq \mathbb{R}$ for the current viewport; the playhead is $p(t)$, the runway $r(t) = \sup\{\delta \ge 0 : [p(t), p(t)+d\,\delta] \subseteq R(t)\}$, with completion flag $c(t) \in \{0,1\}$. The directory gives the timeline byte density $\rho(u)$ — bytes of viewport tile data whose bucket covers sim-time $u$ — exactly integrable ($\int_a^b \rho = \texttt{estimateCost}([a,b]).\mathrm{bytes}$), hence the missing byte cost $b(\Omega)$ of any sim-window $\Omega$. The link's service rate $\mu(\tau)$ (bytes/wall-ms) is unknown and sampled; the state estimate is $\widehat{C}(t)$ with ETA $\widehat{\eta}(\Omega) = b(\Omega)/\widehat{C}(t)$. Weights $\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \lambda > 0$ encode QoE. _Decision variables:_ a control policy $u(t) \in \{\mathrm{advance}, \mathrm{freeze}, \mathrm{creep}\}$, measurable w.r.t. the observation history (probes of $r$, $c$, $b$, $\widehat{\eta}$), with dynamics
$$\dot p(t) = \begin{cases} s & u(t)=\mathrm{advance} \\ 0 & u(t)=\mathrm{freeze} \\ \min(s, \dot F(t)) & u(t)=\mathrm{creep} \end{cases}$$
where $F(t) = p(t) + d\, r(t)$ is the buffered frontier; implicitly also the gate thresholds $(G_{start}, G_{low}, \varphi_r, W_{\max})$ if treated as a tunable policy family. _Minimize_ expected QoE cost over the session $[0,T]$:
$$J = \mathbb{E}\Big[\; \alpha\, N_{\mathrm{stall}} \; + \; \beta \int_0^T \mathbf{1}[u(t)=\mathrm{freeze},\ \text{intent}=\text{play}]\,dt \; + \; \gamma\, t_{\mathrm{start}} \; - \; \lambda \int_0^T \frac{\dot p(t)}{s}\,dt \Big]$$
where $N_{\mathrm{stall}}$ counts entries into freeze while playing and $t_{\mathrm{start}}$ is the play-request-to-first-advance delay. _Subject to:_

- _Data completeness (hard):_ $p(t) \in R(t)$ whenever $u(t) \ne \mathrm{freeze}$ — no frame renders from unloaded sim-time; skipping is disallowed by design, so the only levers are when to stall and how fast to move.
- _Fluid-buffer feasibility (the certificate an unfreeze needs):_ resuming at $t_0$ with runway $r_0$ continues stall-free iff
  $$\forall T \ge 0:\quad \int_{t_0}^{t_0+T} \mu(\tau)\,d\tau \;\ge\; \int_{p(t_0)+d\,r_0}^{\,p(t_0)+d\,|s|\,T} \rho(u)\,du \quad\text{(along direction } d\text{)},$$
  with steady-state necessary condition $\mu \ge |s|\,\bar\rho$ (utilization $<1$). The incumbent's gates and its playthrough predictor are one-window truncations of this condition.
- _Liveness:_ freeze duration per gate $\le W_{\max}$ (a broken network must not hard-lock playback).
- _Observation cost:_ probes of $r$ are metered ($\ge 200$ ms wall spacing).

**Incumbent.** Static two-threshold hysteresis with an ETA lookahead override: the gate passes when $r \ge G_{start}\,|s|\,\phi$ with $\phi = 1$ at start/seek and $\phi = \varphi_r = 2$ after a stall (playback-governor.ts:1538-1574), or the runway is complete, or `predictsPlaythrough` holds — $\max_i \widehat{\eta} \le \max(r/|s|,\,250\,\mathrm{ms})$ (:1933-1960); stall when $r < G_{low}\,|s|$ and not complete and no playthrough prediction (:1887-1923). The estimator is the pessimistic point value $\widehat C = \min(\mathrm{EWMA}_{3s}, \mathrm{EWMA}_{9s})$, duration-weighted and bias-corrected (throughput.ts:55-123) — a heuristic lower confidence bound. Frontier clamp + external-seek discrimination at :543-576 (overruns > 1 wall-s×|s| are treated as seeks, never snapped); 8-s escape hatch → degraded creep (:1582-1607). Everything is greedy and myopic: constants $G_{start}=2000$, $G_{low}=600$, $\varphi_r=2$, $W_{\max}=8000$ wall-ms (:616-620) are transplanted from video-player practice (ExoPlayer's 2.5 s / 5 s), not optimized against this system's measured arrival process, and the lookahead is a single window rather than the full $\forall T$ check.

**Class.** Constrained MDP / optimal stopping with stochastic arrivals; start-stop queueing control — two-threshold hysteresis is provably optimal for M/G/1 N-policy-style problems, which justifies the _shape_ of the incumbent but not its constants. The video analogs are Lyapunov drift-plus-penalty (BOLA, INFOCOM'16) and MPC (RobustMPC, SIGCOMM'15) — here deadlines are exact, not estimated, since the byte oracle is exact. Fluid playout-buffer models and Loynes stability ($\rho<1$) govern feasibility. Online, stochastic; the offline clairvoyant optimum (full arrival trace known) is computable by DP over stall points.

**Knobs.** startGateWallMs=2000, lowWatermarkWallMs=600, resumeFactor=2, maxStartWaitMs=8000, PLAYTHROUGH_MIN_WALL_MS=250, TICK_PROBE_INTERVAL_MS=200, EVAL_INTERVAL_MS=250, CLAMP_MAX_OVERRUN_REAL_MS=1000 (all wall-ms; runway thresholds scale ×|s| at evaluation); estimator half-lives 3000/9000 ms; runway probe horizon $\max(4W,\,|s|\cdot 10{,}000\,\mathrm{ms})$ (spatiotemporal-tileset.ts:125).

**Gap.** STT knows the full future byte profile $\rho(u)$ to dataset end, so the complete fluid feasibility check is computable in one directory walk — it would catch the "thin now, byte-cliff two windows ahead" case the one-window predictor passes. Thresholds are speed-scaled but otherwise static: no adaptation to throughput variance (a jittery link warrants a larger $G_{low}$), and the 8-s escape hatch is a fixed timer rather than a cost-based decision. A drift-plus-penalty or MPC controller over the exact $b(\Omega)$ oracle could provably trade $N_{\mathrm{stall}}$ against startup latency, and the estimator could publish a quantile instead of a point min. Measurable objective: the `getQoeStats()` counters under throughput traces.

### 11.2 Multi-source readiness fold: robust min-gate under cadence jitter

**Setting.** $N$ archives share one playhead; each source registers as _required_ (gates the clock) or _optional_ (continue-and-degrade). `overlayGatesPlayback` defaults TRUE app-side, so visible overlays gate by default. Buffer health folds over the required subset only: runway = min, complete = AND, bytesPending = Σ, buffered ranges = interval intersection (MSE HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA semantics). Heterogeneous archives almost never share a temporal bucket cadence, so healthy frontiers land at fractionally different sim-times and a raw min spuriously stalls (the documented failure is W3C Bug 26436). The fold therefore lifts every incomplete required source within a tolerance band of the _leading_ incomplete runway up to the leader before taking the min; a genuine laggard keeps its real runway and still drags the floor. The same banded fold feeds the gate, the watermark, and the frontier clamp. IMPLEMENTED (shipped 2026-06-19 + tolerance Phase 1).

**Problem.** _Given:_ $n$ required sources; at query time the governor observes per-source runways $r_i \ge 0$ and completion flags $c_i \in \{0,1\}$. Source $i$ discretizes time into buckets of size $\Delta_i$, so jointly healthy sources still show frontier jitter $\varepsilon_{ij}$ with $|\varepsilon_{ij}| \le \max(\Delta_i, \Delta_j)$, plus probe staleness $\le \pi\,|s|$ ($\pi$ = probe interval). Speed $|s|$; watermark $G_{low}|s|$. _Decision variables:_ an estimator $\hat r = f(r_1..r_n, c_1..c_n; \tau)$ of the joint gating runway, parameterized by tolerance $\tau \ge 0$ (sim-ms); downstream, the stall decision $\mathbf{1}[\hat r < G_{low}|s| \wedge \neg\bigwedge_i c_i]$. _Minimize_ the false-stall rate under cadence jitter:
$$\Pr\big[\hat r < G_{low}|s| \;\big|\; \text{all sources healthy: } \min_i r_i^{true} \ge G_{low}|s| \text{ up to jitter } \varepsilon\big]$$
_Subject to (safety — no missed starvation):_
$$\hat r \;\le\; \min_{i:\, c_i = 0} r_i \; + \; \tau$$
(the estimate may exceed the true laggard by at most $\tau$); a completed source never lowers the floor ($c_i = 1 \Rightarrow$ excluded from the min); with $n=0$ required sources, $\hat r = \infty$ (never stall). Structurally, the lift is measured between sources (against the leader), never against the gate or watermark itself.

**Incumbent.** `foldRequiredRunways` (playback-governor.ts:1328-1380), two passes: collect incomplete runways and the leader $L = \max_{i: c_i=0} r_i$, then
$$\hat r \;=\; \min_{i:\, c_i = 0} \begin{cases} L & L - r_i \le \tau \\ r_i & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}, \qquad \tau = \tau_w\,|s|,$$
with $\tau=0$ reproducing the exact raw min. Used by the gate (:1553-1559), the watermark (:1901-1907), and the frontier probe (:1657-1665). The hardcoding: $\tau_w$ defaults to TICK_PROBE_INTERVAL_MS = 200 wall-ms (:624-627) — tied to the governor's own observation resolution, NOT to the per-source cadences $\Delta_i$ that actually generate the jitter. `getBufferedRanges` intersects full per-source range lists (:984-1004, `intersectRanges` :2020-2039); required/optional is a per-source boolean (`addSource` :711-749).

**Class.** Robust order statistics under bounded per-coordinate perturbation; hypothesis testing with an indifference zone (stall iff the laggard is behind by more than the jitter bound); MSE/HTMLMediaElement buffered-intersection semantics as the SoTA anchor. The safety constraint makes it one-sided robust estimation — polynomial; the interesting part is choosing $\tau$, a statistics problem, not a search problem.

**Knobs.** runwayToleranceMs=200 wall-ms (0 = exact raw-min/AND); per-source required flag (default true; app default `overlayGatesPlayback ?? true`); per-source weight (feeds §11.3).

**Gap.** Archive metadata knows every source's actual bucket size (`temporalBucketMs`), so the theoretically right band is per-pair $\tau_{ij} \approx \max(\Delta_i, \Delta_j)$ or per-source $\tau_i = \Delta_i$. The global 200 wall-ms constant under-absorbs jitter for coarse-bucket sources at low speed (residual false stalls) and over-absorbs for fine-bucket ones at high speed (masks up to 200 wall-ms of genuine lag). Measurable objective: stallCount on multi-cadence composites (e.g. radar + fronts) versus stallCount with oracle jitter removal.

### 11.3 Two-level multi-source fairness: max-min run-ahead toward the required laggard

**Setting.** Because the clock min-gates on the required laggard (§11.2), any buffer a leader holds past laggard+slack is dead weight: it cannot render before the laggard catches up, its fetches contend with the laggard's on the shared link, and it feeds cache-pressure eviction that can evict the very runway the gate waits on (§9 — cache eviction). On each existing probe cadence (never a new timer) the governor (1) caps every non-laggard source's forward prefetch via `setPrefetchRunAheadLimit` at laggard + slack, laggards uncapped; and (2) re-weights each incomplete required source's fair share in the process-shared scheduler. The lower level realizing those weights is a true Deficit Round Robin scheduler over a fixed 24-slot budget (packages/core/src/shared-scheduler.ts, request-scheduler.ts): each source with runnable work is credited its weight once per round (deficit clamped to one quantum), dispatches only with deficit ≥ 1, and within eligibility the globally lowest priority value wins; the scheduler is work-conserving, so idle capacity is never wasted. Laggard identity uses enter/exit hysteresis bands and control writes are deadbanded. All IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-22).

**Problem.** _Given:_ a shared link of capacity $C$ bytes/wall-ms, realized at $m = 24$ discrete request slots; $n$ sources on one playhead, subset $Q \subseteq \{1..n\}$ required and incomplete. Source $i$ has runway $r_i(t)$ (sim-ms) and demand density $\beta_i(x)$ = bytes per sim-ms of runway extension at frontier offset $x$ (known exactly from the directory). Runway dynamics under allocation: $\dot r_i = a_i(t)/\beta_i(r_i) - |s|\cdot\mathbf{1}[\text{playing}]$, where $a_i(t) \ge 0$ is the byte rate granted to source $i$, $\sum_i a_i \le C$. The clock's progress is gated by $\hat r = \min_{i \in Q} r_i$ (§11.2). Cache capacity makes over-buffering harmful; control writes (cap/weight changes) carry switching cost $\kappa$ (each may flush/replan a loader's prefetch horizon). _Decision variables:_ per source, a run-ahead cap $\lambda_i \in \mathbb{R}_{>0} \cup \{\infty\}$ (prefetch no further than $\lambda_i$ sim-ms past the playhead) and a scheduler weight $w_i > 0$, re-decided each evaluation epoch; laggard-set membership $\mathcal{L} \subseteq Q$. _Maximize_ the gated runway growth — lexicographic max-min:
$$\text{lexmax}\; \big(\min_{i \in Q} r_i(t),\; \text{2nd-min},\; \dots\big) \quad\text{equivalently minimize the laggard's time-to-gate } \inf\{t : \min_{i\in Q} r_i(t) \ge G\},$$
minus switching costs $\kappa \cdot \#\{\text{cap/weight writes}\}$. _Subject to:_

- _Capacity, DRR-realized:_ $\sum_i a_i(t) \le C$, with $a$ realized by a work-conserving weighted-fair (DRR) scheduler given $w$; over any backlogged interval $[T_1,T_2]$ the slots granted satisfy the DRR bound $\big|S_i-\frac{w_i}{\sum_j w_j}\,m\,(T_2-T_1)\big|\le O(\max_j w_j)$, with no starvation.
- _Dead-weight bound:_ $r_i \le \min_{j \in Q} r_j + \sigma$ for all $i$ — buffer past the min-gate + slack $\sigma$ cannot be consumed and feeds eviction of protected runway.
- _Bounded reweighting:_ $w_i \in [0.25\,b_i,\; 4\,b_i]$ around base weights $b_i$.
- _Optional sources:_ $j \notin Q$ get best-effort share, are cap-constrained by the same dead-weight bound, but never enter the min.
- _Cap semantics:_ caps bound run-_ahead_ only, never the resident window — each source's internal floor keeps its own gates satisfiable.
- _Bounded control traffic:_ writes only past a relative deadband or a to/from-null cap transition.

**Incumbent.** Upper level: `applyMultiSourceFairness` (playback-governor.ts:1727-1805) — laggard = min runway over incomplete required (:1754-1761); every non-laggard capped at laggard+slack, slack $\sigma = \max(\tau_w, 3000\,\mathrm{wall\text{-}ms})\times|s|$ (:1787); weight shed $w_i = b_i \cdot \mathrm{clamp}\big(\frac{\sigma + r_{lag}}{\sigma + r_i},\, 0.25,\, 4\big)$ (:1788-1802) — a smooth $1/x$ approximation of water-filling that lands the laggard exactly on base; hysteresis bands 0.25/0.5 of slack (:352-353, :1774-1786); write throttle at 20% relative change (:342, :1808-1845); one-shot restore on deactivation (:1855-1869). Lower level: DRR with per-round crediting, deficit clamp, and priority-within-eligibility at request-scheduler.ts:479-610 (creditRound :584-593, pickEligible :599-610; the docs record a prior broken variant that credited per-dispatch and starved light sources, :445-478); singleton wiring shared-scheduler.ts:57-93; archive-side sourceId/weight archive.ts:2948-2963 with a per-archive concurrency ceiling :2994-3003. Greedy per-epoch: the cap does most of the work, since DRR is work-conserving and weights only bind while leaders hold queued work; the shed formula is a fixed algebraic form, not the solution of the max-min program.

**Class.** Max-min fair allocation / progressive filling (poly-time exact via water-filling for the static problem); Generalized Processor Sharing realized as WFQ/DRR (Shreedhar & Varghese 1996: O(1) per packet, quantum-bounded unfairness); makespan-style laggard scheduling — feed-the-neediest is validated in the DASH fairness literature (shared global view measured +10% fairness / +8% efficiency vs independent greedy pools); online with switching costs (the throttle and hysteresis are a metrical-task-system-flavored answer). Shaka's cap-any-track-past-the-neediest is the direct SoTA analog.

**Knobs.** RUN_AHEAD_SLACK_WALL_MS=3000 (floored against runwayToleranceMs); weight clamp [0.25, 4]; FAIRNESS_RESEND_FRACTION=0.2; laggard enter/exit bands 0.25/0.5 of slack; `multiSourceFairness=true` kill switch; per-source base weight default 1, live-overridable via `setSourceWeight`; scheduler `maxRequests=24`.

**Gap.** Exact progressive filling would pour ALL surplus capacity into the laggard until it ties the next source (respecting the clamp), which the $1/x$ shed only approximates — and $\beta_i$ never enters the weights, so a laggard behind because its tiles are 10× heavier is under-served relative to true time-to-gate. Slack $\sigma$ is a constant, not derived from cache headroom or eviction pressure; DRR meters slots, not bytes, though group sizes vary by orders of magnitude. Measurable objective: laggard time-to-gate and `cacheStats.runwayEvictions` on multi-heavy composites.

### 11.4 Auto-speed selection: sustainable rate control on a shared link

**Setting.** Opt-in "Auto" speed. The governor computes the maximum sustainable speed from the COMBINED byte cost of an 8-s horizon across every required source (exact directory math for the current viewport). The shared-link rate comes from a wired `getThroughput()` (the §11.1 dual-EWMA), or is recovered as $\max_i(\mathrm{bytes}_i/\mathrm{eta}_i)$ when absent — never $\Sigma\mathrm{bytes}/\max_i\mathrm{eta}$, which double-counts the one pipe by ~$N$ (the multi-heavy racing-ahead bug). It returns Infinity when nothing is left to load and null when honest math is impossible (throughput unknown, or any required source has pending tiles of unknown byte size). The consumer owns policy: clamp, snap to a 13-step ladder, then asymmetric hysteresis — downshift immediately, upshift only on the periodic cadence past a deadband. IMPLEMENTED.

**Problem.** _Given:_ base rate $v_0$ (sim-ms per wall-ms, from §11.5); a discrete multiplier ladder $S = \{0.25, 0.5, \dots, 10\}$; a stochastic shared-link capacity $C(t)$ (bytes/wall-ms) observed via a noisy estimator $\widehat C(t)$; required sources with exact byte-demand functions $B_i(\Omega)$ = bytes not yet resident in sim-window $\Omega$ for the current viewport. At speed $m\,v_0$, the demanded byte rate over a lookahead of $H$ wall-ms is
$$D(m, t) \;=\; \frac{\sum_{i \in Q} B_i\big([p(t),\; p(t) + d\, H\, m\, v_0]\big)}{H}\quad\text{(bytes per wall-ms)}.$$
_Decision variables:_ a speed-multiplier policy $m(t) \in S$, adapted to observations, re-decided at evaluation epochs (periodic cadence + gate entries); abstention (null) is a valid output. _Maximize_ time-average speed minus stall and flapping penalties:
$$\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T m(t)\,dt\Big] \;-\; \mu \cdot \mathbb{E}[N_{\mathrm{stall}}] \;-\; \nu \cdot \mathbb{E}[N_{\mathrm{switch}}]$$
where $N_{\mathrm{switch}}$ counts speed changes (visible UI flapping is a real cost — speed is a user-facing semantic control). _Subject to:_

- _Sustainability (runway-queue stability):_ $D(m(t), t) \le C(t)$ in expectation — otherwise the runway drains and §11.1 stalls; the incumbent enforces the surrogate $D(m, t) \le \eta\, \widehat C(t)$ with safety $\eta = 0.7$.
- _One pipe:_ the required sources share a single link — the bound divides aggregate capacity across $\sum_i B_i$, never per-source full-pipe estimates (which run $\sim n\times$ too fast).
- _Honesty:_ if any $B_i$ is unknown (bytes-blind directory) or $\widehat C$ has no samples, no suggestion is made.
- _Ladder:_ $m(t) \in S$, $m \in [0.25, 10]$; upshifts forbidden on 'waiting' gate entries.

**Incumbent.** Two-stage greedy. (1) `getAutoSpeedSuggestion` (playback-governor.ts:1130-1225) computes the myopic max feasible speed $m^* = \eta\,\widehat{C} / (\Sigma B / H_{sim})$ with $H_{sim} = 8000\,|s|$ measured at the CURRENT speed (:1137; AUTO_SPEED_HORIZON_WALL_MS :321, AUTO_SPEED_SAFETY=0.7 :323); aggregate-rate recovery $\max_i(B_i/\eta_i)$ on the no-`getThroughput` path (:1159-1189, :1210-1220); Infinity when sumTiles=0 (:1192); bytes-blind/eta-blind abstention (:1178-1199). (2) `decideAutoSpeedMultiplier` (auto-speed.ts:69-98) clamps, snaps to SPEED_STEPS (:56-58), then hls.js-style asymmetric hysteresis: downshift immediate with no deadband, upshift cadence-only past a 0.25 relative deadband (:86-95). No memory beyond the previous multiplier, no stall-probability model; safety factor and deadband are fixed constants.

**Class.** The dual of ABR bitrate selection (choose consumption rate at fixed quality instead of quality at fixed rate): BOLA / RobustMPC / hls.js asymmetric factors map directly; hysteresis switching control with switching costs; constrained MDP with abstention. Online, stochastic capacity; the offline optimum with a known capacity trace is a shortest path over (time, speed) — polynomial.

**Knobs.** AUTO_SPEED_HORIZON_WALL_MS=8000; AUTO_SPEED_SAFETY=0.7; SPEED_STEPS 13-step ladder [0.25..10]; upshiftDeadband=0.25; min/maxMultiplier=0.25/10; evaluation phases 'cadence' vs 'waiting' (upshift only on cadence). `SttPlayer` converts to multiplier units (÷ baseRate, stt-player.ts:324-340).

**Gap.** Horizon byte density is sampled at the current speed only — at a higher candidate speed the window is longer and may cross a byte-density spike the myopic estimate misses; evaluating $D(m)$ per ladder step (or fixed-point iterating over $m$) would be exact and cheap given the directory oracle. $\eta=0.7$ and the 0.25 deadband encode risk tolerance that could derive from the EWMA estimator's variance. No lookahead beyond 8 s: a visible future heavy region (storm-cell entry) could pre-emptively downshift. Measurable objective: mean multiplier achieved × (1 − stall fraction) on throughput traces.

### 11.5 Archive-driven playback-parameter derivation

**Setting.** `resolvePlaybackParams` (packages/playback/src/derive-params.ts) is the single pure function turning archive metadata (timeRange, temporalBucketMs, styleHints.suggestedPlaybackSeconds) plus optional authored overrides into fully-resolved playback parameters: base speed, loader time window, resolved range, frame count, and an opt-in trail length. It reconciles authored time ranges against the archive extent (respect in-bounds subsets verbatim; clamp spills to the overlap with a warning; fall back to the extent on zero overlap — the render-blank hazard turned into a loud warning) and enforces the wake invariant. IMPLEMENTED; it replaced two historically divergent showcase formulas (÷30 vs ÷60).

**Problem.** _Given:_ archive facts — span $T$ ms, native temporal bucket $\Delta$ ms (the animation timestep; may be absent), frame count $K = \lceil T/\Delta \rceil$, optional build-time suggestion $P_{hint}$ (wall-s), per-window byte-cost density $\beta(t)$ (bytes per sim-ms, knowable from the directory but NOT an input today). Authored overrides: duration $P_o$, window $W_o$, wake length $\omega$, sub-range $[a_s, a_e]$. A perceptual legibility band $[f_{\min}, f_{\max}]$ (data-frames per wall-second a viewer can read); client budget $M$ (resident bytes) and link capacity $C$. _Decision variables:_ playback duration $P > 0$ wall-s (equivalently base speed $v = T/(1000\,P)$ sim-ms per wall-ms); loader window $W \in (0, T]$ sim-ms; resolved range $[t_s, t_e] \subseteq$ archive extent. _Minimize (multi-objective)_ deviation from perceptual and resource targets:
$$J = w_1\,\mathrm{dist}\Big(\frac{v}{\Delta} \cdot 1000,\; [f_{\min}, f_{\max}]\Big) \; + \; w_2\,\max\big(0,\; \bar\beta \, W - M\big) \; + \; w_3\,\max\big(0,\; \bar\beta\, v - C\big)$$
where $1000\,v/\Delta$ = native frames consumed per wall-second (too high = temporal aliasing/blur, too low = slideshow), $\bar\beta W$ = expected resident bytes, $\bar\beta v$ = steady-state fetch rate at 1×. _Subject to:_

- _Wake invariant (hard):_ $W \ge 2\omega$ when a wake is authored — the trailing half of a comet wake must not evict mid-render.
- _Window sanity:_ $W \le T$.
- _Range validity:_ $[t_s, t_e] \subseteq$ archive extent up to tolerance $tol = \max(\Delta,\, 0.01\,T,\, 1000\,\mathrm{ms})$; authored in-bounds subsets are respected exactly (editorial intent is a hard constraint, not a preference); out-of-bounds authored ranges clamp to the overlap with a warning.
- _Determinism:_ the map (metadata, overrides) $\to$ ($v, W, t_s, t_e$) is pure — identical inputs give identical outputs across every consumer.

**Incumbent.** Constant-based closed form with no density or cost input: $P = P_o \,\|\, P_{hint} \,\|\, 30$ s (derive-params.ts:369-374, DEFAULT_TARGET_PLAYBACK_SECONDS :148); $v = T/(1000P)$ (:374); $W = W_o \,\|\, \min(24\,\Delta, T) \,\|\, 86.4\mathrm{M}$ ms (:376-387); wake lift $W \leftarrow \max(W, 2\omega)$ with warning (:389-400); range reconciliation (:313-364); frameCount (:215-223); trail helper $\max(W/4, 30\,\mathrm{s})$ (:231-233). The frame-rate objective is only implicitly served: $P=30$ s ignores $K$ entirely, so a 10-frame dataset plays at 1 frame per 3 s and a 100k-frame one at 3,333 fps of data churn; 24 resident buckets is a constant unrelated to per-bucket byte cost.

**Class.** Constrained parameter fitting / rate selection against a rate-cost curve; the perceptual band makes it a chance-constrained choice of sampling rate for a signal of known timestep (Nyquist-flavored: the display cannot resolve finer than its refresh). Offline, deterministic, closed-form solvable once the objective is written down — the gap is that no objective is currently encoded, only constants.

**Knobs.** DEFAULT_TARGET_PLAYBACK_SECONDS=30; DEFAULT_TIME_WINDOW_BUCKETS=24; DEFAULT_TIME_WINDOW_MS=86,400,000 (24 h); MIN_TRAIL_LENGTH_MS=30,000; tol components ($\Delta$, 1% of span, 1000 ms floor); all overridable per dataset.

**Gap.** A principled fit would choose $P$ from $K$ (e.g. target 12–30 data-frames/wall-s ⇒ $P \in [K/30, K/12]$, projected onto the hint) and $W$ from directory byte density ($W = \arg\max\{W : \bar\beta W \le M\}$ capped at $24\Delta$) — both computable at build time and emittable via styleHints; the stt-optimize recommend/advisor pipeline already has the stats (see §12). Measurable objective: fraction of showcase datasets whose default playback needs a hand override (today many carry explicit targetPlaybackSeconds), plus buffer-stall rate at defaults.

### 11.6 Scrub interaction QoS: preview-tier degradation and settle-commit

**Setting.** Scrubbing is preview-vs-commit: `beginScrub` freezes the clock and broadcasts `setInteractive(true)` to every source; `scrubTo` moves the clock so resident tiles re-render at the new instant with ZERO fetches; `endScrub` clears the interactive bit FIRST (so a scrub-LOD loader restores its fine tier before the commit's readiness is measured — the G7 preview-only contract), then commits the release position as a real seek (flush stale prefetch, plain startup gate). A shared settle knob lets a UI commit a rested-but-held position early; a settle-commit memo avoids paying a second flush+gate on release-at-committed-position, and gates plus the escape hatch are fully suppressed under a held thumb. The downstream tier policy (`scrubLod` in the packages/core tileset) may serve a coarser spatial zoom — a drop through the existing zoomOverride path, usually an already-resident parent (see §8 — display-zoom selection with hysteresis, overzoom clamp, and scrub degrade) — and/or a coarser temporal-LOD bucket sized to timeline pixel width. STATUS: governor wiring and tileset policy are IMPLEMENTED and tested end-to-end but DEFAULT OFF with zero showcase call sites; the temporal axis is explicitly counted out until a genuinely byte-cheaper baked feature-_reducing_ tier exists (the current coarse-time aggregator re-buckets without reducing feature count — G5; see §4 — summary tiers & LOD).

**Problem.** _Given:_ a drag session in which the user sweeps a scrub position $\theta(t)$ over wall time $t \in [0, T_d]$ with unknown continuation (release time and final position unobserved in advance). A tier menu $\mathcal{Q}$: fine tier $q_0$ and coarse tiers $q_{k,B}$ (spatial zoom drop $k \in \{0..4\}$, temporal bucket $B$), each with per-instant serve cost $c(q, \theta)$ (bytes to fetch + decode if not resident; $\approx 0$ for a resident parent) and distortion $D(q) \ge 0$ ($D(q_0) = 0$). A latency target $\ell^*$ (ideally < one 60 Hz frame from a resident tile). Committing a seek costs a prefetch flush $\Phi$ (all in-flight lookahead discarded) plus gate re-arm; frames not reflecting the current $\theta(t)$ cost staleness $z(t)$. _Decision variables:_ (1) tier policy $q(t) \in \mathcal{Q}$ during the drag; (2) commit times $\{t_1, t_2, \dots\} \subseteq [0, T_d]$ at which a full seek (flush + fetch at fine tier) is issued for the then-current position; (3) the settle threshold $\tau_s$ if restricted to the rest-timer policy family. _Minimize_ expected interaction cost:
$$J = \mathbb{E}\Big[\int_0^{T_d} \big(\underbrace{\mathbf{1}[\text{latency}(t) > \ell^*]}_{\text{unresponsive frame}} + \; \mu\, D(q(t)) + \; \zeta\, z(t)\big)\,dt \;+\; \Phi \cdot |\{t_j\}| \;+\; \gamma_s\, L_{\mathrm{settle}}\Big]$$
where $L_{\mathrm{settle}}$ = wall time from release ($T_d$) until the fine tier is resident at the released position. _Subject to:_

- _Bounded distortion:_ $D(q(t)) \le D_{\max}$ — parent tiles within the fallback ceiling; temporal buckets no coarser than timeline pixel resolution, $B \gtrsim \mathrm{visibleSpan}/\mathrm{pixels}$ (the information-theoretic floor on useful resolution).
- _Preview-never-gates (G7, hard):_ the coarse tier must not enter readiness reporting — gates on release re-arm against the fine tier; no gate may start the clock while the thumb is held.
- _Restore invariant:_ $q(T_d^+) = q_0$ before the commit's readiness is measured.
- _Byte discipline:_ bytes fetched during the drag $\le$ the no-policy baseline (a motion tier that fetches more than full detail is worse than nothing).
- _Spatial ceiling:_ drop $k \le$ PARENT_FALLBACK_LEVELS $= 4$.

**Incumbent.** Governor side (IMPLEMENTED): binary interactive bit on begin/endScrub (playback-governor.ts:895-939); previews are free clock moves (:908-911); commits pay flush + plain gate (`commitSeek` :1442-1464); all gates and the escape hatch suppressed while scrubbing (:1527-1536); settle-commit memo (:925-938, `seekTo` :942-948); constant $\tau_s$ = seekSettleMs = 200 ms exposed as a shared knob (:163, :619) though the timer runs in the UI. Tier policy (IMPLEMENTED, UNCONSUMED): tileset `scrubLod` — spatialZoomDrop clamped to [0,4] via the existing zoomOverride path so the coarse target is usually a resident parent; temporal axis via `pickTemporalLodForZoom` with $B \approx$ visibleSpan/timelinePixelWidth; absent `scrubLod` is the kill switch, and no showcase renderer passes it (docs/roadmap/playback-and-loading.md:306-311). PLANNED / COUNTED-OUT: velocity-scaled degrade, scrub-velocity prefetch (ATLAS), a predictive Funkhouser–Séquin LOD budget controller, and the baked feature-reducing temporal tier that would make the temporal axis pay — all explicitly not scheduled (roadmap :313-348). The policy is binary (interactive yes/no) and static; every constant is hand-fixed.

**Class.** Rate–distortion / QoS adaptation under interaction (NLE proxies, HLS I-frame tracks, VTK DesiredUpdateRate); per-frame tier choice is a multiple-choice knapsack, so the general LOD-budget problem is NP-hard (Funkhouser–Séquin, SIGGRAPH'93); the settle debounce is online optimal stopping with unknown continuation (ski-rental-flavored: commit too early ⇒ wasted flush, too late ⇒ settle latency); the temporal bucket floor is a bounded-resolution sampling argument. Online and user-driven — adversarial rather than stochastic drag models are appropriate.

**Knobs.** seekSettleMs=200 ms (shared settle-commit debounce); scrubLod.spatialZoomDrop ∈ [0,4] (default absent = off); temporal bucket $B$ from timeline pixel width; PARENT_FALLBACK_LEVELS=4.

**Gap.** The objective's own metrics are specified but unmeasured — scrub time-to-first-pixel, fresh-frame fraction, bytes-during-scrub vs baseline, settle latency, pop count (roadmap :329-339). A measured controller could choose $k$ per drag velocity and commit opportunistically when the predicted remaining drag exceeds the flush payback (the directory ETA oracle of §11.1 prices this exactly). The 200 ms settle constant matches video-player practice but was never fit to STT's flush cost, which varies by orders of magnitude across datasets. If neither revival trigger fires by the next format revision the wiring is slated for deletion — and the keep-vs-delete decision itself hinges on running the specified measurement.

## 12. The auto-tuning meta-loop (stt-optimize)

Every subsystem in §1–§7 exposes build-time knobs; `stt-optimize` is the layer that chooses their values. It trades a small, bounded measurement budget (sample encodes through the production encoder, strided tile decodes) against recipe quality, and — through the recipes it emits — trades archive bytes, range-read cost, playback quality, and fidelity against one another under the project's no-thinning rule. Its verbs split into a pre-build search (`recommend`, backed by the analysis pass and four advisors) and post-build verification (`inspect`, `doctor`, `diff`, `order-audit`).

### 12.1 Sampled measurement, per-column cost attribution, and the diff regression gate

**Setting.** The tool's founding doctrine is _measure, don't model_: it began as an analytic predictor (feature size $= 100 + 16\cdot\text{verts} + 20\cdot\text{props}$, compressed $=$ uncompressed$/3$) and was replaced wholesale after measurement overturned expert plans twice — the "octree geometry" plan died when per-column stats showed AV LiDAR geometry was only 12.7% of bytes (id-hash 40%, raw-f64 z 38%), and measured wins across 21 datasets ranged 1.07×–21× with no formula predicting which lever pays (docs/roadmap/stt-packed-format-decisions.md:193,222,470). Today every verb reads from deterministic systematic samples pushed through the real `stt-core` encoder + zstd: the loader keeps every $\lceil N/5000\rceil$-th source row (loader.rs:158-161); `measure_sample` filters to the dominant geometry kind, builds one synthetic tile, and refuses below 24 usable features where framing noise dominates (measure.rs:29-36,136-181); `inspect --sample n` decodes every $\lceil M/n\rceil$-th directory entry, with `--sample 0` skipping decode entirely (directory stats are always exact and free; inspect.rs:243-317); the doctor decodes at most 8 strided tiles (doctor.rs:86). Sampling is deterministic, never random — reproducibility is contractual (inspect.rs:145-151), since ordering churn would rename content-addressed packs (see §13 — byte-reproducible builds). Per-column attribution re-encodes each decoded column _alone_ (single-field Arrow IPC + zstd-19, metadata stripped for byte-stability; inspect.rs:341-346), and the density model extrapolates archive size as features × measured bytes/feature, falling back to formula bytes ÷ FALLBACK_ZSTD_RATIO when unmeasured (density.rs:220-235, 16-21). `diff` (diff.rs:138-215) is the regression metric between builds: exact directory deltas plus sampled per-column deltas, with `compressed_bytes` gating `--fail-on-growth` (diff.rs:120-121).

**Problem.** Three coupled statements.

_Estimation._ Given $M$ tiles with unknown per-column costs, choose sample size $n$ and design $\mathcal{D}$ to

$$\min_{n,\,\mathcal{D}} \; \underbrace{n \cdot c_{\text{decode}}}_{\text{inspection cost}} \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \Pr\bigl(\lVert \widehat{\text{share}} - \text{share} \rVert_\infty > \varepsilon\bigr) \le \alpha, \qquad \mathcal{D} \text{ deterministic,}$$

where $\widehat{\text{share}}_c = \dfrac{\sum_{i \in \mathcal{D}} b_c(i)}{\sum_{c'} \sum_{i \in \mathcal{D}} b_{c'}(i)}$ and $b_c(i)$ = standalone re-encode bytes of column $c$ in tile $i$. For whole-archive size, the estimator is the linear form $\hat S = N \cdot Z(\text{sample tile})/n$ ($Z$ = compressed sample bytes), unbiased only if per-feature compressed cost is homogeneous and per-tile fixed overheads, cross-zoom replication, dedup, and dictionary effects all cancel.

_Attribution._ Let $v : 2^{[m]} \to \mathbb{N}$ be the compressed size of encoding column subset $A \subseteq [m]$ together ($v([m])$ = whole-tile bytes; $v$ is subadditive because zstd shares structure across columns). Find shares $\phi_1..\phi_m$ satisfying efficiency $\sum_j \phi_j = v([m])$ and fairness (symmetry, dummy, monotonicity) — the Shapley value $\phi_j = \sum_{A \subseteq [m]\setminus j} \frac{|A|!\,(m-|A|-1)!}{m!}\,[v(A \cup j) - v(A)]$ is the unique such allocation, at $O(2^m)$ oracle calls.

_Regression gate._ For builds $x_{\text{before}}, x_{\text{after}}$: fail iff $S_{\text{after}} - S_{\text{before}} > 0$ on the exact directory metric; per-column deltas $\Delta_c$ are decision support, flagged when either side sampled (a sampled $\Delta_c$ can be pure sampling disagreement).

**Incumbent.** Fixed-stride systematic sampling with hardcoded budgets at every tier (5000 rows, 24-feature floor, 8 doctor tiles, user-chosen $n$); estimator quality is never quantified — no variance, no confidence interval, no stratification, despite tile costs being strongly stratified by zoom (exact per-zoom stats are already computed). Systematic strides silently risk aliasing: entries are sorted by the space–time curve, so a stride ≈ #buckets can sample one bucket phase. Attribution is the singleton proxy $\phi_j^{\text{inc}} = v(\{j\}) / \sum_k v(\{k\})$ — it violates efficiency (the per-column sum exceeds whole-tile bytes, documented at inspect.rs:10-15, measure.rs:90-95) and misattributes whenever cross-column redundancy is asymmetric, so results are used strictly as shares, never wire accounting. The single-synthetic-tile design amortizes per-tile overhead over the whole sample rather than real tile occupancy, and mixed-geometry sources measure only the dominant kind (measure.rs:141-160). The diff gate metric itself is exact, so the gate is sound; only its per-column attribution lacks error bars.

**Class.** Survey sampling — systematic vs stratified designs, Neyman allocation (exact strata sizes are free from the directory); ratio estimation for shares; cooperative-game cost allocation (Shapley approximation; leave-one-out at $m{+}1$ calls is the standard cheap compromise); the diff gate is a paired before/after comparison whose sampled sub-metrics call for a significance test. All offline and deterministic.

**Knobs.** MAX_SAMPLE_FEATURES=5000 (loader.rs:85), MIN_MEASURE_FEATURES=24 (measure.rs:36), DOCTOR_SAMPLE_TILES=8; stride $= \lceil \text{total}/n\rceil$ (inspect.rs:149-151, loader.rs:161); COLUMN_ZSTD_LEVEL=19 fixed so shares are cross-dataset comparable (inspect.rs:30-33); FALLBACK_ZSTD_RATIO=3.0 (density.rs:16-21); MeasureSettings defaults {zstd 3, no quantization} (measure.rs:41-61); `inspect --sample 0` = directory-only fast mode.

**Gap.** (1) Stratify the decode sample by zoom (and time bucket) proportional to blob bytes — strata are enumerable for free, killing both aliasing risk and most estimator variance at the same $n$, still deterministic. (2) Report per-ColumnCost dispersion so the doctor's 3%-share threshold and diff's per-column deltas read against noise; a noise-aware tolerance could replace the binary `decode_sampled` caveat. (3) Leave-one-out or sampled-Shapley attribution restores efficiency at negligible cost; encoding $k$ synthetic tiles at the observed features-per-tile distribution corrects the overhead bias. A closed-loop `--auto-measure` (sampled slice → measure → adjust → full build) is explicitly DEFERRED until doctor/inspect prove demand, since it multiplies build cost. Measurable: share-estimate error vs exhaustive decode at fixed $n$; $|\hat S - S^\*|$ across the showcase fleet.

### 12.2 Build-recipe search: one-pass coordinate descent over the build-parameter space

**Setting.** The outer loop turns a source dataset into a recommended `stt-build` recipe. The analysis pass (crates/stt-optimize/src/analysis/) computes spatial/temporal/geometry/density summaries plus one measured sample encode (§12.1); four advisors then run in a fixed order — quantize, temporal, layout, budget (advisors/mod.rs:70-77) — each examining one knob group against the shared default-settings baseline and emitting `Advice{flag, value, why, projected, lossy, suggestion_only, confidence}` (advisors/mod.rs:43-67). `generate_recommendations` (recommend.rs:45-79) attaches the zoom range (§12.3) and temporal bucket (§2's incumbent) from the analyzers plus a scalar confidence built from fixed penalties (recommend.rs:82-116); `to_command` (recommend.rs:156-197) assembles a pasteable command containing only entries with `!lossy && !suggestion_only`. Lossy levers (quantization, budgets) and human-judgment levers (spatial ordering with playback tension, `--min-zoom-field`) are surfaced with measured projections but never auto-applied; `--auto` fills only flags the user did not pass, and `--auto=encode` applies only reversible levers. There is exactly one pass: no advisor sees another's output, and the composed recipe is never re-measured.

**Problem.** Given a dataset $D$ of $N$ features with bounds, timestamps, geometries and properties; a sample surrogate oracle $\widehat{S}(\theta)$ (encode a 5000-feature stride sample through the production encoder under settings $\theta$, measure compressed bytes); and a build-parameter space

$$\Theta = \underbrace{\{0..18\}^2}_{z_{\min},z_{\max}} \times \underbrace{\mathcal{B}}_{\text{bucket}} \times \underbrace{(\{\bot\}\cup L_q)}_{q_c} \times \underbrace{\{0,1\}}_{q_a} \times \underbrace{\{3,19\}}_{\ell} \times \underbrace{\{\text{sp},\text{tm},\text{h3}\}}_{o} \times \underbrace{\mathbb{N}}_{P} \times \underbrace{2^{\mathcal{L}}}_{T} \times \underbrace{\mathbb{N}\cup\{\bot\}}_{n_a} \times \underbrace{\{\bot,\text{h3},\text{qb}\}}_{s} \times \cdots$$

(zooms, temporal bucket, coordinate/attribute quantization, zstd level, blob ordering, pack size, temporal-LOD tiers, adaptive window, summary tier, feature caps, LOD field). Choose

$$\theta = (z_{\min}, z_{\max}, B, q_c, q_a, \ell, o, P, T, n_a, s, \text{minZoomField}, \text{maxTileFeatures}) \in \Theta,$$

plus the meta-decision per knob (auto-apply vs surface-only: the `lossy`, `suggestion_only` bits), to minimize the vector objective

$$\min_{\theta\in\Theta}\;\bigl(S(\theta),\; R(\theta),\; -Q(\theta),\; \Delta(\theta)\bigr)$$

where $S$ = archive wire bytes, $R$ = expected range-request cost over the reader workload, $Q$ = playback quality (buffered-range feasibility, watchable frame count), $\Delta$ = fidelity loss (dropped features, positional/attribute error). Subject to (hard, for anything auto-applied): $\Delta_{\text{count}}(\theta)=0$ (no thinning); $\Delta_{\text{pos}}(\theta)=0$ unless the user opts in; playback feasibility (ordering must not starve time-window reads); $z_{\min}\le z_{\max}$; every LOD tier $t\in T$ satisfies $t \equiv 0 \pmod B$. The code never scalarizes: lossy knobs ($q_c$, $q_a$, caps) enter only a Pareto-frontier report, never the $\arg\min$.

**Incumbent.** A single round of coordinate descent from the default recipe, with per-coordinate candidate sets of size 1–2 and the sample oracle: the quantize advisor trials one ladder precision against the default baseline (quantize.rs:40-61) — the incumbent of §3's quantization rate–distortion problem (the quarter-pixel-at-max-zoom pick); the layout advisor trials zstd 19 vs 3 (layout.rs:63-122) and simulates orderings (layout.rs:137-196) — the incumbent of §6's blob-ordering problem; the temporal advisor and budget advisor are threshold rules with no measurement at all (advisors/temporal.rs:56-66; budget.rs:67-73) — respectively §2's temporal-LOD incumbent and §12.4. `run_all` concatenates the marginals; `to_command` composes them without re-measuring the joint recipe. Cross-knob interactions survive only as prose: the LOD×adaptive clash appends a "consider one or the other first" sentence (advisors/temporal.rs:57-65), while bucket×ordering×zstd interactions are silently assumed away — the zstd-19 win is measured on _unquantized_ data, quantize wins at level 3, and the ordering simulation uses byte weights from the default encoding.

**Class.** Black-box / surrogate-assisted combinatorial optimization; one iteration of block-coordinate descent; multi-objective configuration tuning (database knob tuning, compiler flag selection — the OpenTuner/BOCA class, with this project holding the anti-model position: sampled exact evaluation over analytic cost models). The joint problem is NP-hard already via its ordering sub-problem; the code solves a separable relaxation in $O(\sum_k |C_k|)$ oracle calls.

**Knobs.** Advisor order fixed (quantize, temporal, layout, budget); confidence penalties −20 if $N{<}1000$, −10 if $N{<}10^4$, −15 if >3 density issues, −10 sparse, −10 very-complex geometry (recommend.rs:86-115); MAX_SAMPLE_FEATURES=5000 (loader.rs:85).

**Gap.** (1) No composed-recipe verification: one extra `measure_sample` at the assembled $\theta^\*$ would catch non-additive effects (e.g. zstd-19's shrink shrinking further after quantization, changing whether advice clears its 5% High bar). (2) No iteration: 2–3 rounds of coordinate descent with the same oracle would capture first-order interactions at trivial cost. (3) The confidence scalar is an ad-hoc penalty sum, not a calibrated posterior — measured-vs-unmeasured already carries the honest signal. Closed-loop `--auto-measure` remains DEFERRED (§12.1). Measurable: gap between recommended-recipe archive bytes and best-of-grid over the same knob set on fixture datasets.

### 12.3 Zoom-range selection (spatial resolution allocation)

**Setting.** `min_zoom`/`max_zoom` are CLI flags defaulting to 0/14 (tiler.rs:182-183), but when unset the build consumes the recommendation from `stt-optimize`'s spatial analysis (stt-build.rs:1677-1683) — the algorithm lives here. `spatial::analyze` scans every zoom 0..=18, bucketing feature centroids into $(x,y)$ tiles per zoom (spatial.rs:101-105, 130-173); `recommend_zoom_levels` (spatial.rs:187-236) picks the range, and the doctor re-checks the min side post-build (z0-bomb, doctor.rs:465-508). Because every zoom level re-ships the entire dataset (§1's full-pyramid replication), the range directly multiplies archive size (density.rs:115-152 sums per-zoom estimated bytes) — making this the single biggest size lever and, under the no-thinning rule, the sanctioned shrink mechanism ("zoom clamp").

**Problem.** Given $N$ feature centroids with projected bounding-box area $A$ (m²), mean latitude $\varphi$, world width $W = 40{,}075{,}017$ m, per-zoom occupancy statistics $\{(\text{occ}_z, \bar f_z)\}_{z=0}^{18}$ ($\text{occ}_z$ = occupied tiles, $\bar f_z$ = mean features per occupied tile), and a query workload $q$: choose $z_{\min}, z_{\max} \in \{0,\dots,18\}$, $z_{\min}\le z_{\max}$, to

$$\min_{z_{\min}, z_{\max}}\; \sum_{z=z_{\min}}^{z_{\max}} c(z) \;+\; \lambda\, \mathbb{E}_{q}\bigl[\mathrm{fetch\_cost}(q \mid z_{\min}, z_{\max})\bigr]$$

where $c(z) \approx$ bytes to re-ship the dataset at zoom $z$, subject to: (i) _detail preservation_ — tile ground size at $z_{\max}$ at most the typical inter-feature spacing $s$, i.e. $\dfrac{W\cos\varphi}{2^{z_{\max}}} \le s$ with $s=\sqrt{A/N}$ (uniform-packing proxy for median nearest-neighbour distance); (ii) _overview utility_ — $\bar f_{z_{\min}} \ge 2$ (an overview level must aggregate); (iii) _no dead depth_ — $\text{occ}_{z_{\max}}>0 \wedge \bar f_{z_{\max}} \ge 1$; (iv) _no dead shallowness_ (doctor) — $z_{\min} \ge \bigl\lceil \log_2 (360/\max(\text{lon-ext},\text{lat-ext})) \bigr\rceil$ clamped to $[4, z_{\max}{-}1]$ when the extent is under 2°. Multi-objective in practice (size vs deep-zoom fidelity); the incumbent collapses the objective entirely into the constraints.

**Incumbent.** Closed-form solve of constraint (i) at equality — $z_{\max}=\mathrm{round}\bigl(\log_2\frac{W\cos\varphi}{\sqrt{A/N}}\bigr)$, the Tippecanoe `-zg` analogue (spatial.rs:265-312) — then a linear occupancy walk enforcing (iii) (spatial.rs:216-226); $z_{\min}$ from a linear walk enforcing (ii) (spatial.rs:195-204); $z_{\min} > z_{\max}$ resets $z_{\min}=0$ (spatial.rs:229-231). This is a global-average model: for clustered data $\sqrt{A/N}$ underestimates local spacing (acknowledged at spatial.rs:249-251), corrected only coarsely by the clamp. No cost term is ever evaluated — the range is never shrunk for size; that pressure surfaces only later as density issues (density.rs:309-320) and the doctor's z0-bomb.

**Class.** Quantizer/grid resolution selection; histogram bin-width choice (Freedman–Diaconis-style closed forms vs data-driven risk minimization); Tippecanoe `-zg`. Offline, deterministic, polynomial — the candidate grid is 19×19, so exhaustive search is trivial; costing each candidate is the hard part.

**Knobs.** Spacing proxy $s=\sqrt{A/N}$; degenerate-axis floor = one tile at z18 (spatial.rs:287-289); rounding of $\log_2(W\cos\varphi/s)$ (spatial.rs:301-311); avg-feature thresholds 2.0/1.0; MAX_SUPPORTED_ZOOM=18 (spatial.rs:178); doctor Z0_MIN_ZOOM=4, Z0_EXTENT_DEG=2°; `--min-zoom`/`--max-zoom` defaults 0/14.

**Gap.** The problem is small enough to solve near-exactly: build a per-zoom byte model from the zoom-coverage scan (density.rs already models per-zoom cost) plus a resolution-loss penalty, and pick the Pareto point directly instead of constraint extremes. A sampled nearest-neighbour median (or per-cluster k-NN-distance quantile) in place of $\sqrt{A/N}$ would fix the clustered-data bias without relying on the clamp. Measurable: archive bytes at equal rendered detail (features distinguishable at max zoom).

### 12.4 Overview-tier triggering and load shedding under the no-thinning constraint

**Setting.** The budget advisor decides how overloaded overview tiles become servable without dropping data — the auto-tuning incumbent for §4's summary-tier mechanics (granularity mapping and aggregate materialization live there). The density model predicts per-$(x,y,t)$ loads at every recommended zoom with two skew statistics: p95 features/tile and the top-1%-of-cells feature share (density.rs:157-250, 200-214). `summary_tier_advice` (budget.rs:143-222) recommends an _additive_ aggregated tier via two triggers — the skew trigger was added because the original average-based rule missed hotspot data entirely (empty cells drag the average down). Scheme: H3 for Localized distributions, Quadbin otherwise. `hotspot_lod_advice` (budget.rs:231-271) proposes a per-feature zoom floor `--min-zoom-field` when one hotspot holds >50% of features and the sample carries a plausible low-cardinality rank column (budget.rs:290-360) — always suggestion_only. Only as explicit lossy opt-in does `oversized_tile_advice` (budget.rs:78-132) surface `--maximum-tile-features 10000`, non-destructive alternatives listed first. The doctor mirrors both post-build (doctor.rs:540-612).

**Problem.** Given predicted per-cell loads $\{|c| : c \in \text{cells}(z)\}$ at each zoom, total features $N$, overview zoom $z_{\min}$, spatial class $\chi$, hotspot shares, and sampled property columns: choose $(s, \sigma, \phi, \beta)$ — summary tier on/off $s \in \{0,1\}$ with scheme $\sigma \in \{\text{h3}, \text{quadbin}\}$; LOD-floor column $\phi \in \text{cols} \cup \{\bot\}$ (low-rank features get $z_{\text{floor}}(f) > z_{\min}$); lossy cap $\beta \in \mathbb{N} \cup \{\bot\}$ — to

$$\min\; \max_{c \,\in\, \text{cells}(z_{\min})} \text{serveCost}(c \mid s, \sigma, \phi, \beta) \;+\; \mu \cdot \text{addedTierBytes}(s, \sigma)$$

(the worst overview tile must be fetchable/renderable; a summary tier replaces $|c|$ raw points with $O(\text{cells within } c)$ aggregates), subject to the lexicographic no-thinning order: zero-data-loss solutions strictly dominate — $\beta \ne \bot$ is admissible only by explicit user opt-in (its per-tile enforcement is §4's two-constraint knapsack); the floor $\phi$ drops nothing but makes features invisible below their floor (a visibility cost the advice must state); $\phi$ must reference a numeric rank column (categorical candidates need a prior data transform, hence suggestion_only).

**Incumbent.** Threshold-rule classification with no cost evaluation: the two summary triggers are hard conjunctions of constants — dense-uniform ($N > 10^6 \wedge \bar f_{z_{\min}} > 5000$) or hotspot-skewed ($N > 10^5 \wedge \text{top1\%share} \ge 0.6 \wedge \max|c| > 10^4$, budget.rs:158-166); scheme choice is a single `if` on the spatial class (budget.rs:170-174); the LOD candidate is a deterministic sort (numeric first, lowest cardinality, name — budget.rs:353-359) with no estimate of deferred features or hotspot relief; $\beta$ is always exactly 10000. Nothing is measured against the objective — `addedTierBytes` is never computed even though the density model could.

**Class.** Load shedding / admission control under a fairness (no-drop) constraint; histogram and sketch design (replace raw points with aggregates once cardinality exceeds display resolution — datashading, server-side binning); the top-1%-share trigger is Gini/heavy-hitter detection (noted in code as Sedona's "cluster the partitioner can't balance" signal, budget.rs:37-39); LOD floors ≈ priority-based generalization (Tippecanoe drop-rate by attribute). Deterministic, offline, polynomial.

**Knobs.** OVERSIZED_TILE_FEATURES=10000, SUMMARY_TIER_MIN_FEATURES=1M, SUMMARY_TIER_HEAVY_AVG=5000, SUMMARY_TIER_SKEW_SHARE=0.60, SUMMARY_TIER_SKEW_MIN_FEATURES=100k, HOTSPOT_SHARE=0.5, MAX_LOD_CANDIDATE_CARDINALITY=16, SMALL_INT_MAX_ABS=32 (budget.rs:23-60); doctor mirrors SUMMARY_FEATURE_FLOOR=1M, OVERSIZED_BLOB_BYTES=1 MiB (doctor.rs:97-100).

**Gap.** (1) Compute the objective: predicted peak overview-tile bytes with/without the tier and the tier's added bytes are both derivable from the existing density model + measured bytes/feature — turning triggers into a measured cost comparison (letting the tier fire between the 100k/1M floors when it pays, or stay off above them when it doesn't). (2) Choose the summary cell resolution (currently implicit in the builder) jointly with $z_{\min}$. (3) Simulate the floor assignment for $\phi$ (per-rank feature counts are known from the sample) and report the deferred share instead of Low-confidence prose. Measurable: peak overview tile bytes and first-paint fetch size.

### 12.5 Doctor: post-build feasibility checking with projected-win-ranked repair moves

**Setting.** `doctor()` (doctor.rs:163-194) runs 7 rules over a built packed tileset plus its inspect report: raw-f64-column, expensive-feature-ids, dead-columns, z0-bomb, unpaged-large, oversized-blobs, missing-summary-tier. Directory rules read only free directory stats; dead-columns and point-dominance decode at most 8 stride-sampled tiles (doctor.rs:86, 245-311). Each finding carries this tileset's _measured_ numbers, a preference-ordered remediation list of concrete builder flags (structural fixes first, lossy budgets last with a DROP warning — doctor.rs:564-574), and where derivable a projected win computed from measured column shares — never a re-encode, every projection labeled "(estimated from measured column costs)" (doctor.rs:34-36). Guards prevent false positives: the id-cost rule is suppressed under 256 features/tile where IPC framing dominates (doctor.rs:395-400); sampled-evidence rules self-declare. The raw-f64 rule is the post-build echo of §3's quantization problem: flagged columns project a win of share × 60% assumed shrink (doctor.rs:321-383).

**Problem.** Given a built archive $x$ with directory entries $E$, metadata, inspect report (per-zoom stats, per-column costs $\{(\text{share}_c, \text{bpf}_c, \text{note}_c)\}$, total $S$), and a sampled decode $\tilde{x}$: define the feasibility (health) set $\mathcal{H} = \bigcap_{r=1}^{7} \{x : g_r(x) \le 0\}$ with constraints —
$g_1$: $\sum_{c \in F_{\text{f64}}} \text{share}_c = 0$ where $F_{\text{f64}} = \{c \notin \text{reserved}: \text{note}_c = \text{plain-f64} \wedge \text{share}_c \ge 0.03\}$;
$g_2$: $\text{bpf}_{id} \le 4$ (given $\ge 256$ features/decoded tile);
$g_3$: no property column constant/all-null across $>1$ sampled tiles;
$g_4$: $z_{\min} > 4 \vee \text{extent} \ge 2° \vee z_{\min} \ge \text{floor}(\text{extent})$ where $\text{floor} = \mathrm{clamp}(\lceil \log_2 (360/\text{ext}) \rceil, 4, z_{\max}{-}1)$;
$g_5$: paged directory $\vee\ |E| \le 10^4$;
$g_6$: $\max_e \text{len}(e) \le 1$ MiB;
$g_7$: summary tier present $\vee$ features $\le 10^6$ $\vee$ points $< 50\%$ of sampled rows.
Output the violated set ranked by $(\text{severity}_r, \text{code}_r)$, each with a repair move $\Delta\theta_r$ (builder flags) and projected improvement $\widehat{\Delta S}_r$ — e.g. $\widehat{\Delta S}_1 = S \cdot 0.6 \sum_{c \in F_{\text{f64}}} \text{share}_c$; severity is thresholded on measured shares (Critical iff $\sum \text{share}_c \ge 0.5$). Structurally this is one step of guided local search over build-parameter space, evaluated on the _real_ objective (the built archive) rather than the sample surrogate.

**Incumbent.** Fixed rule table with hand-tuned constants in one pass (doctor.rs:163-194): hard thresholds on measured statistics, the fixed 0.6 shrink prior with linear share arithmetic, a 2-level severity step, deterministic (severity, code, message) sort. The z0-bomb rule exactly solves its embedded 1-D subproblem (minimal zoom floor covering the bounds with ~2–8 tiles, doctor.rs:473-479). No rule interaction handling — raising `--min-zoom` per z0-bomb also removes some oversized blobs, and the two projections would double-count — and no total-repair-value ordering. The doctor chooses nothing itself: it emits candidate moves $\{\Delta\theta_r\}$ for the user/build loop.

**Class.** Constraint checking / lint-rule systems; anomaly detection with fixed thresholds vs learned baselines; the ranked-remediation output is one iteration of best-improvement local search with independent first-order improvement estimates — the same structure as database index-advisor "what-if" analysis, which the projections mimic without re-encoding.

**Knobs.** RAW_F64_MIN_SHARE=0.03 / CRITICAL=0.5 / SHRINK=0.6; ID_BPF_INFO=4 / WARN=6 / MIN_FEATURES_PER_TILE=256; DOCTOR_SAMPLE_TILES=8; Z0_MIN_ZOOM=4, Z0_EXTENT_DEG=2.0; UNPAGED_TILE_LIMIT=10000; OVERSIZED_BLOB_BYTES=1 MiB; SUMMARY_FEATURE_FLOOR=1M (doctor.rs:67-100). Hard design constraints: never re-encode (doctor.rs:8-13); deterministic ordering (doctor.rs:187-192); lossy remediations always last and warned (doctor.rs:564-574); RESERVED_COLUMNS must track the encoder's reserved set or property rules misattribute (doctor.rs:44-63).

**Gap.** (1) Rank by projected bytes reclaimed rather than severity/code, and fuse overlapping projections into a joint what-if per remediation set. (2) The 0.6 shrink prior ("measured passes landed 50–75%") is replaceable with real evidence: re-encoding one flagged column from the already-decoded sample tiles costs milliseconds — the same standard the advisors hold themselves to. (3) Thresholds like the ID_BPF ceilings and the 3% share floor are dataset-size invariant; scaling by archive size would stop tiny archives tripping noise rules. Measurable: bytes reclaimed per applied finding vs projected.

## 13. Cross-cutting constraints & rejected designs

Where §1–§12 each own a subsystem's decisions, this section owns the constraints that bind _all_ of them: byte-determinism of the build, the conformance rule set that makes format validity executable, and the no-thinning doctrine that partitions every size lever into what a solver may touch. It also treats the measurement corpus in `docs/roadmap/measurements-*.md` as what it formally is — a set of exact samples of the objective surfaces every other section optimizes over — and closes the document with the recurring problem shapes and the shared constraint spine.

Several concepts surveyed by this section's source read were folded into their home subsystems and appear here only as cross-references: cross-timestep delta chains (REJECTED) and content-addressed dedup under random-access constraints (see §6 — dedup vs locality); blob linear ordering and pack cutting under the blended bytes + reads×gap cost (see §6); directory page partitioning and pruning-descriptor design (see §7); schema-template hoisting and the DEFERRED dataset-global dictionary hoist (see §3); the lossless LOD pyramid and the counted-out $O(N)$ home-zoom decomposition (see §1, with tier mechanics in §4); the "measure, don't model" doctrine and the summary-tier skew oracle (see §12).

### 13.1 Byte-reproducible deterministic builds

**Setting.** The immutable-CDN economics require that an identical rebuild re-derive identical content addresses, so a re-sync uploads nothing and unchanged data never invalidates the edge — reproducibility is a spec-level design decision (D6), not an implementation nicety.
