Two layers implement it: blob and directory-entry order carry a total tiebreak (z, x, y, time_start, temporal_bucket_ms) appended to the curve key, making pack layout independent of input arrival order and thread scheduling (crates/stt-core/src/pack/mod.rs:875-977); and payload metadata is assembled from sorted BTreeMaps, with Arrow ≥ 59 serializing IPC metadata in stable key order, closing the per-process HashMap-order gap that existed under Arrow 54 (docs/spec/stt-packed-format.md:926-938). Determinism dictates micro-decisions — degenerate vertex-value-quant affines are pinned ({o:0, s:1} for all-sentinel columns, {o:min, s:1} for constant columns) purely so bytes stay reproducible (spec §5.2.6), and golden-fixture generators use SpatialMajor rather than Auto so hashes are stable. The deploy side extends the invariant in time: deploys are additive, and origin GC deletes an object only when it is unreferenced _and_ older than a retention window (scripts/r2-sync.sh, default 7 days, one-deploy grace, `--no-prune` for full republishes).

**Problem.** Given a logical dataset $D$ (features + build flags), a build function $\mathrm{Build}(D, \pi, \sigma)$ potentially dependent on input permutation $\pi$ and execution schedule $\sigma$, content addressing $h$ (blake3-128), an object store holding version $V_{old}$, and per-byte upload/cache-invalidation cost.

Hard constraint (determinism):
$$\forall \pi, \pi', \sigma, \sigma': \quad \mathrm{Build}(D, \pi, \sigma) = \mathrm{Build}(D, \pi', \sigma') \quad \text{(byte-for-byte, per object)}$$
including across processes and machines — no hash-seed, iteration-order, or float-nondeterminism dependence.

Objective _enabled_ by the constraint — incremental deploy cost between versions $D \to D'$:
$$\min \sum_{o \,\in\, O(D') \setminus O(D)} |o| \qquad \text{where } O(\cdot) = \{h(b) : b \in \mathrm{Build}(\cdot)\}$$
Determinism makes this exactly the logically-changed set: unchanged tiles re-derive the same $h$, so re-sync skips them, and identical tiles across datasets/versions share one physical object.

Deploy-consistency constraint (GC safety): an object $o$ may be deleted at time $\tau$ only if
$$o \notin \mathrm{refs}(M_{cur}) \;\wedge\; \tau - \mathrm{birth}(o) > T_{ttl} + T_{session}$$
where $M_{cur}$ is the current manifest, $T_{ttl}$ the maximum manifest cache TTL, and $T_{session}$ the longest expected open session — cached manifests and live sessions keep resolving old pack names. Decision variables: the canonical total order on blobs/entries (tiebreak key), the canonical serialization of every map-shaped structure (sorted keys, pinned degenerate values), and the retention window $T$ with a prune policy per deploy class (grace / `--min-age` / `--no-prune`). Correctness never depends on the objective — a full rebuild is self-consistent and the manifest swap atomic; this is a pure cache-efficiency objective sitting on a hard constraint. The constraint also underwrites §12's measurement validity: sample-encode evaluations $\hat{J}(\theta)$ are noise-free and replicable only because the encoder is deterministic, and the acceptance test for the 2026-07-28 ingest refactor was literally byte-identical archives (`diff -r`, same SHA over every file).

**Incumbent.** Not a search — a constraint _discipline_: total-tiebreak sort keys at PackWriter finalize (pack/mod.rs:875-977; spec §5 step 1, §7-D6), sorted-BTreeMap metadata assembly, pinned degenerate affines, guarded by the active test `same_tile_encodes_byte_identically` (crates/stt-core/tests/reproducible_build.rs:180 — formerly an `#[ignore]`d canary under Arrow 54) and the v2_golden byte pin (crates/stt-core/tests/v2_golden.rs). GC/grace lives in scripts/r2-sync.sh per the spec §2 retention contract. Its historical failures price the constraint: the FNV-1a synthetic-id migration and the Arrow-54 metadata-order gap each forced a one-time full fleet re-upload — the 2026-07-26 break cost 29.3 GiB, 1,324 objects, 100% re-upload.

**Class.** Reproducible builds (Nix/Bazel, reproducible-builds.org); canonicalization (XML C14N, canonical CBOR); Merkle/CAS dedup; deterministic parallel reduction; cache-coherent GC with lease/retention semantics, analogous to grace periods in distributed GC. The GC condition is a safety constraint; choosing $T$ trades storage cost against stale-session breakage risk.

**Knobs.** `R2_PRUNE_RETENTION` (default 7 d), `--no-prune` / `--prune-now`, pack-manifest TTL (max-age 60–14400 s observed), immutable objects at max-age 31536000.

**Gap.** PLANNED/open: choose the retention window from measured session-length and manifest-TTL distributions ($P(\text{broken session}) < \epsilon$) instead of a 7-day constant; and formalize deploy ordering (packs → frontend → manifests, the 15-second-exposure procedure in shipping.md) as a two-phase-commit-like protocol with a provable zero-404 window. Golden byte pins may change only with intentional, reviewed encoder changes — the pin is the constraint's regression oracle.

### 13.2 The conformance rule set as the constraint system defining format validity

**Setting.** `stt-validate` plus docs/spec/conformance.md make format validity an executable constraint system, and its rules are precisely the feasible-region boundaries of the encoder problems in §3–§6. The centerpiece is the drift classifier (crates/spatiotemporal-tiles/src/bin/stt-validate/schema.rs:114; the asymmetric drift rule at docs/spec/conformance.md:203): it hard-fails a type-family or column-set change across tiles of one column, sanctioning _only_ integer-width and optional-reserved-presence variation. This rule forced a real revert: the first attribute-quantizer refusal keyed off a tile's sample (span + median-relative-error) — so one column shipped UInt16 in one tile and Float64 in another, and the validator correctly hard-failed the archive; the shipped rule is a single magnitude test at `i32::MAX` (crates/stt-core/src/arrow_tile/quantize.rs:282-286), a property of the column's _domain_, so every tile decides identically. Other executable rules: descriptor covering (verify_paged_structure checks every page descriptor covers its leaf and cross-page key order is monotonic), frame-only encodings (FrameOnlyEncodings, crates/stt-core/src/arrow_tile/encode.rs:141), must-understand capability gating via manifest.capabilities, and canonical metadata key order as a SHOULD (conformance.md:370).

**Problem.** Two layers. _(a) Validity as constraint satisfaction._ Given an archive $A = (M, \Delta, \{C_k\})$ — manifest $M$, directory $\Delta$ with pages $p$ and descriptors $D(p)$, tiles $T$ with per-column encodings $e_{T,c}$ — and domain-level column statistics $\theta_c$, $A$ is valid iff it satisfies the MUST set:

- (Invariance / drift) for every column $c$ and tiles $T_1, T_2$: $\mathrm{family}(e_{T_1,c}) = \mathrm{family}(e_{T_2,c})$, with variation permitted only inside the sanctioned set $V = \{\text{integer width},\ \text{optional-reserved presence}\}$. Equivalently: every shape-class decision must factor through domain statistics, $\mathrm{family}(e_{T,c}) = g(\theta_c)$, never through the tile's sample $S_{T,c}$;
- (Capability closure) every re-typing family used anywhere appears in $\mathrm{capabilities}(M)$; a reader refuses on unknown must-understand members — silent misdecode converted to loud refusal;
- (Covering) $\forall p: D(p) \supseteq \bigcup_{e \in p} \big(b(e), z(e), [\tau^-_e, \tau^+_e]\big)$ — descriptors admit false positives only, never false negatives (see §7 for the page-partitioning problem this constrains);
- (Frame-only) encodings requiring TILE_META ($st$/$et$/$vq$) appear only inside frames that carry it;
- (Exactness) default encodings are bijective on their values; the only lossy members are opt-in and declared (see 13.3);
- (Template budget) distinct realized schemas per dataset stay in single digits, $|\{\mathrm{schema}(T)\}| \le K$ (see §3).

_(b) Rule-set design as the meta-problem._ Given an encoder adaptivity menu (per-tile encoding choices $E_c$, §3) and a population of independently-written readers, choose the rule set $R = (R_{MUST}, R_{SHOULD}, V)$ to maximize the bytes recoverable by per-tile adaptivity, subject to: (i) every conforming reader decodes every conforming archive (no coordination beyond $R$); (ii) every violation of $R_{MUST}$ is detectable by a validator from the archive alone (executability); (iii) forward compatibility is additive, via must-understand capabilities and reserved descriptor kinds. The sanctioned set $V$ is the decision variable: enlarging $V$ buys compression and costs portability — MLT's near-failure from an uncapped codec zoo is the recorded boundary lesson (see §3).

**Incumbent.** A fixed, hand-chosen $V$ enforced by the drift classifier (schema.rs:114), covering checks in verify_paged_structure, the frame-only guard (encode.rs:141), and the domain-magnitude quantizer rule (quantize.rs:282-286) that replaced the sample-dependent one. The rule set is hardcoded per spec version; the validator checks structure, not semantics.

**Class.** Constraint satisfaction with an invariance flavor: the drift rule is exactly "the decision must be invariant to which rows the tile sampled" — constrained classification under distribution shift. The meta-problem is language/protocol design under a portability budget (must-understand capability negotiation, cap-the-toolbox).

**Knobs.** Effectively none at runtime — validity is not tunable. The evolution levers are structural: the sanctioned-variation set $V$, the template budget $K$, reserved descriptor kinds (kind 1 = per-page column stats, additive), and capability registration for any new re-typing family.

**Gap.** The constraint system is sound but incomplete: recorded project history shows a v1→v2 `reoptimize` defect that flattened and scrambled coordinates in 106 AV archives _passed_ `stt-validate` — the catch came from comparing `stt-optimize export` bboxes. A principled extension would add semantic invariants (bbox/statistical fingerprints of decoded content) as conformance checks, closing the gap between structural validity and correctness. Smaller: per-tile width/presence flips generate benign-but-noisy drift warnings; the PLANNED dataset-global attribute-range pin (see §3) would eliminate the noise by pinning each column's leaf and affine once.

### 13.3 The no-thinning doctrine as a lexicographic ordering on size levers

**Setting.** The project's binding product invariant: the default build is comprehensive — no feature is ever dropped to save bytes. Sanctioned size levers are the zoom-range clamp, temporal bucketing, and lossless byte levers; summary tiles ship _in addition to_ the raw tier, never instead of it (advisor at crates/stt-optimize/src/advisors/budget.rs:138-178); temporal-LOD tiers are strict re-bucketings holding exactly the union of their base buckets, and any non-lossless tier must be a DECLARED variant, never silent (time-model normative content contract, docs/spec/time-model.md:153,166). A per-tile feature-drop budget exists in the code but is default-off (crates/stt-build/src/build_options.rs:299-319, enforced at tiler.rs:1499-1513 — the knapsack itself is §4's problem). On the tuning side, `--auto` applies only reversible levers; lossy quantization is suggested loudly, never silently applied; thinning and budgets are never auto-applied (see §12).

**Problem.** Partition the build-lever space $\Theta$ into classes:
$$\Theta = \Theta_0 \sqcup \Theta_1 \sqcup \Theta_2 \sqcup \Theta_\infty$$
where $\Theta_0$ = reversible/lossless levers (zoom clamp, temporal bucket width $\Delta$, zstd level, blob ordering, pack size, seq-id remap, compact times, alignment); $\Theta_1$ = lossy opt-in levers (coordinate world-grid, vertex-value quantization, fixed-point attribute quantization, vertex-time precision), each with a user gate $u_c \in \{0,1\}$; $\Theta_2$ = declared additive products (summary tier, temporal-LOD tiers; reduced M4-style tiers — PLANNED); $\Theta_\infty$ = thinning levers (feature-drop budgets).

Let $A(\theta)$ = archive bytes, $Q(\theta)$ = expected query cost, and $d(\theta; D)$ = reconstruction distortion with $d = 0$ iff only $\Theta_0$ deviates from exactness-preserving values. The doctrine is the lexicographic program:
$$\min_{\theta \in \Theta} \big(A(\theta),\, Q(\theta)\big) \quad \text{subject to}$$

- (Completeness, hard, first priority) for every query $q = (V, z, W)$: $\mathrm{decode}(\mathrm{fetch}(q)) \supseteq \{f : f \cap (V, W) \neq \emptyset\}$ with feature identity preserved across tiers — the base tier is always lossless;
- (Lossy gate) $d(\theta) > 0$ only where $u_c = 1$ for each contributing lever $c$, and every such lever is echoed loudly in output (opt-in, never silent);
- (Additivity) $\Theta_2$ products only add bytes: $\mathrm{content}(\text{coarse tier}) = \bigcup \mathrm{content}(\text{base buckets})$; any reduced tier must be declared in metadata;
- (Policy infeasibility) $\theta_{\Theta_\infty} = \mathbf{0}$ in every automated problem — thinning is excluded from the feasible set by policy, not weighted against;
- (Automation boundary) the auto-tuner's feasible set is $\Theta_{auto} = \Theta_0$ with user-set coordinates frozen; $\Theta_{auto} \cap \Theta_1 = \emptyset$.

The ordering is lexicographic, not weighted: completeness dominates bytes at any ratio; enlarging the feasible set to $\Theta_0 \cup \Theta_1$ is a human decision that no byte saving can trigger automatically.

**Incumbent.** The doctrine is implemented as a feasibility partition rather than penalty weights: `--auto=encode` applies exactly the reversible set (seq ids, zstd level, blob ordering, pack size, vertex-time precision); quantization lives behind explicit flags with echoed precisions; the summary advisor only ever adds a tier (budget.rs:138-178); the drop budget defaults off (build_options.rs:299-319). Hardcoded where the class boundaries themselves sit — e.g. compact times ride in $\Theta_0$ because they are exact by construction, despite costing +3.4% wire (see 13.4).

**Class.** Lexicographic multi-objective optimization; rate–distortion with a hard $D = 0$ constraint (lossless coding) plus a human-gated relaxation; policy-constrained algorithm configuration. The M4 bound (Jugel et al., VLDB 2014 — min/max/first/last per pixel column is sufficient _and_ necessary for pixel-identical line rendering, a hard $4w$ bound) marks the frontier of reduction that remains compatible with the doctrine via declared tiers.

**Knobs.** `--temporal-bucket` (default 1 h), min/max zoom clamps, `--summary-tier {h3|quadbin}` + `--summary-sub-buckets` (≤ 32), `--temporal-lod <widths>`, `--quantize-*` family (all opt-in), `--auto` / `--auto=encode` / `--auto=encode+quantize`, feature-drop budget (default off).

**Gap.** The doctrine's cost is integer factors of storage — full per-zoom replication is up to 11× on earthquakes, and M4 tiers could cut zoomed-out playback bytes up to 100× — and every recorded escape stays _inside_ the constraint set because it is exact or declared: the home-zoom decomposition (PLANNED/counted-out, see §1), M4/MinMaxLTTB declared reduced tiers (PLANNED, trigger: base-tier bandwidth-bound wide-window playback), and the joint space×time LOD policy (PLANNED; the 2026-07-10 sweep found no published precedent). REJECTED permanently: silent thinning in any automated path — the doctrine is the reason $\Theta_\infty$ appears in no other section's formulation.

### 13.4 The measurement corpus as empirical samples of the objective surfaces

**Setting.** `docs/roadmap/measurements-*.md` (e.g. measurements-2026-07.md:28,148) is the project's empirical record: per-lever A/Bs at fixed flags, cold-start captures, ordering simulations, fleet-wide per-column stats. Its samples are exact — determinism (13.1) makes every evaluation replicable — and they repeatedly overturned intuition: compact times cost +3.4% _wire_ while saving −13.1% _uncompressed_ (kept, with the pinned verdict "a decode/memory lever, NOT a wire lever"); the u32 vertex_time tier is −14.25% uncompressed / +10.8% wire on its column; ranking blob orderings by request count recommended an ordering that transferred 669 MiB where the alternative took 184 MiB (see §6); external evidence in the same record shows delta encodings inverting under zstd (MLT's 3–6× collapsing to 1.12–1.96× post-gzip; Parquet DELTA_BINARY_PACKED ~3× _larger_). Measured build wins across 21 datasets ranged 1.07×–21× with no formula predicting which lever pays — the finding that produced §12's measure-don't-model doctrine.

**Problem.** Given the corpus $M = \{(\theta_i, D_i, y_i)\}$ with $y_i = J(\theta_i; D_i) \in \mathbb{R}^d$ observed _without noise_ (determinism), where $\theta$ ranges over the product of §1–§12's knob spaces and $J$ stacks the objectives (archive bytes, wire bytes, uncompressed bytes, cold-start requests and bytes, at-rest inflation, decode cost); a dataset population $P$ with instance features $\phi(D)$ (feature count $N$, tail-share $\mathrm{share}_{1\%}$, tile-size distribution, occupied space/time cardinalities); a two-fidelity oracle — the ~30 s deterministic sample-encode $\hat{J}_s$ through the production encoder versus the expensive full build $J$; and the policy constraints of 13.3 ($\pi$ may only auto-set $\Theta_0$ coordinates).

Decide (a) the shipped default policy $\pi: \phi(D) \to \theta$ — today a piecewise-constant function, i.e. the constants frozen in code — and (b) the next experiments to run (active design). Minimize
$$\mathbb{E}_{D \sim P}\big[w \cdot J(\pi(\phi(D)); D)\big] \;+\; \lambda \cdot (\text{experiment cost})$$
subject to: every estimate of $J$ comes from the production encoder at target settings, never a surrogate analytic model (the anti-model constraint, see §12); $\pi(\phi(D)) \in \Theta_0$ on automated coordinates with user-set coordinates frozen; and recorded anti-lessons as constraints on the objective's _form_ itself — the read-cost objective MUST be the blended $\mathrm{bytes} + g \cdot \mathrm{reads}$, never request count alone (see §6).

**Incumbent.** Humans reading the docs, distilled into frozen constants: the descriptor geometry frozen by one A/B simulation, `pageEntries = 4096` as the "sim-validated sweet spot" (see §7), ordering thresholds fixed after a 36-archive audit (measured picks: spatial 16 / time-major 12 / hilbert3 8 / morton3 0; 12 of 36 moved after two heuristic fixes — see §6), alignment 8 (refunding 445–1300 B/blob), and multi-objective judgment calls recorded as pinned decisions (compact times). The corpus's cold-start captures locate residual cost precisely — surviving directory leaves are 54.1–68.6% of all cold-start bytes on sparse datasets (earthquakes-v2: 5 requests / 335 KiB, leaves 68.6%; flights: 5 requests / 5.69 MiB, leaves 1.1%) and 4–5 requests reach first frame whether the archive is 46 MB or 807 MB — but the located levers remain untried constants.

**Class.** Algorithm configuration over an instance distribution (OtterTune-class knob tuning), in the unusual noise-free regime; multi-fidelity black-box optimization (cheap sample-encode vs full build); optimal experiment design / active learning; response-surface modeling over instance features. The sign flips (compact times, delta-under-zstd) certify that the surfaces are non-monotone in intuitive directions, which is exactly why analytic surrogates were REJECTED (see §12).

**Knobs.** The corpus itself has none — its levers are which datasets sit in the measurement fleet, sample sizes, and capture recency.

**Gap.** The corpus is consumed by humans and compiled into scalars where the record itself names $\phi(D)$-conditioned functions: per-dataset `pageEntries` chosen by the same simulator that froze the descriptor (see §7 — the recorded, untried halving lever); an access-pattern-weighted ordering query mix (see §6 — the open gate on making `measured` the auto default); joint rather than per-lever search over $\Theta$ against the sampled $\hat{J}$ (see §12). Stale samples are a known liability — the cold-start capture has not been re-run post-republish (recorded as K10). The summary-tier skew oracle (a heavy-tail statistic replacing a misleading average — see §12) is the prototype of what the whole corpus should become: measured distribution statistics feeding per-dataset decisions.

### 13.5 Synthesis: recurring shapes and the shared constraint spine

Across §1–§12 the same few problem shapes recur. **Knapsack / budgeted selection:** zoom-placement and replication (§1), the two-cap feature-drop knapsack (§4, default-off per 13.3), viewport cell budgets (§8), prefetch horizons (§9). **Partition:** temporal bucketing as 1-D equal-mass partitioning (§2), pack cutting (§6), contiguity-constrained directory paging solvable by DP (§7). **Linear arrangement:** blob ordering under range-coalescing cost, NP-hard MinLA restricted to a curve menu (§6), and intra-tile row permutation (§3). **Online control with cancellation:** EDF request scheduling with supersession (§9), Graham-style list scheduling on the decode pool (§10), stall-vs-progress gating and fairness (§11), hysteresis against switching costs (§8), Belady-referenced eviction (§9). **Black-box configuration:** the sample-encode tuner (§12) sitting over all of it, fed by the corpus of 13.4.

Every incumbent is a greedy, thresholded, or hand-frozen feasible point of a well-posed optimization problem — and the constraint spine is why the incumbents can afford to be simple. Four constraints thread through everything: **determinism** (13.1), which makes dedup sound, deploys incremental, and measurements noise-free; **no-thinning** (13.3), which removes lossy levers from every automated feasible set; **random access** — standalone decode and directory-only seek, the pair that killed delta chains (§6) and shapes payload, directory, and reader alike; and **bounded client memory**, which is why uncompressed bytes are weighted over wire bytes in §3 and why §8–§11 are eviction and admission problems at all. The rejected designs are the spine's documentation: delta chains (breaks random access and dedup at once), request-count ranking (inconsistent with the reader's own byte/request trade), analytic size models (off by dataset-shaped factors of 1.07–21×), sample-dependent type decisions (breaks conformance invariance), and silent thinning (breaks the product's core promise). Most of the engineering recorded here was not spent solving the optimization problems — it was spent shaping their feasible regions so that greedy solutions are safe, and the sections above are the map of where principled solvers can now win inside those regions.
